# 雅思口语 Part 2＋Part 3｜截图话题逐题练习

按12张截图整理；编写日期：2026-09-10。包含97个Part 2话题，每篇英文正文严格180词；每个话题配6个Part 3问答，共582个回答。

## 阅读说明与来源边界

截图只展示话题名，没有展开题卡和Part 3问题。本文件保留全部话题，不把相近话题删掉；题卡标题为题意整理，提示点为备考提示，不冒充原应用的逐字题卡。Part 3是在检索话题范围、检查旧稿后编写的完整练习问法，不保证与原应用逐题相同。截图中的“共7个问题”通常可能包括1张Part 2题卡和后续问题，但本文不把这种推断当作已核实事实；无论截图显示多少，每话题统一提供6个Part 3问答。

来源顺序：能贴题的旧答案优先保留；不足180词的补写；存在残句或角度不符的修订；没有合适故事的给出新编示范。新增日期、对话、人物能力或结果不代表已向本人核实。旧稿Part 3有些回答只重复大主题，本版保留相关词块并改写论证，避免答非所问。

人物一致性：Luwen主要沿用大学运动朋友；小学绘画朋友改名Ming以区分相遇时间；Mrs Chen沿用退休教师邻居并补充爱好；Anna为新编示范人物。Lily改为独立本地糕点店人物，避免把真实连锁品牌说成她家的公司。VR“已购买”和“希望拥有”分别用现有设备与未来升级的角度；同一经历在不同题目中的变化属于备考版本，请按实际经历选用。

Part 2词数口径：英文词计数，缩写和连字符词按一个词计；标题、来源、提示及Part 3不计入180词。180词是本次整理要求，不等于自动保证口述时长。

Part 3每题用两段展示：第一段“观点1—解释1—例子1”，第二段“观点2—解释2—例子2”。标签用于看结构，练习时把每段的三句连续说出来即可。相近话题会复用同一组讨论答案，每处均完整列出，方便直接练习。

网络检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)；[Ieltsanswers 当季话题整理](https://www.ieltsanswers.com/ielts-speaking-test-questions-with-model-answers.html)；[Part 2与Part 3话题汇编](https://ieltspracticeonline.com/ielts-speaking-part-2-3-forecast-q3-2026/)；[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)；[动物故事话题核对](https://ieltspeaking.com/topics/part2/describe-a-story-book-with-animals-in-it.html)。网页用于题材核对，不复制网上范文。

## P001 节约时间的方法/改变

截图：第4张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a change that helps you save time.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P06计划、Part1 Typing改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The change I want to describe is organising my work before opening messages each morning. I started doing this a few months ago, when several deadlines arrived at the same time.

Previously, I jumped between coding, messages and small requests. I felt busy all day, but important tasks were still unfinished. My friend Luwen suggested writing down three priorities and dealing with similar tasks together.

Now I spend a few minutes making a short list. I begin with the task that needs the most concentration and check messages during planned breaks. I also keep my notes in one place instead of searching through different files.

The change is simple, but it has made my day feel much less chaotic. I spend less time deciding what to do next, and I can usually finish with a clearer idea of what I have achieved. It also leaves me more energy for English practice in the evening.

Most importantly, the method is easy to repeat on an ordinary working day, so I can keep using it even when my schedule becomes especially demanding.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：计划与条理；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P001-Q1 Why do people plan their daily activities?

**观点1：** Planning helps people identify priorities. **解释1：** Without a clear order, easy tasks can replace important ones. **例子1：** For example, a student can revise a difficult chapter before checking messages.

**观点2：** It also makes cooperation easier. **解释2：** Other people can prepare when they know the schedule. **例子2：** For instance, teammates can arrange transport before a training session.

#### P001-Q2 Should children learn to make plans?

**观点1：** Yes, small plans can develop responsibility. **解释1：** Children learn that an activity requires preparation. **例子1：** For example, they can pack school materials the evening before.

**观点2：** However, plans should leave room for play. **解释2：** An overly detailed schedule can make ordinary days stressful. **例子2：** For instance, a free afternoon lets a child choose a game independently.

#### P001-Q3 Is a flexible plan better than a strict one?

**观点1：** Usually, flexibility makes a plan more realistic. **解释1：** Unexpected delays are part of everyday life. **例子1：** For example, extra travel time prevents one late bus from ruining a schedule.

**观点2：** However, some fixed points are necessary. **解释2：** Other people need commitments they can rely on. **例子2：** For instance, a group still needs an agreed meeting time.

#### P001-Q4 How can someone become more organised?

**观点1：** They can give important items a fixed place. **解释1：** This reduces the effort needed to find things. **例子1：** For example, keeping keys by the door avoids a rushed search.

**观点2：** They can also review priorities regularly. **解释2：** A list becomes less useful when circumstances change. **例子2：** For instance, a worker can update tomorrow's tasks before leaving the office.

#### P001-Q5 Does planning always lead to success?

**观点1：** Planning improves the chance of success. **解释1：** It turns a large goal into manageable actions. **例子1：** For example, a revision timetable can spread practice across several weeks.

**观点2：** However, results also depend on execution. **解释2：** A detailed plan achieves little if nobody follows it. **例子2：** For instance, a team must actually practise the skills listed in its programme.

#### P001-Q6 Why do some people dislike making plans?

**观点1：** Some people value freedom and spontaneity. **解释1：** A fixed schedule can feel restrictive during leisure time. **例子1：** For example, a traveller may want to explore a street unexpectedly.

**观点2：** Others have experienced unrealistic plans. **解释2：** Repeated failure can make planning itself seem pointless. **例子2：** For instance, an exhausting study timetable may discourage someone from trying again.


## P002 公共场所丢物品

截图：第4张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe something you lost in a public place.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 玩具车、公共地点素材改编；丢失情节补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a time when I left my wallet in a sports shop near my home. It happened one weekend when I was looking for some frisbee equipment.

The owner gave me several useful suggestions, and I became completely absorbed in our conversation. After paying, I put my wallet on the counter while checking the equipment. Then I picked up my bag and walked out without it.

I only noticed the problem when I reached a nearby cafe. At first, I felt quite anxious because my wallet contained some important cards. I checked my bag carefully and then retraced my steps.

Fortunately, the shop owner had found it and kept it behind the counter. He asked me to describe it before returning it. I felt relieved and thanked him sincerely. Since that experience, I have developed a simple habit of checking my wallet, phone and keys before leaving a public place.

Although I recovered everything, the anxiety was unpleasant enough to change my behaviour, and I now give myself a moment to check before walking away anywhere.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：失物与记忆；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P002-Q1 Why do people lose their belongings?

**观点1：** Distraction is one common reason. **解释1：** People forget objects when their attention moves elsewhere. **例子1：** For example, a customer may leave a wallet while discussing a purchase.

**观点2：** Poor organisation is another cause. **解释2：** Objects become hard to track without a normal storage place. **例子2：** For instance, keys left in different rooms are easy to misplace.

#### P002-Q2 What should people do after finding a lost item?

**观点1：** They should make recovery practical. **解释1：** Handing the item to responsible staff gives the owner a clear contact point. **例子1：** For example, a station's information desk can keep a found bag.

**观点2：** They should also protect the owner's privacy. **解释2：** Personal details should not be displayed unnecessarily. **例子2：** For instance, a public post should avoid showing an entire identity document.

#### P002-Q3 How can people stop themselves losing things?

**观点1：** A short checking routine can help. **解释1：** Repeating the same action reduces dependence on memory. **例子1：** For example, someone can check keys, wallet and phone before leaving.

**观点2：** Keeping possessions organised also matters. **解释2：** Fewer loose items are easier to manage. **例子2：** For instance, a small pouch can hold cards and tickets during a journey.

#### P002-Q4 Are children more likely to lose things than adults?

**观点1：** Children may lose things because they become absorbed in play. **解释1：** Their attention can change very quickly. **例子1：** For example, a child may leave a jacket after joining a game.

**观点2：** However, busy adults also misplace possessions. **解释2：** Stress and multitasking can interrupt ordinary routines. **例子2：** For instance, a commuter may leave a bag while answering an urgent call.

#### P002-Q5 Can technology help people recover belongings?

**观点1：** Technology can make a search more focused. **解释1：** A location tool may narrow down where an object was left. **例子1：** For example, a tracker can help locate misplaced keys.

**观点2：** Online communication can also reach helpful people. **解释2：** Local groups connect owners with people in the same area. **例子2：** For instance, a cafe visitor might recognise a description of a lost umbrella.

#### P002-Q6 Why do lost objects sometimes matter beyond their price?

**观点1：** Some objects preserve personal memories. **解释1：** Replacing the item does not replace its history. **例子1：** For example, an old toy may remind someone of a parent's care.

**观点2：** Other objects are important for daily tasks. **解释2：** Their loss creates inconvenience even if they are inexpensive. **例子2：** For instance, losing house keys can interrupt an entire evening.


## P003 一见且想继续了解的人

截图：第4张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe someone you met once and would like to know better.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P08越南咖啡店主改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to talk about a cafe owner I met during a holiday in Vietnam. I only spoke to him that afternoon, but I would enjoy getting to know him better.

My friend and I visited his small cafe before leaving the city. We ordered drinks and spent some time talking about our trip. When we tried to pay, we discovered that the cafe accepted cash, while we only had our cards left.

Instead of becoming annoyed, the owner calmly asked us to wait. He spoke to a neighbouring shop and arranged a practical way for us to complete the payment. His patience saved us from a very awkward situation.

I would like to learn how he developed that approach to dealing with people. Running a small business must involve unexpected problems, yet he stayed friendly and resourceful. If I returned, I would ask about his experience and the story behind the cafe.

I think a longer conversation would reveal much more about his character than a quick photograph or a brief online review of the place ever could.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：朋友与人际；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P003-Q1 What makes someone a good friend?

**观点1：** Reliability is important in friendship. **解释1：** People need to know that support is available when it matters. **例子1：** For example, a friend can listen patiently during a difficult week.

**观点2：** Shared enjoyment also keeps the relationship alive. **解释2：** Friendship should include ordinary pleasant experiences. **例子2：** For instance, a regular game gives two people time to laugh together.

#### P003-Q2 Why are some people more popular than others?

**观点1：** Considerate people make others feel comfortable. **解释1：** Listening and including newcomers help people feel welcome. **例子1：** For example, a captain who supports beginners can become well liked.

**观点2：** Visible ability can also attract attention. **解释2：** People often admire skills they hope to develop. **例子2：** For instance, an excellent musician may draw others into a school club.

#### P003-Q3 Is it easy to maintain friendships after leaving school?

**观点1：** It becomes harder because routines separate. **解释1：** Friends may work in different places and have limited free time. **例子1：** For example, a spontaneous meeting may require planning several weeks ahead.

**观点2：** However, regular small contact can preserve a connection. **解释2：** Friendship does not require meeting every day. **例子2：** For instance, a thoughtful message can show continuing interest.

#### P003-Q4 Can people with different interests become close friends?

**观点1：** Yes, shared values can matter more than identical hobbies. **解释1：** People can respect and support each other despite different preferences. **例子1：** For example, a quiet reader and an athlete may both value honesty.

**观点2：** Different interests can also enrich a friendship. **解释2：** Each person introduces the other to unfamiliar experiences. **例子2：** For instance, one friend might suggest a first museum visit.

#### P003-Q5 Why do people want to meet someone again?

**观点1：** An interesting conversation can create curiosity. **解释1：** People want to understand a perspective they have only briefly encountered. **例子1：** For example, a resourceful shop owner may have useful stories about business.

**观点2：** A welcoming manner also encourages further contact. **解释2：** Feeling respected makes a second conversation appealing. **例子2：** For instance, a patient listener can leave a stronger impression than an impressive job title.

#### P003-Q6 Should friends always agree with one another?

**观点1：** No, honest disagreement can be helpful. **解释1：** A friend may notice a problem that someone has overlooked. **例子1：** For example, they can question an unrealistic study plan.

**观点2：** However, disagreement should remain respectful. **解释2：** Criticising the idea is different from insulting the person. **例子2：** For instance, offering a practical alternative keeps the conversation constructive.


## P004 印象深刻的课程

截图：第4张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a lesson that made a strong impression on you.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P46冥想课程改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The lesson I remember most clearly was an introductory meditation class I attended with a friend last year. At the time, work and study were making my routine feel quite stressful.

I expected the teacher to give us complicated instructions. Instead, she asked us to sit comfortably, notice our breathing and gently return our attention whenever our minds wandered. She explained that getting distracted did not mean we had failed.

That point stayed with me because I usually judge myself quite harshly when learning something new. During the first exercise, I kept thinking about unfinished work. However, I gradually became less frustrated and simply tried again.

After the class, my friend and I discussed how to include a short practice in our mornings. The lesson did not magically remove our responsibilities, but it gave us a manageable way to pause. I remember it because the teacher made an unfamiliar activity feel practical and approachable.

I would happily recommend a similar introductory lesson to a busy friend, especially someone who wants a simple routine rather than another complicated goal to achieve.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：学习与教学；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P004-Q1 What makes a lesson memorable?

**观点1：** Active participation makes a lesson memorable. **解释1：** Doing something gives learners a concrete experience to recall. **例子1：** For example, practising a frisbee catch is clearer than hearing instructions alone.

**观点2：** A supportive teacher also helps. **解释2：** People learn more comfortably when mistakes are treated patiently. **例子2：** For instance, a language teacher can encourage a hesitant student to finish speaking.

#### P004-Q2 Is learning alone better than learning with others?

**观点1：** Learning alone offers control over pace. **解释1：** People can repeat difficult material without holding anyone back. **例子1：** For example, a learner can replay a short coding demonstration.

**观点2：** Group learning provides feedback and motivation. **解释2：** Other people notice problems and suggest different approaches. **例子2：** For instance, a teammate can point out why a catching technique is not working.

#### P004-Q3 What qualities make a good teacher?

**观点1：** A good teacher explains ideas clearly. **解释1：** They connect unfamiliar material with something learners understand. **例子1：** For example, a simple daily example can clarify an abstract concept.

**观点2：** Patience is equally valuable. **解释2：** Different students need different amounts of practice. **例子2：** For instance, a teacher may demonstrate the same movement again without embarrassing a beginner.

#### P004-Q4 Can adults learn new skills as well as children?

**观点1：** Adults can learn effectively through clear goals. **解释1：** They often understand why a skill matters to them. **例子1：** For example, someone learning English for work may practise consistently.

**观点2：** Children may be more willing to experiment. **解释2：** They can explore without worrying as much about appearing inexperienced. **例子2：** For instance, children often try unfamiliar games immediately.

#### P004-Q5 Can online videos replace teachers?

**观点1：** Videos are useful for accessible explanations. **解释1：** Learners can pause and repeat a demonstration whenever needed. **例子1：** For example, a beginner can watch a basic cooking technique several times.

**观点2：** However, videos cannot always provide personal feedback. **解释2：** The learner may repeat an error without noticing it. **例子2：** For instance, a teacher can correct an unclear pronunciation immediately.

#### P004-Q6 Why is practice important when learning?

**观点1：** Practice helps knowledge become usable. **解释1：** Understanding an explanation does not guarantee smooth performance. **例子1：** For example, knowing keyboard positions differs from typing a report accurately.

**观点2：** Practice also reveals specific weaknesses. **解释2：** Learners can then focus their effort more effectively. **例子2：** For instance, recording a speech may show that examples need clearer explanations.


## P005 送朋友的礼物

截图：第4张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a present you would like to give a friend.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 准备送给谁；礼物是什么；为什么适合对方；计划何时赠送及预期感受。

**答案来源：** Luwen、飞盘、礼物素材改编；计划补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to give my friend Luwen a good quality frisbee with a personal message on it. He has supported me a lot, so I want the gift to reflect our shared interest.

We became friends through sport, and he helped me when I was still learning the basics. He was patient when I missed easy catches and encouraged me to keep practising. A frisbee would remind us of those early training sessions.

I would choose one suitable for regular practice rather than simply buying the most expensive model. I would also ask other teammates to write a few short messages on a card. That personal element would make the present more meaningful.

I hope to give it to him at a small gathering after training. I imagine he would be pleased because it is something he could actually use. For me, it would be a simple way to thank him for his time and encouragement.

I would also explain why I chose it, because the story behind a present can make an ordinary object feel much more personal.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：礼物与心意；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P005-Q1 Why do people give presents?

**观点1：** Presents express attention and affection. **解释1：** They show that someone has thought about the recipient. **例子1：** For example, a sports-related gift can recognise a friend's hobby.

**观点2：** They also mark shared occasions. **解释2：** An object can become connected with a particular memory. **例子2：** For instance, a birthday card may preserve messages from a close group of friends.

#### P005-Q2 How should people choose a suitable present?

**观点1：** They should begin with the recipient's interests and needs. **解释1：** A gift is useful when it fits the person's life. **例子1：** For example, suitable equipment may support regular training.

**观点2：** They should also consider the relationship and occasion. **解释2：** A very expensive gift can sometimes feel uncomfortable. **例子2：** For instance, a small thoughtful item may suit a casual friendship better.

#### P005-Q3 Are gifts more meaningful when they are handmade?

**观点1：** They can be because the effort is visible. **解释1：** Personal details make the object distinctive. **例子1：** For example, a handmade card can refer to a shared joke.

**观点2：** However, thoughtfulness is not limited to handmade objects. **解释2：** A carefully chosen purchase can show equal understanding. **例子2：** For instance, a book about a friend's interest may feel very personal.

#### P005-Q4 Should people give practical or decorative gifts?

**观点1：** Practical gifts suit people who value daily usefulness. **解释1：** Repeated use can keep the giver in mind. **例子1：** For example, a comfortable item for a hobby can become part of a routine.

**观点2：** Decorative gifts can still carry emotional value. **解释2：** Not every meaningful object needs a function. **例子2：** For instance, a framed family photograph can make a room feel warmer.

#### P005-Q5 Do children and adults prefer different gifts?

**观点1：** Children may respond strongly to immediate excitement. **解释1：** A colourful toy provides an obvious activity. **例子1：** For example, a remote-control car can become a favourite plaything.

**观点2：** Adults may consider usefulness and personal meaning more carefully. **解释2：** Their needs often reflect work and responsibilities. **例子2：** For instance, something that supports a relaxing hobby may be particularly appreciated.

#### P005-Q6 Is receiving a gift more enjoyable than giving one?

**观点1：** Receiving can make people feel valued. **解释1：** A thoughtful gift shows that someone understands them. **例子1：** For example, a personal birthday decoration can create a warm surprise.

**观点2：** Giving can also be rewarding. **解释2：** The giver sees their care make another person happy. **例子2：** For instance, choosing the right present for a supportive friend can feel satisfying.


## P006 帮别人提升健康

截图：第4张，显示共3个问题。话题检索参考：[Ieltsanswers 当季话题整理](https://www.ieltsanswers.com/ielts-speaking-test-questions-with-model-answers.html)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe someone you helped to develop a healthier routine.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P30/P36冥想建议改编；非医疗效果陈述。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to talk about my friend Luwen, whom I helped to build a more balanced daily routine. A few months ago, he was preparing for the CFA exam while continuing sports training.

He had a lot on his plate and often looked exhausted. During our conversations, he said that he kept checking his phone late at night and found it difficult to switch off. I suggested trying a short period of quiet meditation and putting his phone aside before bed.

At first, he thought the activity would be boring. I did not pressure him, but offered to practise with him for a few minutes. Keeping the first attempt short made it easier for him to continue.

Later, he told me that this small routine helped him feel calmer in the evening. I was pleased that he had found something manageable. The difficult part was starting; regular encouragement made the change feel less like another task on his list.

I learned that offering practical support can be more useful than simply telling someone that they should change their habits.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：生活习惯与医疗职业；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P006-Q1 How can people build healthier daily routines?

**观点1：** They can begin with manageable changes. **解释1：** Small actions are easier to repeat during a busy week. **例子1：** For example, a short walk after lunch is a realistic starting point.

**观点2：** Supportive surroundings also help. **解释2：** A convenient option requires less willpower. **例子2：** For instance, keeping a regular break in the schedule makes it easier to leave the desk.

#### P006-Q2 Why do some people struggle to exercise regularly?

**观点1：** Time pressure can make exercise difficult. **解释1：** Work and family responsibilities may use most of the day. **例子1：** For example, a long commute leaves little time for a sports session.

**观点2：** Some people also choose activities they dislike. **解释2：** An unpleasant routine is harder to maintain. **例子2：** For instance, someone who dislikes running may prefer a relaxed team game.

#### P006-Q3 Can friends encourage better habits?

**观点1：** Yes, friends can make a new activity more enjoyable. **解释1：** Shared company reduces the feeling of doing another task. **例子1：** For example, two friends can take a regular evening walk.

**观点2：** They can also provide gentle accountability. **解释2：** Knowing someone is expecting you can support consistency. **例子2：** For instance, a training partner may help someone keep an agreed practice time.

#### P006-Q4 What attracts people to healthcare careers?

**观点1：** Some people want work that helps others directly. **解释1：** Seeing a practical benefit can make effort feel meaningful. **例子1：** For example, a nurse may value reassuring an anxious family.

**观点2：** Others are interested in science and problem solving. **解释2：** They enjoy applying knowledge to complex situations. **例子2：** For instance, a student may like both biology and careful practical work.

#### P006-Q5 What qualities are useful in healthcare work?

**观点1：** Careful attention is essential. **解释1：** Small details can matter when people depend on accurate information. **例子1：** For example, a worker needs to communicate instructions clearly.

**观点2：** Empathy is also valuable. **解释2：** People may feel worried or confused in unfamiliar situations. **例子2：** For instance, a patient explanation can make a family feel more comfortable asking questions.

#### P006-Q6 Should workplaces encourage staff to take breaks?

**观点1：** Yes, reasonable breaks can support concentration. **解释1：** Continuous demanding work can leave people mentally tired. **例子1：** For example, a brief pause may help someone return to a complicated report.

**观点2：** Breaks also show respect for employees' needs. **解释2：** A sustainable routine is easier to maintain. **例子2：** For instance, predictable lunch time lets staff organise meals and short walks.


## P007 去过的靠近自然的地方

截图：第4张，显示共4个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a natural place (e.g. parks, mountains, etc)

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P56原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to talk about a quiet place I really like, and it is Zhangjiajie National Forest Park in Hunan Province, China.

I was first introduced to this place a few years ago through photos and documentaries. What impressed me most were its unique pillar-like mountains and the wild monkeys there. I do not go there very often because it is quite far from where I live, but it is still one of the quietest and most unforgettable places I have ever been to.

When I was there, I was completely stunned by the scenery. There were all kinds of trees reaching high into the sky, and I could hear birds chirping in the morning. The fresh air and the sea of green trees made me feel calm and peaceful.

In such tranquil surroundings, I felt incredibly relaxed. The whole experience was therapeutic and refreshing, and it also raised my awareness of environmental protection.

I would happily return with someone who enjoys walking slowly, because this is a place where noticing small details is more rewarding than rushing between attractions.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：自然与植物；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P007-Q1 Why do people enjoy visiting natural places?

**观点1：** Natural surroundings provide a change from demanding routines. **解释1：** People can slow down and focus on something beyond work. **例子1：** For example, a walk among trees can feel refreshing after computer tasks.

**观点2：** They also offer shared recreation. **解释2：** Friends can spend time together without a complicated programme. **例子2：** For instance, a park visit can combine conversation and gentle exercise.

#### P007-Q2 Should cities provide more green spaces?

**观点1：** Yes, nearby greenery makes recreation more accessible. **解释1：** Residents do not always have time to travel far. **例子1：** For example, a small neighbourhood park can support an evening walk.

**观点2：** Green spaces also improve shared surroundings. **解释2：** They give people pleasant places to meet and rest. **例子2：** For instance, shaded seating can make a busy district more welcoming.

#### P007-Q3 How can people be encouraged to protect nature?

**观点1：** Practical activities make the issue tangible. **解释1：** People see the effect of their own behaviour. **例子1：** For example, collecting litter can reveal how much waste visitors leave.

**观点2：** Clear facilities also support better choices. **解释2：** Good intentions are easier to follow when alternatives are convenient. **例子2：** For instance, visible bins can help visitors dispose of rubbish properly.

#### P007-Q4 Why do some people enjoy growing plants?

**观点1：** Growing plants offers visible progress. **解释1：** Small changes provide a sense of achievement. **例子1：** For example, a new leaf can reward weeks of regular care.

**观点2：** The activity can also create a calming routine. **解释2：** People focus on simple tasks away from screens. **例子2：** For instance, checking a few pots can become a pleasant morning habit.

#### P007-Q5 Should children spend more time outdoors?

**观点1：** Yes, outdoor activity offers different experiences from classroom learning. **解释1：** Children can move, explore and notice living things. **例子1：** For example, a park visit may lead to questions about insects.

**观点2：** However, the activity should suit the setting. **解释2：** Adults need to balance freedom with practical supervision. **例子2：** For instance, a nature walk can use clear boundaries while allowing exploration.

#### P007-Q6 Can tourism harm natural places?

**观点1：** Yes, heavy use can damage the environment. **解释1：** Visitors may leave rubbish or disturb sensitive areas. **例子1：** For example, walking away from paths can affect vegetation.

**观点2：** However, well-managed tourism can support protection. **解释2：** Visitor interest can create reasons to maintain a place carefully. **例子2：** For instance, clear routes and educational signs can guide more considerate behaviour.


## P008 印象深刻的聚会

截图：第4张，显示共5个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a gathering you remember clearly.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P31春节合照改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The gathering I would like to describe was a family reunion during Spring Festival a few years ago. My grandparents, my parents and I finally had a chance to spend an evening together.

We had dinner at home and talked about what had happened during the year. Nobody was in a hurry to leave, which made the atmosphere especially relaxed. After dinner, we decided to take a family photograph.

Just as everyone was ready, our dog rushed into the room and started barking. We all burst out laughing, and the camera captured that moment. The photograph was not perfectly arranged, but it showed how happy we felt.

I remember the gathering because it was full of small, ordinary moments that now seem precious. Work and distance make family meetings less frequent than I would like. Whenever I look at that photograph, I remember the conversation, the laughter and the feeling of being completely at home.

I hope we can arrange more evenings like that, because familiar company and unhurried conversation are things I appreciate more as my routine becomes busier.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：家庭与回忆；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P008-Q1 Why do family gatherings matter?

**观点1：** They give relatives time to reconnect. **解释1：** Everyday responsibilities can prevent proper conversations. **例子1：** For example, a shared dinner lets people discuss more than practical messages.

**观点2：** They also create shared memories. **解释2：** Repeated occasions become part of a family's history. **例子2：** For instance, a funny moment in a group photograph may be remembered for years.

#### P008-Q2 How can families spend more meaningful time together?

**观点1：** They can choose activities that allow conversation. **解释1：** An expensive outing is unnecessary if nobody has time to talk. **例子1：** For example, cooking a simple meal together can feel personal.

**观点2：** They should also protect some time from interruptions. **解释2：** Constant messages divide attention. **例子2：** For instance, putting phones aside during dinner can help everyone participate.

#### P008-Q3 Why do people keep old family objects?

**观点1：** Old objects can preserve emotional connections. **解释1：** They remind people of someone who gave or repaired them. **例子1：** For example, a childhood toy can represent a father's care.

**观点2：** They can also pass stories between generations. **解释2：** A physical object makes family history easier to discuss. **例子2：** For instance, an old photograph may lead to stories about grandparents.

#### P008-Q4 What makes people proud of relatives?

**观点1：** People feel proud when relatives overcome difficulties. **解释1：** They understand the effort behind the visible result. **例子1：** For example, completing a demanding course can mean more to family than strangers.

**观点2：** Kind behaviour also creates pride. **解释2：** A relative's character reflects what the family values. **例子2：** For instance, patiently helping a distressed child can leave a lasting impression.

#### P008-Q5 Are family celebrations different now from the past?

**观点1：** Some celebrations now involve more technology. **解释1：** People can include relatives who cannot travel. **例子1：** For example, a video call can connect a distant family member with a birthday.

**观点2：** However, busy routines can reduce time together. **解释2：** Work and distance make arrangements harder. **例子2：** For instance, a reunion may need to fit several different schedules.

#### P008-Q6 Do expensive presents show greater affection?

**观点1：** Not necessarily, because thoughtfulness matters more than price. **解释1：** A suitable gift shows understanding of the recipient. **例子1：** For example, a handmade card can refer to a shared memory.

**观点2：** However, practical value can also be meaningful. **解释2：** A gift may solve a real difficulty. **例子2：** For instance, useful equipment can support a relative's long-standing hobby.


## P009 喜欢的建筑

截图：第4张，显示共3个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an interesting building

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P51原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, the unusual building that I'd like to talk about is a courtyard house. In Chinese, we call it Siheyuan.

The history of this ancient building can date back hundreds of years. It is a traditional and unusual type of residence mainly located in Beijing.

A small or medium-sized courtyard usually has its main gate built on one side of the yard. Behind the gate, there is a screen wall, which helps block the wind and protect privacy. The buildings are arranged around a square yard, and each room faces the center, which makes the space feel organized and peaceful.

I find it interesting because it offers space, comfort and privacy. Since there is a small yard, people can plant flowers and plants there, making the whole space lively and gorgeous. It also represents China's profound history and diverse culture.

I would really enjoy spending an afternoon there with a book, listening to the sounds from the courtyard. The design makes me think about how a home can support both personal privacy and time together with family members.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：建筑与居住；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P009-Q1 Why do people enjoy visiting traditional buildings?

**观点1：** Traditional buildings make history visible. **解释1：** Visitors can see how earlier generations organised daily life. **例子1：** For example, a courtyard suggests how a family shared outdoor space.

**观点2：** They also provide visual variety. **解释2：** Distinctive designs make a place memorable. **例子2：** For instance, older houses can give a neighbourhood character beside modern offices.

#### P009-Q2 Should cities preserve old buildings?

**观点1：** Yes, valuable buildings can protect local identity. **解释1：** Once demolished, an original setting cannot easily be recreated. **例子1：** For example, a historic street can show a city's development.

**观点2：** However, preservation needs practical maintenance. **解释2：** A neglected building cannot serve visitors or residents well. **例子2：** For instance, repairs may improve safety while retaining important design features.

#### P009-Q3 What makes a home comfortable?

**观点1：** A practical layout supports everyday activities. **解释1：** People need suitable space for rest, work and storage. **例子1：** For example, a quiet corner can separate study from family noise.

**观点2：** Personal connections also create comfort. **解释2：** Familiar objects make a space feel like home. **例子2：** For instance, family photographs can make a simple room welcoming.

#### P009-Q4 Are tall buildings always suitable for city life?

**观点1：** Tall buildings can use limited land efficiently. **解释1：** They place many homes or workplaces in a compact area. **例子1：** For example, an office tower can accommodate several companies.

**观点2：** However, daily access needs careful planning. **解释2：** Crowded lifts and surrounding traffic can create inconvenience. **例子2：** For instance, many people leaving at once may face long waits.

#### P009-Q5 Should a building's appearance matter more than its function?

**观点1：** Function should usually come first. **解释1：** People must use the space comfortably every day. **例子1：** For example, an attractive office is frustrating without suitable light or quiet.

**观点2：** However, appearance still affects experience. **解释2：** Thoughtful design can make a place pleasant and distinctive. **例子2：** For instance, a welcoming entrance can improve how visitors feel.

#### P009-Q6 Why might people enjoy visiting a home but dislike living there?

**观点1：** A visit highlights atmosphere and novelty. **解释1：** People can appreciate an unusual setting for a short time. **例子1：** For example, a courtyard may feel peaceful during an afternoon.

**观点2：** Permanent residence involves practical demands. **解释2：** Maintenance, transport and comfort matter throughout the year. **例子2：** For instance, a beautiful house may be inconvenient for a daily commute.


## P010 最不喜欢的电影

截图：第4张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a film you particularly disliked.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体对象；怎样接触或获得；外观、内容或用途；它为什么对你重要。

**答案来源：** 新编示范：虚构未命名动作片，避免与喜欢的电影冲突。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe an action film I watched at home with a friend a few months ago. I cannot remember its title clearly, but I remember feeling very disappointed by it.

We chose it because the trailer suggested an exciting story with clever characters. Unfortunately, most of the interesting moments had already appeared in that short preview. The full film relied on long action scenes, while the characters had little personality.

What annoyed me most was that their decisions did not make much sense. Important problems were solved by sudden coincidences, so I stopped caring about what would happen next. My friend and I kept checking how much time was left.

I do enjoy action films, but I still want a convincing story. This one felt repetitive and empty. Afterwards, we talked about films with sharper dialogue, such as Let the Bullets Fly. The contrast helped me understand that expensive visuals alone are not enough to keep me interested.

Next time, I would read a few different opinions before choosing, rather than trusting an exciting trailer by itself.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：影视与视频；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P010-Q1 What makes a film or programme enjoyable?

**观点1：** A convincing story helps people care about the outcome. **解释1：** Characters need motives that viewers can understand. **例子1：** For example, a difficult choice can create more interest than a long action scene.

**观点2：** Presentation also matters. **解释2：** Acting, sound and visual details support the atmosphere. **例子2：** For instance, an unusual office design can strengthen a mysterious television story.

#### P010-Q2 Why do different people enjoy different kinds of entertainment?

**观点1：** People use entertainment to meet different needs. **解释1：** Some want excitement while others want a quiet break. **例子1：** For example, a tired worker may choose a gentle comedy.

**观点2：** Personal experience also shapes taste. **解释2：** Familiar themes can feel more meaningful. **例子2：** For instance, someone who plays sport may enjoy a documentary about teamwork.

#### P010-Q3 Are online videos useful for learning?

**观点1：** They can make a process easy to observe. **解释1：** Visual demonstrations show steps that text may not explain clearly. **例子1：** For example, a learner can watch how to hold a frisbee.

**观点2：** However, quality varies considerably. **解释2：** An attractive presentation does not guarantee a reliable explanation. **例子2：** For instance, viewers should question a dramatic claim without supporting evidence.

#### P010-Q4 Is watching at home better than going to a cinema?

**观点1：** Watching at home offers convenience. **解释1：** People can choose a time that fits their routine. **例子1：** For example, friends can watch together after dinner without travelling.

**观点2：** A cinema provides a more focused experience. **解释2：** The large screen and shared atmosphere can increase involvement. **例子2：** For instance, an impressive visual sequence may feel more powerful there.

#### P010-Q5 Why do people share entertaining videos?

**观点1：** They want to share an enjoyable reaction. **解释1：** A short clip can make someone laugh quickly. **例子1：** For example, friends may send a funny scene during a busy day.

**观点2：** Sharing also expresses personal interests. **解释2：** It gives people a reason to start a conversation. **例子2：** For instance, a science video can lead to discussion among curious colleagues.

#### P010-Q6 Should entertainment always teach people something?

**观点1：** No, relaxation is a valid purpose. **解释1：** People sometimes need a break from demanding tasks. **例子1：** For example, a light comedy can make an ordinary evening pleasant.

**观点2：** However, meaningful ideas can add lasting value. **解释2：** Viewers may continue thinking after the programme ends. **例子2：** For instance, a story about work and identity can inspire a thoughtful conversation.


## P011 教你新技能的人

截图：第4张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[Ieltsanswers 当季话题整理](https://www.ieltsanswers.com/ielts-speaking-test-questions-with-model-answers.html)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a person who taught you a new skill.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P39飞盘基础、P01帮助新人改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The person I want to describe is Luwen, who taught me how to catch and throw a frisbee. I met him through my university team, where he often helped beginners.

During my first training session, I struggled to catch even simple passes. I kept moving my hands too late and felt embarrassed when the disc fell to the ground. Luwen did not make fun of me. He demonstrated the pancake catch slowly and explained how to position my hands.

Then he stood a short distance away and gave me several gentle passes. Once I became more confident, we increased the distance and added some movement. Breaking the skill into small steps made it much easier to understand.

By the end of the session, I could complete a few successful catches. I felt excited and wanted to practise again. I admire Luwen because he combined clear instruction with patience, which helped me enjoy learning instead of worrying about looking foolish.

I would use the same patient approach if I had the chance to introduce another beginner to the sport in future.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：学习与教学；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P011-Q1 What makes a lesson memorable?

**观点1：** Active participation makes a lesson memorable. **解释1：** Doing something gives learners a concrete experience to recall. **例子1：** For example, practising a frisbee catch is clearer than hearing instructions alone.

**观点2：** A supportive teacher also helps. **解释2：** People learn more comfortably when mistakes are treated patiently. **例子2：** For instance, a language teacher can encourage a hesitant student to finish speaking.

#### P011-Q2 Is learning alone better than learning with others?

**观点1：** Learning alone offers control over pace. **解释1：** People can repeat difficult material without holding anyone back. **例子1：** For example, a learner can replay a short coding demonstration.

**观点2：** Group learning provides feedback and motivation. **解释2：** Other people notice problems and suggest different approaches. **例子2：** For instance, a teammate can point out why a catching technique is not working.

#### P011-Q3 What qualities make a good teacher?

**观点1：** A good teacher explains ideas clearly. **解释1：** They connect unfamiliar material with something learners understand. **例子1：** For example, a simple daily example can clarify an abstract concept.

**观点2：** Patience is equally valuable. **解释2：** Different students need different amounts of practice. **例子2：** For instance, a teacher may demonstrate the same movement again without embarrassing a beginner.

#### P011-Q4 Can adults learn new skills as well as children?

**观点1：** Adults can learn effectively through clear goals. **解释1：** They often understand why a skill matters to them. **例子1：** For example, someone learning English for work may practise consistently.

**观点2：** Children may be more willing to experiment. **解释2：** They can explore without worrying as much about appearing inexperienced. **例子2：** For instance, children often try unfamiliar games immediately.

#### P011-Q5 Can online videos replace teachers?

**观点1：** Videos are useful for accessible explanations. **解释1：** Learners can pause and repeat a demonstration whenever needed. **例子1：** For example, a beginner can watch a basic cooking technique several times.

**观点2：** However, videos cannot always provide personal feedback. **解释2：** The learner may repeat an error without noticing it. **例子2：** For instance, a teacher can correct an unclear pronunciation immediately.

#### P011-Q6 Why is practice important when learning?

**观点1：** Practice helps knowledge become usable. **解释1：** Understanding an explanation does not guarantee smooth performance. **例子1：** For example, knowing keyboard positions differs from typing a report accurately.

**观点2：** Practice also reveals specific weaknesses. **解释2：** Learners can then focus their effort more effectively. **例子2：** For instance, recording a speech may show that examples need clearer explanations.


## P012 学习并喜欢历史的人

截图：第5张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[Ieltsanswers 当季话题整理](https://www.ieltsanswers.com/ielts-speaking-test-questions-with-model-answers.html)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a person who enjoys learning history.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿邻居Mrs Chen、博物馆素材改编；历史爱好补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to talk about my neighbour, Mrs Chen, a retired teacher who enjoys learning history. I got to know her through short conversations in our neighbourhood.

She often reads books about local places and watches historical documentaries. What makes her interest special is that she connects what she learns with ordinary streets and buildings. A place I would normally pass without noticing can become interesting when she explains its background.

Once, we talked about traditional courtyard houses. She described how their layout shaped family life and encouraged me to look carefully at the shared outdoor space. Her explanation made me want to visit one myself.

I think she loves history because it helps her understand how people lived and why places developed in particular ways. She is curious rather than simply interested in memorising dates. I enjoy talking with her because she can turn a familiar subject into a story that feels personal and worth exploring.

Her enthusiasm also shows me that learning does not need to stop when someone retires or leaves a formal classroom for good.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：历史与文化；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P012-Q1 Why should people learn about history?

**观点1：** History helps explain the present. **解释1：** Current places and customs developed through earlier choices. **例子1：** For example, an old neighbourhood's layout can reveal how people once lived.

**观点2：** It also broadens perspective. **解释2：** People realise that their own routine is not the only possible one. **例子2：** For instance, a museum can show different ways families worked and travelled.

#### P012-Q2 How can teachers make history interesting?

**观点1：** They can connect events with individual stories. **解释1：** Human experiences are easier to imagine than isolated dates. **例子1：** For example, a diary can make a historical period feel concrete.

**观点2：** They can also use physical places and objects. **解释2：** Seeing evidence encourages curiosity. **例子2：** For instance, a visit to a courtyard house can support a lesson about family life.

#### P012-Q3 Are museums better than books for learning history?

**观点1：** Museums make objects and settings visible. **解释1：** Visitors can connect an explanation with something physical. **例子1：** For example, an old tool shows details that a short description may miss.

**观点2：** Books allow deeper and more focused study. **解释2：** Readers can follow an argument at their own pace. **例子2：** For instance, a biography can explain the background behind one museum exhibit.

#### P012-Q4 Why do some people lose interest in history?

**观点1：** They may experience it as memorising disconnected facts. **解释1：** Without context, dates and names can seem irrelevant. **例子1：** For example, a student may forget a timeline after an exam.

**观点2：** Others cannot see a connection with their lives. **解释2：** The subject feels distant from current concerns. **例子2：** For instance, local history may attract someone who dislikes abstract summaries.

#### P012-Q5 Can older people help younger people understand the past?

**观点1：** Yes, personal memories make change tangible. **解释1：** Older people can describe everyday experiences in detail. **例子1：** For example, a grandparent can explain what commuting used to involve.

**观点2：** However, one person's memory is only one perspective. **解释2：** It should be compared with other information. **例子2：** For instance, photographs or written records may clarify details of a family story.

#### P012-Q6 Should local traditions be preserved?

**观点1：** Many traditions deserve preservation because they express shared identity. **解释1：** They connect people with earlier generations. **例子1：** For example, a familiar festival meal can bring a family together.

**观点2：** However, traditions can adapt to changing needs. **解释2：** Keeping meaning does not require copying every detail. **例子2：** For instance, distant relatives may join a celebration through a video call.


## P013 和朋友度过的愉快夜晚

截图：第5张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an enjoyable evening with friends.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P38海鲜聚餐改为朋友聚会。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to describe an evening when I had a seafood dinner with friends during a trip to Qingdao. We had spent the day exploring the city and wanted somewhere relaxed to eat.

We went to a market near the coast and looked at the fresh seafood on offer. After comparing a few stalls, we chose some prawns, crabs and clams. A nearby restaurant cooked them for a small fee, so the meal felt both fresh and affordable.

While waiting, we talked about funny moments from the trip. Nobody was checking work messages, and we were able to enjoy a proper conversation. The food was tender and full of flavour, but the company mattered just as much.

After dinner, we took a gentle walk near the sea. The evening was memorable because there was no complicated plan or expensive activity. We simply had enough time to eat, talk and appreciate being together away from our normal routines.

I would happily repeat that kind of evening, because it gave us space to enjoy both the place and each other's company properly.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：家庭与回忆；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P013-Q1 Why do family gatherings matter?

**观点1：** They give relatives time to reconnect. **解释1：** Everyday responsibilities can prevent proper conversations. **例子1：** For example, a shared dinner lets people discuss more than practical messages.

**观点2：** They also create shared memories. **解释2：** Repeated occasions become part of a family's history. **例子2：** For instance, a funny moment in a group photograph may be remembered for years.

#### P013-Q2 How can families spend more meaningful time together?

**观点1：** They can choose activities that allow conversation. **解释1：** An expensive outing is unnecessary if nobody has time to talk. **例子1：** For example, cooking a simple meal together can feel personal.

**观点2：** They should also protect some time from interruptions. **解释2：** Constant messages divide attention. **例子2：** For instance, putting phones aside during dinner can help everyone participate.

#### P013-Q3 Why do people keep old family objects?

**观点1：** Old objects can preserve emotional connections. **解释1：** They remind people of someone who gave or repaired them. **例子1：** For example, a childhood toy can represent a father's care.

**观点2：** They can also pass stories between generations. **解释2：** A physical object makes family history easier to discuss. **例子2：** For instance, an old photograph may lead to stories about grandparents.

#### P013-Q4 What makes people proud of relatives?

**观点1：** People feel proud when relatives overcome difficulties. **解释1：** They understand the effort behind the visible result. **例子1：** For example, completing a demanding course can mean more to family than strangers.

**观点2：** Kind behaviour also creates pride. **解释2：** A relative's character reflects what the family values. **例子2：** For instance, patiently helping a distressed child can leave a lasting impression.

#### P013-Q5 Are family celebrations different now from the past?

**观点1：** Some celebrations now involve more technology. **解释1：** People can include relatives who cannot travel. **例子1：** For example, a video call can connect a distant family member with a birthday.

**观点2：** However, busy routines can reduce time together. **解释2：** Work and distance make arrangements harder. **例子2：** For instance, a reunion may need to fit several different schedules.

#### P013-Q6 Do expensive presents show greater affection?

**观点1：** Not necessarily, because thoughtfulness matters more than price. **解释1：** A suitable gift shows understanding of the recipient. **例子1：** For example, a handmade card can refer to a shared memory.

**观点2：** However, practical value can also be meaningful. **解释2：** A gift may solve a real difficulty. **例子2：** For instance, useful equipment can support a relative's long-standing hobby.


## P014 擅长做手工的人

截图：第5张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[Ieltsanswers 当季话题整理](https://www.ieltsanswers.com/ielts-speaking-test-questions-with-model-answers.html)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe someone who is skilled at making or repairing things by hand.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P42父亲修车改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The person I'd like to describe is my father. He is patient and practical, and I particularly admire his ability to repair small things by hand.

One memory comes from when I was about seven. My favourite toy was a red remote-control car. While I was playing with our dog, Max, he suddenly bit the car and damaged one of its wheels. I thought the toy was ruined and became very upset.

My father sat down with it that evening and examined the broken parts. Using simple tools, glue and tape, he gradually put the wheel back into place. He tested it several times instead of rushing the job. Eventually, the car could move again.

What impressed me was not just the result, but the calm way he worked. He looked closely at the problem and used what was available. I still keep that car because it reminds me of his skill, patience and care.

It also made me appreciate repairing an object rather than immediately replacing it, especially when the object is connected with an important memory from childhood.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：手工与创造力；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P014-Q1 Why is creativity useful in daily life?

**观点1：** Creativity helps people use limited resources. **解释1：** They can find another approach instead of immediately spending money. **例子1：** For example, rearranging furniture may improve a small room.

**观点2：** It also helps people respond to unfamiliar problems. **解释2：** A standard solution may not suit every situation. **例子2：** For instance, a shopkeeper may cooperate with a neighbour to solve a payment problem.

#### P014-Q2 Can creativity be taught?

**观点1：** It can be encouraged through practice. **解释1：** Trying different solutions helps people become less afraid of unusual ideas. **例子1：** For example, children can design several objects from the same materials.

**观点2：** A supportive environment also matters. **解释2：** People need room to make mistakes without ridicule. **例子2：** For instance, a teacher can discuss what a failed design still teaches.

#### P014-Q3 Should children learn to make things by hand?

**观点1：** Yes, practical activities develop patience. **解释1：** Children see that a result requires several careful steps. **例子1：** For example, making a card involves planning, cutting and decorating.

**观点2：** They also connect ideas with physical materials. **解释2：** Children discover limits that are hard to understand through pictures alone. **例子2：** For instance, a paper bridge must actually hold its own shape.

#### P014-Q4 Are handmade gifts better than purchased gifts?

**观点1：** Handmade gifts can feel personal. **解释1：** The giver invests time and includes individual details. **例子1：** For example, a card can mention a shared memory.

**观点2：** However, a purchased gift may be more useful. **解释2：** Understanding the recipient matters more than the production method. **例子2：** For instance, suitable sports equipment can support a friend's regular hobby.

#### P014-Q5 Why do people repair old objects?

**观点1：** Repairing can save money and materials. **解释1：** A small damaged part does not always make the whole item useless. **例子1：** For example, replacing a wheel may restore a toy car.

**观点2：** People also want to preserve memories. **解释2：** A replacement cannot carry the same personal history. **例子2：** For instance, a repaired childhood toy may represent a parent's care.

#### P014-Q6 Is imagination important at work?

**观点1：** Yes, imagination helps people consider alternatives. **解释1：** A difficult task may need more than an existing routine. **例子1：** For example, a team can present complex data through a clearer visual explanation.

**观点2：** However, ideas need practical checking. **解释2：** An attractive possibility may fail in real use. **例子2：** For instance, a new design should be tested before everyone depends on it.


## P015 听不感兴趣的话

截图：第5张，显示共4个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a conversation you kept listening to despite lacking interest.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿Luwen与CFA素材改编；谈话情节补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a conversation with my friend Luwen after a training session. He was preparing for the CFA exam and wanted to talk about a detailed topic he had been studying.

I was tired and had hoped for a light conversation about the game. Instead, he began explaining a complicated calculation. I could follow some of it, but I was not particularly interested in that detail at the time.

However, I kept listening because he seemed enthusiastic and clearly wanted to share his progress. I put my phone away and asked him to explain one part in simpler language. That made the conversation easier to follow and gave him a chance to organise his thoughts.

In the end, I felt glad that I had not interrupted him rudely. Friends do not always have identical interests, but listening can still show care. I would hope for the same patience when I become excited about a technical problem at work.

That small effort to understand each other is one reason I think our friendship has remained strong over time.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：倾听与消息；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P015-Q1 What shows that someone is listening carefully?

**观点1：** Relevant questions show attention. **解释1：** The listener responds to what was actually said. **例子1：** For example, asking about one unclear step shows an effort to understand.

**观点2：** Appropriate body language also helps. **解释2：** People feel heard when attention is visibly directed towards them. **例子2：** For instance, putting a phone away can make a conversation more comfortable.

#### P015-Q2 Should people listen to topics they do not enjoy?

**观点1：** Sometimes, because listening shows consideration. **解释1：** The topic may matter to the person speaking. **例子1：** For example, a friend may want to share progress on a difficult exam.

**观点2：** However, people can set polite limits. **解释2：** Attention becomes less useful when someone is exhausted or rushed. **例子2：** For instance, they can suggest continuing the conversation another time.

#### P015-Q3 Why do people sometimes reply slowly to messages?

**观点1：** They may be concentrating on another responsibility. **解释1：** A delayed reply does not necessarily indicate a lack of care. **例子1：** For example, a student may put the phone away while revising.

**观点2：** The message may also be unclear about urgency. **解释2：** People cannot always guess when an answer is needed. **例子2：** For instance, a group organiser should state a decision deadline.

#### P015-Q4 Is face-to-face conversation better than messaging?

**观点1：** Face-to-face conversation provides more emotional information. **解释1：** Expressions and tone help people interpret meaning. **例子1：** For example, a sensitive disagreement may be easier to resolve in person.

**观点2：** Messaging offers flexibility. **解释2：** People can communicate when their schedules do not match. **例子2：** For instance, friends in different cities can maintain regular contact through short messages.

#### P015-Q5 How can misunderstandings be reduced?

**观点1：** People can check what they have understood. **解释1：** Repeating the main point reveals differences early. **例子1：** For example, teammates can confirm the agreed meeting time.

**观点2：** Clear language also reduces confusion. **解释2：** Unnecessary detail may hide the main request. **例子2：** For instance, a work message can state the task and deadline before explaining the background.

#### P015-Q6 Do people need to respond to every message immediately?

**观点1：** No, immediate replies can interrupt important work. **解释1：** People need time when they can concentrate. **例子1：** For example, constant notifications can make a complex report take longer.

**观点2：** However, expectations should be communicated. **解释2：** Others need to know how urgent matters will be handled. **例子2：** For instance, a team can agree to call when a quick answer is essential.


## P016 喜欢拍照的人

截图：第5张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[Ieltsanswers 当季话题整理](https://www.ieltsanswers.com/ielts-speaking-test-questions-with-model-answers.html)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe someone who enjoys taking photographs.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿拍照、家庭合照、青岛素材改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to talk about my friend Luwen, who enjoys taking photographs whenever we go out together. I noticed this interest during our trips and group activities.

He does not only take pictures of famous attractions. He also notices small details, such as the colour of the sky, an interesting reflection or someone laughing naturally. He says those ordinary moments often tell a better story than a carefully arranged pose.

During a seaside trip, he took several photos while we were choosing food and chatting. At the time, I barely noticed the camera. Later, the pictures helped me remember the atmosphere of the whole evening, rather than just the place itself.

I like his approach because the photographs preserve shared experiences. He also asks before posting a picture of someone online, which I appreciate. His interest has encouraged me to pay more attention to my surroundings and to keep a few meaningful photographs instead of hundreds of almost identical ones.

I now try to notice the story in a picture, rather than judge it only by its technical quality.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：拍照与记录；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P016-Q1 Why do people take photographs?

**观点1：** Photographs preserve experiences. **解释1：** They help people remember details that might otherwise fade. **例子1：** For example, a family picture can recall the atmosphere of a celebration.

**观点2：** Photography also encourages observation. **解释2：** People begin noticing light, expressions and unusual shapes. **例子2：** For instance, a walk may become more interesting when someone looks for a distinctive view.

#### P016-Q2 Are printed photographs still valuable?

**观点1：** Yes, printed pictures can feel more personal. **解释1：** They become visible parts of a home rather than hidden files. **例子1：** For example, a family photograph on a shelf invites conversation.

**观点2：** Digital photographs are easier to store and share. **解释2：** People can keep many images without physical space. **例子2：** For instance, relatives can receive holiday pictures immediately.

#### P016-Q3 Should people ask before photographing strangers?

**观点1：** Usually, asking is considerate when someone is the clear subject. **解释1：** People may not want their image recorded or shared. **例子1：** For example, a close portrait should involve permission.

**观点2：** The situation also matters. **解释2：** A broad public scene differs from an intrusive personal photograph. **例子2：** For instance, people in a distant crowd may be incidental to a landscape.

#### P016-Q4 Do tourists take too many pictures?

**观点1：** Some do, and it can reduce direct enjoyment. **解释1：** They may focus on recording every moment instead of experiencing the place. **例子1：** For example, a visitor might watch a whole performance through a screen.

**观点2：** However, a few thoughtful pictures can enrich memories. **解释2：** Photography can encourage attention to detail. **例子2：** For instance, an unusual doorway may inspire someone to learn local history.

#### P016-Q5 Has technology changed photography?

**观点1：** It has made taking pictures more accessible. **解释1：** People can record ordinary moments without carrying separate equipment. **例子1：** For example, a phone can capture an unexpected family laugh.

**观点2：** It has also increased the need to select carefully. **解释2：** Large collections can become difficult to manage. **例子2：** For instance, choosing a few meaningful images may be more useful than keeping every attempt.

#### P016-Q6 Is a good photograph mainly about expensive equipment?

**观点1：** No, observation and timing are often more important. **解释1：** A clear idea can make an ordinary subject interesting. **例子1：** For example, a natural smile may matter more than technical sharpness.

**观点2：** However, suitable equipment can help in difficult conditions. **解释2：** Some situations demand features that basic devices lack. **例子2：** For instance, photographing distant action may require a different setup.


## P017 小时候学到的新技能

截图：第5张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a skill you learned as a child.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿Part1 Typing改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The skill I would like to describe is typing, which I began learning in primary school. We had basic computer lessons, and using a keyboard felt exciting and unfamiliar at the same time.

At first, I searched for every letter with one finger. I made frequent mistakes and had to look back and forth between the screen and the keys. Our teacher showed us a more organised way to place our hands and gave us short passages to practise.

I improved gradually through repetition. It was satisfying to finish a sentence with fewer mistakes than before. I also started to understand that speed was less useful if I had to spend a long time correcting errors.

Today, typing is part of my everyday work as a data scientist. I use it for coding, messages and learning notes. Looking back, a fairly ordinary school lesson gave me a practical skill that has remained useful for years, which makes the experience especially meaningful.

It is satisfying to realise that patient practice in childhood can still make an ordinary working day easier now.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：学习与教学；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P017-Q1 What makes a lesson memorable?

**观点1：** Active participation makes a lesson memorable. **解释1：** Doing something gives learners a concrete experience to recall. **例子1：** For example, practising a frisbee catch is clearer than hearing instructions alone.

**观点2：** A supportive teacher also helps. **解释2：** People learn more comfortably when mistakes are treated patiently. **例子2：** For instance, a language teacher can encourage a hesitant student to finish speaking.

#### P017-Q2 Is learning alone better than learning with others?

**观点1：** Learning alone offers control over pace. **解释1：** People can repeat difficult material without holding anyone back. **例子1：** For example, a learner can replay a short coding demonstration.

**观点2：** Group learning provides feedback and motivation. **解释2：** Other people notice problems and suggest different approaches. **例子2：** For instance, a teammate can point out why a catching technique is not working.

#### P017-Q3 What qualities make a good teacher?

**观点1：** A good teacher explains ideas clearly. **解释1：** They connect unfamiliar material with something learners understand. **例子1：** For example, a simple daily example can clarify an abstract concept.

**观点2：** Patience is equally valuable. **解释2：** Different students need different amounts of practice. **例子2：** For instance, a teacher may demonstrate the same movement again without embarrassing a beginner.

#### P017-Q4 Can adults learn new skills as well as children?

**观点1：** Adults can learn effectively through clear goals. **解释1：** They often understand why a skill matters to them. **例子1：** For example, someone learning English for work may practise consistently.

**观点2：** Children may be more willing to experiment. **解释2：** They can explore without worrying as much about appearing inexperienced. **例子2：** For instance, children often try unfamiliar games immediately.

#### P017-Q5 Can online videos replace teachers?

**观点1：** Videos are useful for accessible explanations. **解释1：** Learners can pause and repeat a demonstration whenever needed. **例子1：** For example, a beginner can watch a basic cooking technique several times.

**观点2：** However, videos cannot always provide personal feedback. **解释2：** The learner may repeat an error without noticing it. **例子2：** For instance, a teacher can correct an unclear pronunciation immediately.

#### P017-Q6 Why is practice important when learning?

**观点1：** Practice helps knowledge become usable. **解释1：** Understanding an explanation does not guarantee smooth performance. **例子1：** For example, knowing keyboard positions differs from typing a report accurately.

**观点2：** Practice also reveals specific weaknesses. **解释2：** Learners can then focus their effort more effectively. **例子2：** For instance, recording a speech may show that examples need clearer explanations.


## P018 攒钱买特别物品

截图：第5张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a time you saved money for a special purchase.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体物品；为什么想要；试用、储蓄或购买过程；价格与预算如何影响你的决定。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P13/P19 VR素材改编；储蓄过程补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to talk about saving money for a VR headset. I became interested after trying one and being impressed by how immersive the experience felt.

The headset was expensive enough that I did not want to buy it immediately. Instead, I set a spending limit and compared different options. For a few months, I cut back on impulse purchases and put aside part of my spare money.

The hardest part was being patient when I saw advertisements or discounts. I reminded myself that the purpose was to buy something I really wanted without creating pressure on my normal expenses. I also checked whether the model would suit the activities I planned to use it for.

When I finally bought it, I felt pleased and a little proud. The purchase represented several months of planning rather than a sudden decision. It also taught me that waiting can make it easier to distinguish a lasting interest from a temporary desire.

I would use a similar saving plan again if another expensive item became a serious priority for me in future.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：消费与储蓄；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P018-Q1 Why is saving money important?

**观点1：** Savings provide flexibility when circumstances change. **解释1：** People can deal with unexpected costs more comfortably. **例子1：** For example, a broken computer may need replacement before the next payday.

**观点2：** Saving also supports larger goals. **解释2：** A regular habit makes an expensive purchase more manageable. **例子2：** For instance, someone can gradually put money aside for useful training.

#### P018-Q2 Why do people buy things they do not need?

**观点1：** Advertisements can create a temporary desire. **解释1：** Attractive presentation makes an item feel more valuable than it is. **例子1：** For example, a discount may encourage an unnecessary electronic upgrade.

**观点2：** People may also seek a quick emotional reward. **解释2：** Buying something new can briefly make a difficult day feel better. **例子2：** For instance, someone may order clothes after a stressful week.

#### P018-Q3 Should children learn to manage money?

**观点1：** Yes, simple practice develops awareness of limits. **解释1：** Children can learn that spending on one thing affects other choices. **例子1：** For example, a small allowance can be divided between snacks and savings.

**观点2：** Parents should also explain value. **解释2：** Price alone does not show whether something is useful. **例子2：** For instance, a durable school bag may be better than a fashionable replacement.

#### P018-Q4 Is shopping easier than saving?

**观点1：** Shopping is often easier in the short term. **解释1：** The reward is immediate and the process can be convenient. **例子1：** For example, a person can order a product with very little effort.

**观点2：** Saving becomes easier with a clear purpose. **解释2：** A specific goal makes delaying a purchase feel worthwhile. **例子2：** For instance, planning a family trip can motivate regular saving.

#### P018-Q5 Are expensive products always better?

**观点1：** No, price may reflect branding rather than practical quality. **解释1：** A buyer should consider the features they will actually use. **例子1：** For example, basic headphones may suit occasional listening.

**观点2：** However, higher quality can justify extra cost. **解释2：** A durable item may last longer or perform more reliably. **例子2：** For instance, comfortable sports shoes can be worth paying more for.

#### P018-Q6 How can people avoid spending beyond their budget?

**观点1：** They can decide a limit before browsing. **解释1：** This reduces the influence of attractive displays and upgrades. **例子1：** For example, a shopper can compare only products within an affordable range.

**观点2：** They can also delay nonessential purchases. **解释2：** Waiting reveals whether the interest lasts. **例子2：** For instance, someone may lose enthusiasm for a gadget after a few days.


## P019 喜欢的城市自然之地

截图：第5张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a natural place you enjoy within your city.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿Part1附近公园改编；具体场景补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a public park near my home in Shanghai. It is not a famous attraction, but it is one of the places I enjoy most in my everyday routine.

There are trees along the walking paths and several quiet areas where people can sit. The surrounding streets are busy, so the change in atmosphere is noticeable as soon as I enter. I sometimes go there after work, especially when I have spent the whole day looking at a screen.

Usually, I walk slowly and let my thoughts settle. If a friend joins me, we talk about ordinary things rather than work. I also take photographs when the light looks interesting through the trees.

I like this place because it is easy to reach and does not require a special plan. I do not have to wait for a holiday to enjoy some greenery. Even a short visit can make a demanding day feel more balanced and manageable.

I hope it stays accessible and well maintained, because small local parks can make everyday city life more pleasant.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：自然与植物；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P019-Q1 Why do people enjoy visiting natural places?

**观点1：** Natural surroundings provide a change from demanding routines. **解释1：** People can slow down and focus on something beyond work. **例子1：** For example, a walk among trees can feel refreshing after computer tasks.

**观点2：** They also offer shared recreation. **解释2：** Friends can spend time together without a complicated programme. **例子2：** For instance, a park visit can combine conversation and gentle exercise.

#### P019-Q2 Should cities provide more green spaces?

**观点1：** Yes, nearby greenery makes recreation more accessible. **解释1：** Residents do not always have time to travel far. **例子1：** For example, a small neighbourhood park can support an evening walk.
