**观点2：** Green spaces also improve shared surroundings. **解释2：** They give people pleasant places to meet and rest. **例子2：** For instance, shaded seating can make a busy district more welcoming.

#### P019-Q3 How can people be encouraged to protect nature?

**观点1：** Practical activities make the issue tangible. **解释1：** People see the effect of their own behaviour. **例子1：** For example, collecting litter can reveal how much waste visitors leave.

**观点2：** Clear facilities also support better choices. **解释2：** Good intentions are easier to follow when alternatives are convenient. **例子2：** For instance, visible bins can help visitors dispose of rubbish properly.

#### P019-Q4 Why do some people enjoy growing plants?

**观点1：** Growing plants offers visible progress. **解释1：** Small changes provide a sense of achievement. **例子1：** For example, a new leaf can reward weeks of regular care.

**观点2：** The activity can also create a calming routine. **解释2：** People focus on simple tasks away from screens. **例子2：** For instance, checking a few pots can become a pleasant morning habit.

#### P019-Q5 Should children spend more time outdoors?

**观点1：** Yes, outdoor activity offers different experiences from classroom learning. **解释1：** Children can move, explore and notice living things. **例子1：** For example, a park visit may lead to questions about insects.

**观点2：** However, the activity should suit the setting. **解释2：** Adults need to balance freedom with practical supervision. **例子2：** For instance, a nature walk can use clear boundaries while allowing exploration.

#### P019-Q6 Can tourism harm natural places?

**观点1：** Yes, heavy use can damage the environment. **解释1：** Visitors may leave rubbish or disturb sensitive areas. **例子1：** For example, walking away from paths can affect vegetation.

**观点2：** However, well-managed tourism can support protection. **解释2：** Visitor interest can create reasons to maintain a place carefully. **例子2：** For instance, clear routes and educational signs can guide more considerate behaviour.


## P020 有条理的人

截图：第5张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[Ieltsanswers 当季话题整理](https://www.ieltsanswers.com/ielts-speaking-test-questions-with-model-answers.html)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a person who makes plans a lot and is good at planning

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P06原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, I'd like to talk about Luwen, a friend who is really good at planning.

I knew him through my university frisbee team. At one time, he was preparing for the CFA exam while attending club training, so he had tons of tasks on his plate. However, he was not affected too much by stress. He made realistic daily plans, split big tasks into smaller steps and used simple tools to prioritize his work.

What impressed me most was that he could still keep calm and efficient even when he struggled with all kinds of tasks. I feel he is organized, self-driven and reliable. I really want to learn this habit from him.

One practical detail I noticed was that he left some empty space in his schedule. If a task took longer than expected, he could adjust without abandoning the whole plan. He also checked his progress at the end of the week. That flexibility made his planning useful rather than stressful, and it is the part I would most like to copy in my own routine.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：计划与条理；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P020-Q1 Why do people plan their daily activities?

**观点1：** Planning helps people identify priorities. **解释1：** Without a clear order, easy tasks can replace important ones. **例子1：** For example, a student can revise a difficult chapter before checking messages.

**观点2：** It also makes cooperation easier. **解释2：** Other people can prepare when they know the schedule. **例子2：** For instance, teammates can arrange transport before a training session.

#### P020-Q2 Should children learn to make plans?

**观点1：** Yes, small plans can develop responsibility. **解释1：** Children learn that an activity requires preparation. **例子1：** For example, they can pack school materials the evening before.

**观点2：** However, plans should leave room for play. **解释2：** An overly detailed schedule can make ordinary days stressful. **例子2：** For instance, a free afternoon lets a child choose a game independently.

#### P020-Q3 Is a flexible plan better than a strict one?

**观点1：** Usually, flexibility makes a plan more realistic. **解释1：** Unexpected delays are part of everyday life. **例子1：** For example, extra travel time prevents one late bus from ruining a schedule.

**观点2：** However, some fixed points are necessary. **解释2：** Other people need commitments they can rely on. **例子2：** For instance, a group still needs an agreed meeting time.

#### P020-Q4 How can someone become more organised?

**观点1：** They can give important items a fixed place. **解释1：** This reduces the effort needed to find things. **例子1：** For example, keeping keys by the door avoids a rushed search.

**观点2：** They can also review priorities regularly. **解释2：** A list becomes less useful when circumstances change. **例子2：** For instance, a worker can update tomorrow's tasks before leaving the office.

#### P020-Q5 Does planning always lead to success?

**观点1：** Planning improves the chance of success. **解释1：** It turns a large goal into manageable actions. **例子1：** For example, a revision timetable can spread practice across several weeks.

**观点2：** However, results also depend on execution. **解释2：** A detailed plan achieves little if nobody follows it. **例子2：** For instance, a team must actually practise the skills listed in its programme.

#### P020-Q6 Why do some people dislike making plans?

**观点1：** Some people value freedom and spontaneity. **解释1：** A fixed schedule can feel restrictive during leisure time. **例子1：** For example, a traveller may want to explore a street unexpectedly.

**观点2：** Others have experienced unrealistic plans. **解释2：** Repeated failure can make planning itself seem pointless. **例子2：** For instance, an exhausting study timetable may discourage someone from trying again.


## P021 看过的名人采访

截图：第5张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an interview with a famous person that you watched.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体对象；怎样接触或获得；外观、内容或用途；它为什么对你重要。

**答案来源：** 新编示范：未指定真实采访；运动主题借用旧素材。
示范提醒：采访、广告或社区通知的具体情节为练习补写，未确认某次真实节目、广告或新闻事件。


### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to describe an online interview with a well-known athlete that I watched one evening at home. I came across it while looking for something interesting about sport.

The interviewer asked about training, setbacks and the pressure of competition. What interested me most was the discussion of failure. Instead of presenting success as something easy, the athlete explained that progress involved repeating basic skills and learning from disappointing results.

That idea reminded me of my own experience on a frisbee team. When a match went badly, our captain Luwen encouraged us to concentrate on the next action rather than complain about the score. The interview expressed a similar attitude on a larger stage.

I enjoyed it because it showed the ordinary effort behind a public image. I would not remember every detail of the conversation, but its main message stayed with me. It made sporting success feel less mysterious and gave me something practical to apply to my own learning.

I would choose another interview like it because honest discussion of effort is more useful than a polished image.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：名人与采访；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P021-Q1 Why do people admire famous athletes?

**观点1：** Athletes demonstrate achievement through visible effort. **解释1：** People can see skill and determination during competition. **例子1：** For example, a comeback may show how someone responds to pressure.

**观点2：** They can also represent shared interests. **解释2：** Supporters feel connected through following the same sport. **例子2：** For instance, a local champion may bring attention to a community team.

#### P021-Q2 Should famous people be role models?

**观点1：** They can offer useful examples of effort. **解释1：** Young people may become interested in a positive activity. **例子1：** For example, an athlete can encourage a child to try sport.

**观点2：** However, fame does not guarantee good judgement. **解释2：** Public achievements reveal only part of someone's character. **例子2：** For instance, being a skilled performer does not make every personal opinion reliable.

#### P021-Q3 Why do people watch interviews with celebrities?

**观点1：** Interviews can reveal the process behind achievement. **解释1：** Viewers want more than a polished public image. **例子1：** For example, an athlete discussing setbacks may explain how progress happened.

**观点2：** They also satisfy personal curiosity. **解释2：** Fans enjoy learning about interests and experiences. **例子2：** For instance, a musician's account of learning an instrument can feel more personal than a performance.

#### P021-Q4 What makes a good interview?

**观点1：** Good questions invite specific explanations. **解释1：** Vague questions often produce rehearsed answers. **例子1：** For example, asking about a difficult decision can reveal someone's thinking.

**观点2：** Careful listening is equally important. **解释2：** The interviewer should respond to interesting details. **例子2：** For instance, a follow-up question can clarify an unexpected point rather than rushing to the next topic.

#### P021-Q5 Is local fame different from international fame?

**观点1：** Local fame often involves direct community connections. **解释1：** People may know the person's everyday contribution. **例子1：** For example, a respected coach can be familiar to players and families.

**观点2：** International fame depends more on media exposure. **解释2：** Most followers never meet the person. **例子2：** For instance, an athlete's image may be shaped mainly by broadcasts and online coverage.

#### P021-Q6 Would meeting a famous person change someone's life?

**观点1：** It might provide encouragement or a useful insight. **解释1：** A personal conversation can make an ambition feel more concrete. **例子1：** For example, a young player may gain motivation from practical advice.

**观点2：** However, lasting change still requires action. **解释2：** Admiration alone does not develop a skill. **例子2：** For instance, the player must continue training after the meeting ends.


## P022 快乐人士

截图：第5张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a happy person you know.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** Luwen、运动与冥想素材改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The happy person I would like to talk about is my friend Luwen. We became friends through sport, and I have always enjoyed his calm, positive company.

He is not someone who laughs loudly all the time. His happiness is more visible in the way he appreciates ordinary things. After a training session, he can be pleased about a good catch, a useful conversation or simply having time to eat with friends.

I remember a day when our plans changed because of the weather. Several of us were disappointed, but he suggested an easy indoor activity and helped everyone relax. He did not pretend that the problem was wonderful; he simply found something we could still enjoy.

I think his balanced routine contributes to this attitude. He makes room for exercise, friendships and quiet breaks even when he is busy. Being around him reminds me that happiness does not have to depend on a major achievement or an expensive experience.

His example encourages me to notice small enjoyable moments instead of waiting for everything in my life to become perfect.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：快乐与微笑；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P022-Q1 What makes people happy in everyday life?

**观点1：** Supportive relationships are a major source of happiness. **解释1：** People feel valued when someone listens and shares their time. **例子1：** For example, a relaxed meal with friends can improve an ordinary evening.

**观点2：** Progress also creates satisfaction. **解释2：** Completing something difficult shows that effort has mattered. **例子2：** For instance, finishing a challenging project can leave someone feeling capable.

#### P022-Q2 Can money make people happier?

**观点1：** Money can reduce practical pressure. **解释1：** Meeting essential needs leaves people with more choices. **例子1：** For example, affordable housing can make daily life less stressful.

**观点2：** However, spending does not guarantee lasting enjoyment. **解释2：** A new object may lose its appeal quickly. **例子2：** For instance, a gadget may be forgotten while a shared family experience remains meaningful.

#### P022-Q3 Why do people smile at social events?

**观点1：** Smiling often expresses genuine enjoyment. **解释1：** Shared activities create moments of warmth and amusement. **例子1：** For example, an unexpected joke can make a whole family laugh.

**观点2：** It can also help others feel welcome. **解释2：** A friendly expression reduces tension between unfamiliar people. **例子2：** For instance, a host's smile can make a new guest more comfortable.

#### P022-Q4 Are children happier than adults?

**观点1：** Children may enjoy the present more easily. **解释1：** They often have fewer long-term responsibilities. **例子1：** For example, an afternoon game can absorb their full attention.

**观点2：** Adults can also find deeper satisfaction through choice and achievement. **解释2：** Independence gives them control over meaningful goals. **例子2：** For instance, completing a project or supporting family can create lasting pride.

#### P022-Q5 How can schools make students feel happier?

**观点1：** Schools can create a supportive atmosphere. **解释1：** Students need to feel safe asking questions and making mistakes. **例子1：** For example, patient feedback can help a nervous learner participate.

**观点2：** They should also provide varied activities. **解释2：** Enjoyment does not come only from academic success. **例子2：** For instance, clubs let children build friendships through art or sport.

#### P022-Q6 Is happiness mainly a matter of attitude?

**观点1：** Attitude can influence what people notice. **解释1：** Appreciating ordinary moments can make a routine more enjoyable. **例子1：** For example, someone may value a quiet walk after work.

**观点2：** However, circumstances also matter. **解释2：** A positive outlook cannot remove every practical difficulty. **例子2：** For instance, heavy responsibilities may require real support rather than advice to smile more.


## P023 改变重要决定

截图：第5张，显示共2个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an important decision you later changed.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P20小目标、P46冥想改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a decision about my study routine that I later changed. When I first began improving my English seriously, I decided to study for a long period every evening.

The plan sounded impressive, but it did not fit my working life. After a demanding day, I often felt too tired to complete it. Missing one session made me frustrated, and I started to associate studying with failure.

After discussing the problem with Luwen, I changed my decision. Instead of insisting on a long session, I chose a shorter daily practice and left more demanding work for free days. I also focused on a small number of useful questions at a time.

The new plan was less ambitious on paper, but much easier to maintain. I felt relieved because I could see progress without exhausting myself. Changing my decision taught me that a realistic routine can be more valuable than a perfect plan that I rarely follow.

I now see changing a plan as a useful response to experience, rather than a sign that I lack determination.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：决定与改变；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P023-Q1 Should important decisions be made quickly?

**观点1：** Usually, important decisions deserve time for relevant information. **解释1：** Rushing can hide costs or practical difficulties. **例子1：** For example, choosing a course requires checking its schedule and content.

**观点2：** However, endless delay can also be unhelpful. **解释2：** Some uncertainty remains even after careful thought. **例子2：** For instance, a suitable opportunity may disappear while someone keeps comparing similar options.

#### P023-Q2 Who should help young people make decisions?

**观点1：** Parents can provide experience and support. **解释1：** They may notice consequences that young people overlook. **例子1：** For example, they can help compare the practical costs of study options.

**观点2：** Young people should still have a voice. **解释2：** They are more committed when they understand and accept the choice. **例子2：** For instance, selecting a hobby should reflect the child's actual interests.

#### P023-Q3 Why do people change their decisions?

**观点1：** New information can alter what seems sensible. **解释1：** The original plan may no longer fit the situation. **例子1：** For example, bad weather can make an outdoor event unsuitable.

**观点2：** Experience also reveals unrealistic expectations. **解释2：** Trying something shows limits that planning missed. **例子2：** For instance, a long study session may prove impossible after a full working day.

#### P023-Q4 Is advice from friends useful for decisions?

**观点1：** Yes, friends can offer a different perspective. **解释1：** They may recognise habits or strengths that someone overlooks. **例子1：** For example, a friend can suggest a more realistic daily routine.

**观点2：** However, advice should be evaluated personally. **解释2：** Friends do not share every responsibility or preference. **例子2：** For instance, a career choice must fit the decision-maker's own circumstances.

#### P023-Q5 What should a group do when members disagree?

**观点1：** The group should clarify its shared purpose. **解释1：** People may agree on the goal while preferring different methods. **例子1：** For example, friends may all want time together despite disagreeing about the venue.

**观点2：** It should then compare practical options fairly. **解释2：** Listening prevents the loudest person from deciding everything. **例子2：** For instance, budget and travel time can guide a compromise.

#### P023-Q6 Does a good result mean a decision was good?

**观点1：** A good result can suggest the choice worked well. **解释1：** It may show that planning matched the circumstances. **例子1：** For example, a manageable study routine can lead to consistent practice.

**观点2：** However, luck also affects outcomes. **解释2：** The decision process matters as well as the result. **例子2：** For instance, a risky choice may succeed once without being sensible to repeat.


## P024 本地名人

截图：第5张，显示共3个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a locally famous person.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P07原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe Luwen, a frisbee coach who is well known in our local sporting community. I know him through my university team and admire the way he supports players.

It dates back to about three years ago when I was first introduced to him after I joined my university frisbee team. Luwen is my coach. He looks confident, friendly, calm and approachable.

He has won countless city championships and was once ranked number one in the whole city. His skills and determination truly set him apart. There are several reasons why I admire him. First of all, he is a natural unifier of the team. Whenever anyone runs into difficulties, he is always the first to check in and see how he can help.

In addition, I remember watching a crucial match where his team was losing. His calmness and confidence were inspiring. He managed to turn the whole situation around and finally made it. That is why I really look up to him, not just as an athlete, but also as a person who motivates others.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：名人与采访；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P024-Q1 Why do people admire famous athletes?

**观点1：** Athletes demonstrate achievement through visible effort. **解释1：** People can see skill and determination during competition. **例子1：** For example, a comeback may show how someone responds to pressure.

**观点2：** They can also represent shared interests. **解释2：** Supporters feel connected through following the same sport. **例子2：** For instance, a local champion may bring attention to a community team.

#### P024-Q2 Should famous people be role models?

**观点1：** They can offer useful examples of effort. **解释1：** Young people may become interested in a positive activity. **例子1：** For example, an athlete can encourage a child to try sport.

**观点2：** However, fame does not guarantee good judgement. **解释2：** Public achievements reveal only part of someone's character. **例子2：** For instance, being a skilled performer does not make every personal opinion reliable.

#### P024-Q3 Why do people watch interviews with celebrities?

**观点1：** Interviews can reveal the process behind achievement. **解释1：** Viewers want more than a polished public image. **例子1：** For example, an athlete discussing setbacks may explain how progress happened.

**观点2：** They also satisfy personal curiosity. **解释2：** Fans enjoy learning about interests and experiences. **例子2：** For instance, a musician's account of learning an instrument can feel more personal than a performance.

#### P024-Q4 What makes a good interview?

**观点1：** Good questions invite specific explanations. **解释1：** Vague questions often produce rehearsed answers. **例子1：** For example, asking about a difficult decision can reveal someone's thinking.

**观点2：** Careful listening is equally important. **解释2：** The interviewer should respond to interesting details. **例子2：** For instance, a follow-up question can clarify an unexpected point rather than rushing to the next topic.

#### P024-Q5 Is local fame different from international fame?

**观点1：** Local fame often involves direct community connections. **解释1：** People may know the person's everyday contribution. **例子1：** For example, a respected coach can be familiar to players and families.

**观点2：** International fame depends more on media exposure. **解释2：** Most followers never meet the person. **例子2：** For instance, an athlete's image may be shaped mainly by broadcasts and online coverage.

#### P024-Q6 Would meeting a famous person change someone's life?

**观点1：** It might provide encouragement or a useful insight. **解释1：** A personal conversation can make an ambition feel more concrete. **例子1：** For example, a young player may gain motivation from practical advice.

**观点2：** However, lasting change still requires action. **解释2：** Admiration alone does not develop a skill. **例子2：** For instance, the player must continue training after the meeting ends.


## P025 对结果开心的重要决定

截图：第5张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an important decision whose outcome pleased you.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P46原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to describe my decision to take a meditation course with a friend. I made it last year, and I was pleased with how it helped me build a calmer routine.

It was last year when I was under a lot of stress from both study and daily life. My best friend and I discussed it and finally made the decision to take a meditation course together.

The reason why we made this decision was that both of us had a tight schedule and often struggled with sleep. My friend helped me make the decision because he had already tried meditation for a while and found it helpful. He told me it could calm my mind and help me get my head straight.

In the end, I felt it was a wise decision. It helped me recharge myself, regain inner peace and build a better routine.

The result mattered because I could continue using what I learned after the course ended. It became a small practical improvement to everyday life, rather than just a pleasant afternoon with a friend.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：决定与改变；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

**观点2：** However, endless delay can also be unhelpful. **解释2：** Some uncertainty remains even after careful thought. **例子2：** For instance, a suitable opportunity may disappear while someone keeps comparing similar options.

#### P025-Q2 Who should help young people make decisions?

**观点1：** Parents can provide experience and support. **解释1：** They may notice consequences that young people overlook. **例子1：** For example, they can help compare the practical costs of study options.

**观点2：** Young people should still have a voice. **解释2：** They are more committed when they understand and accept the choice. **例子2：** For instance, selecting a hobby should reflect the child's actual interests.

**观点2：** Experience also reveals unrealistic expectations. **解释2：** Trying something shows limits that planning missed. **例子2：** For instance, a long study session may prove impossible after a full working day.

**观点1：** Yes, friends can offer a different perspective. **解释1：** They may recognise habits or strengths that someone overlooks. **例子1：** For example, a friend can suggest a more realistic daily routine.

**观点2：** However, advice should be evaluated personally. **解释2：** Friends do not share every responsibility or preference. **例子2：** For instance, a career choice must fit the decision-maker's own circumstances.

**观点1：** The group should clarify its shared purpose. **解释1：** People may agree on the goal while preferring different methods. **例子1：** For example, friends may all want time together despite disagreeing about the venue.

## P026 做困难事情并成功的人

截图：第6张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe someone who succeeded at something difficult.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P06/P36 Luwen备考改编；通过考试结果补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to talk about my friend Luwen, who passed a demanding professional exam while managing a busy routine. He was preparing for the CFA exam and also taking part in sports training.

The main difficulty was finding enough time and energy to study consistently. Some evenings, he was tired before he even opened his books. Instead of giving up, he broke the material into smaller sections and made a realistic plan for each week.

He also reviewed mistakes carefully rather than simply repeating questions he already understood. When he felt overwhelmed, he took a short break and returned to one manageable task. I sometimes talked with him about his progress and encouraged him to continue.

When he told me he had passed, I felt very pleased for him. I admired the steady effort behind the result more than the certificate itself. His experience showed me that difficult goals become more manageable when people combine patience, planning and regular practice.

His success also encouraged me to approach my own English study with more patience and a more realistic routine.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：困难与成功；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P026-Q1 How would you define success?

**观点1：** Success can mean achieving a personally meaningful goal. **解释1：** The standard should reflect what someone values. **例子1：** For example, learning to communicate confidently may matter more than a prestigious title.

**观点2：** It can also include a sustainable way of living. **解释2：** Achievement is less satisfying if it destroys everything else. **例子2：** For instance, a career should leave some room for important relationships.

#### P026-Q2 Is talent more important than hard work?

**观点1：** Talent can make the beginning easier. **解释1：** Some people understand a skill with less initial effort. **例子1：** For example, a naturally coordinated player may learn a basic movement quickly.

**观点2：** However, consistent practice develops dependable performance. **解释2：** Ability alone does not prepare someone for every difficulty. **例子2：** For instance, regular training helps an athlete respond calmly during a close match.

#### P026-Q3 Why do people give up difficult goals?

**观点1：** Some goals are too large or vague. **解释1：** People cannot see a manageable next step. **例子1：** For example, an enormous reading target may feel discouraging after one missed day.

**观点2：** A lack of feedback also weakens motivation. **解释2：** People need to notice that effort is producing progress. **例子2：** For instance, tracking a small improvement can encourage another practice session.

#### P026-Q4 Can failure be useful?

**观点1：** Yes, failure can reveal a specific weakness. **解释1：** People can adjust their approach using that information. **例子1：** For example, a lost match may expose poor communication.

**观点2：** However, reflection is necessary. **解释2：** Repeating the same actions without analysis may produce the same outcome. **例子2：** For instance, a student should review errors rather than simply attempt another identical test.

#### P026-Q5 Does money show how successful a company is?

**观点1：** Income is important because a company must remain viable. **解释1：** It needs resources to pay staff and continue operating. **例子1：** For example, a shop cannot survive indefinitely without covering costs.

**观点2：** However, reputation and useful service matter too. **解释2：** A short-term profit may hide poor treatment of customers. **例子2：** For instance, honest advice can build trust and repeat business.

#### P026-Q6 How can people stay motivated over a long period?

**观点1：** They can divide a goal into visible stages. **解释1：** Small achievements make progress easier to recognise. **例子1：** For example, completing one project can support a larger career ambition.

**观点2：** They can also connect the work with a clear purpose. **解释2：** Knowing why something matters helps during dull periods. **例子2：** For instance, learning English may support a desired professional opportunity.


## P027 受欢迎的人

截图：第6张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a popular person you know.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P01原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The popular person I'd like to describe is Luwen, one of my closest friends from my university frisbee team. Other players enjoy his company because he is helpful and patient.

I first got to know him when I joined the team. He was the captain, and he was always the first person to check in when someone ran into difficulties. For example, when I was new to frisbee, he gave me practical suggestions about throwing and catching, especially the safest way to catch, called the pancake catch. He encouraged other team members when we were falling behind in important matches.

I feel he is helpful, reliable and patient. He is not just a friend, but a mentor to me, because he showed me how to support others with calmness and confidence.

For example, new members often approach him with questions because he makes them feel welcome. He remembers what it was like to be inexperienced and avoids showing off. I think that combination of ability and consideration explains why people want to train with him and spend time together afterwards.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：朋友与人际；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P027-Q1 What makes someone a good friend?

**观点1：** Reliability is important in friendship. **解释1：** People need to know that support is available when it matters. **例子1：** For example, a friend can listen patiently during a difficult week.

**观点2：** Shared enjoyment also keeps the relationship alive. **解释2：** Friendship should include ordinary pleasant experiences. **例子2：** For instance, a regular game gives two people time to laugh together.

#### P027-Q2 Why are some people more popular than others?

**观点1：** Considerate people make others feel comfortable. **解释1：** Listening and including newcomers help people feel welcome. **例子1：** For example, a captain who supports beginners can become well liked.

**观点2：** Visible ability can also attract attention. **解释2：** People often admire skills they hope to develop. **例子2：** For instance, an excellent musician may draw others into a school club.

#### P027-Q3 Is it easy to maintain friendships after leaving school?

**观点1：** It becomes harder because routines separate. **解释1：** Friends may work in different places and have limited free time. **例子1：** For example, a spontaneous meeting may require planning several weeks ahead.

**观点2：** However, regular small contact can preserve a connection. **解释2：** Friendship does not require meeting every day. **例子2：** For instance, a thoughtful message can show continuing interest.

#### P027-Q4 Can people with different interests become close friends?

**观点1：** Yes, shared values can matter more than identical hobbies. **解释1：** People can respect and support each other despite different preferences. **例子1：** For example, a quiet reader and an athlete may both value honesty.

**观点2：** Different interests can also enrich a friendship. **解释2：** Each person introduces the other to unfamiliar experiences. **例子2：** For instance, one friend might suggest a first museum visit.

#### P027-Q5 Why do people want to meet someone again?

**观点1：** An interesting conversation can create curiosity. **解释1：** People want to understand a perspective they have only briefly encountered. **例子1：** For example, a resourceful shop owner may have useful stories about business.

**观点2：** A welcoming manner also encourages further contact. **解释2：** Feeling respected makes a second conversation appealing. **例子2：** For instance, a patient listener can leave a stronger impression than an impressive job title.

#### P027-Q6 Should friends always agree with one another?

**观点1：** No, honest disagreement can be helpful. **解释1：** A friend may notice a problem that someone has overlooked. **例子1：** For example, they can question an unrealistic study plan.

**观点2：** However, disagreement should remain respectful. **解释2：** Criticising the idea is different from insulting the person. **例子2：** For instance, offering a practical alternative keeps the conversation constructive.


## P028 想再去一次的城市

截图：第6张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a city that you have been to and would like to visit again

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P53原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The city I'd like to talk about is Qingdao, a gorgeous seaside city in China.

Last year, my family and I went there by car, and it took us about six hours to get there. We stayed there for several days. In summer, Qingdao is distinctive, vibrant and thriving. It has its own unique atmosphere.

What impressed me most was the seafood and the beer festival. The city is famous for its fresh and affordable seafood. We picked out crabs, prawns and abalone, and then had them cooked right away for a small processing fee. After that, we enjoyed Qingdao's world-renowned beer festival, which attracted tourists from all over the world.

I enjoyed visiting it because we were engrossed in a vivid and festive atmosphere. I would certainly enjoy going back when I have time.

Next time, I'd leave more free space in the schedule. I'd like to revisit the market, walk along the coast and enjoy a slower morning with my family. Returning would let us appreciate familiar places without feeling that we had to see everything at once.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：城市与拥挤；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P028-Q1 Why do people move to large cities?

**观点1：** Large cities often offer more professional opportunities. **解释1：** Different organisations create a wider range of possible roles. **例子1：** For example, a data specialist may find more relevant teams there.

**观点2：** They also provide varied services and activities. **解释2：** Residents can explore interests beyond work. **例子2：** For instance, someone may join a sports club or attend cultural events.

#### P028-Q2 What difficulties do city residents face?

**观点1：** High costs can create pressure. **解释1：** Housing and daily expenses may absorb a large part of income. **例子1：** For example, a young worker may need to share accommodation.

**观点2：** Crowded transport can also be tiring. **解释2：** A short distance may still take considerable time. **例子2：** For instance, rush-hour traffic can make an ordinary commute stressful.

#### P028-Q3 Why do tourists return to the same city?

**观点1：** Familiarity can make a trip more relaxed. **解释1：** Visitors already know some places they enjoy. **例子1：** For example, a family may return to a favourite seafood market.

**观点2：** A second visit also allows deeper exploration. **解释2：** The first trip may have covered only major attractions. **例子2：** For instance, returning visitors can spend time in quieter neighbourhoods.

#### P028-Q4 How can cities become more pleasant to live in?

**观点1：** Convenient transport can reduce everyday stress. **解释1：** Residents need practical alternatives to crowded roads. **例子1：** For example, a reliable connection can make commuting more predictable.

**观点2：** Accessible public spaces are also important. **解释2：** People need places to rest and meet outside home. **例子2：** For instance, a nearby park gives workers a simple evening break.

#### P028-Q5 Should tourists visit famous attractions or ordinary neighbourhoods?

**观点1：** Famous attractions can provide an introduction to a city. **解释1：** They often reveal distinctive scenery or history. **例子1：** For example, a waterfront may show important parts of the skyline.

**观点2：** Ordinary neighbourhoods offer a different perspective. **解释2：** Visitors can see how local people use the city. **例子2：** For instance, a market visit can reveal everyday food and conversation.

#### P028-Q6 Are crowded places always unpleasant?

**观点1：** No, crowds can create a lively atmosphere. **解释1：** Shared excitement can make an event feel special. **例子1：** For example, spectators cheering together can enrich a festival or match.

**观点2：** However, crowding becomes unpleasant when it prevents basic comfort. **解释2：** People need space, clear routes and reasonable access. **例子2：** For instance, a packed station feels stressful when someone must catch a train.


## P029 好的购物服务

截图：第6张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a time when you received good service in a shop/store

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P47原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to talk about a time when I received excellent service in a toy shop near my home.

When I was about seven years old, my father bought me a red remote-control car from that small store. It was not expensive, but I played with it every day in the park near my home. One afternoon, our naughty pet dog bit the car, and one of the wheels came off.

My father took me back to the shop. The owner was really hospitable and helpful. He didn't blame me for being careless. Instead, he checked the car carefully and fixed the wheel for free. He also gave me some practical suggestions about how to protect it.

I felt thankful because his service was warm, patient and customer-oriented. That experience left a deep impression on me.

The owner could easily have treated the repair as an inconvenience. Instead, he made a young customer feel welcome and reassured my father. That personal attention is why I remember the shop so clearly, even though the object itself was a fairly ordinary toy.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：购物与服务；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P029-Q1 What makes shopping service good?

**观点1：** Good service begins with understanding the customer's needs. **解释1：** Staff should listen before recommending a product. **例子1：** For example, a sports shop can ask how experienced a beginner is.

**观点2：** Helpful support after a purchase also matters. **解释2：** Customers want a practical response when problems occur. **例子2：** For instance, a small repair can leave a stronger impression than a sales pitch.

#### P029-Q2 Why do people shop online?

**观点1：** Online shopping saves travel time. **解释1：** People can compare options without visiting several shops. **例子1：** For example, a busy worker can check prices during a short break.

**观点2：** It also provides a broad range of choices. **解释2：** Local shops may not stock a particular item. **例子2：** For instance, someone can search for equipment suited to a specialised hobby.

#### P029-Q3 What advantages do physical shops offer?

**观点1：** Physical shops let customers inspect products directly. **解释1：** Appearance online may not show comfort or fit. **例子1：** For example, trying on shoes can prevent a poor purchase.

**观点2：** They also allow immediate conversation. **解释2：** A knowledgeable employee can respond to specific questions. **例子2：** For instance, a beginner can ask how to choose and use suitable sports equipment.

#### P029-Q4 Why do shopping centres attract people?

**观点1：** They combine several activities in one place. **解释1：** People can organise errands and entertainment together. **例子1：** For example, friends can shop and then have dinner.

**观点2：** They also provide a predictable environment. **解释2：** Visitors know they can find facilities and shelter. **例子2：** For instance, a family may choose a shopping centre during bad weather.

#### P029-Q5 Can small shops compete with large stores?

**观点1：** Yes, small shops can build personal relationships. **解释1：** Regular customers may value familiar and thoughtful service. **例子1：** For example, a baker can remember someone's preferences.

**观点2：** They can also specialise effectively. **解释2：** A narrow focus may provide knowledge that general stores lack. **例子2：** For instance, a sports shop can advise beginners on equipment and technique.

#### P029-Q6 How should businesses respond to complaints?

**观点1：** They should first understand the specific problem. **解释1：** Listening carefully prevents an irrelevant or defensive response. **例子1：** For example, staff can ask what happened before suggesting a repair.

**观点2：** They should then offer a clear next step. **解释2：** Customers need realistic expectations about the solution. **例子2：** For instance, explaining a replacement process is more useful than an empty apology.


## P030 拥挤地方

截图：第6张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a crowded place you have visited.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿青岛啤酒节素材改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a busy festival area I visited during a family trip to Qingdao. We went there after enjoying a seafood dinner and expected a lively evening.

There were people everywhere, moving between food stalls and entertainment areas. Groups were talking, taking photographs and looking for places to sit. The lights and music made the whole place feel festive, but walking through it required patience.

At one point, we had to stop because the people ahead of us were moving very slowly. We decided to stay together and choose a meeting point in case someone became separated. Once we found a less crowded corner, we could relax and enjoy the atmosphere.

I liked the energy of the place, although I would not want that environment every day. It was enjoyable because we were on holiday and had no urgent schedule. The experience reminded me that a crowd can feel exciting or stressful depending on why you are there.

I would visit again for a special occasion, but I would choose a quieter place for a private conversation.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：城市与拥挤；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P030-Q1 Why do people move to large cities?

**观点1：** Large cities often offer more professional opportunities. **解释1：** Different organisations create a wider range of possible roles. **例子1：** For example, a data specialist may find more relevant teams there.

**观点2：** They also provide varied services and activities. **解释2：** Residents can explore interests beyond work. **例子2：** For instance, someone may join a sports club or attend cultural events.

#### P030-Q2 What difficulties do city residents face?

**观点1：** High costs can create pressure. **解释1：** Housing and daily expenses may absorb a large part of income. **例子1：** For example, a young worker may need to share accommodation.

**观点2：** Crowded transport can also be tiring. **解释2：** A short distance may still take considerable time. **例子2：** For instance, rush-hour traffic can make an ordinary commute stressful.

#### P030-Q3 Why do tourists return to the same city?

**观点1：** Familiarity can make a trip more relaxed. **解释1：** Visitors already know some places they enjoy. **例子1：** For example, a family may return to a favourite seafood market.

**观点2：** A second visit also allows deeper exploration. **解释2：** The first trip may have covered only major attractions. **例子2：** For instance, returning visitors can spend time in quieter neighbourhoods.

#### P030-Q4 How can cities become more pleasant to live in?

**观点1：** Convenient transport can reduce everyday stress. **解释1：** Residents need practical alternatives to crowded roads. **例子1：** For example, a reliable connection can make commuting more predictable.

**观点2：** Accessible public spaces are also important. **解释2：** People need places to rest and meet outside home. **例子2：** For instance, a nearby park gives workers a simple evening break.

#### P030-Q5 Should tourists visit famous attractions or ordinary neighbourhoods?

**观点1：** Famous attractions can provide an introduction to a city. **解释1：** They often reveal distinctive scenery or history. **例子1：** For example, a waterfront may show important parts of the skyline.

**观点2：** Ordinary neighbourhoods offer a different perspective. **解释2：** Visitors can see how local people use the city. **例子2：** For instance, a market visit can reveal everyday food and conversation.

#### P030-Q6 Are crowded places always unpleasant?

**观点1：** No, crowds can create a lively atmosphere. **解释1：** Shared excitement can make an event feel special. **例子1：** For example, spectators cheering together can enrich a festival or match.

**观点2：** However, crowding becomes unpleasant when it prevents basic comfort. **解释2：** People need space, clear routes and reasonable access. **例子2：** For instance, a packed station feels stressful when someone must catch a train.


## P031 钦佩的运动员

截图：第6张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a successful sportsperson you admire

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P07原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to talk about a frisbee athlete named Luwen, whom I really admire.

It dates back to about three years ago when I was first introduced to him after I joined my university frisbee team. Luwen is my coach. He is in his thirties, very tall and quite strong. He has an oval face, which makes him look charming and distinctive.

He has won countless city championships and was once ranked number one in the whole city. His skills and determination truly set him apart. There are several reasons why I admire him. First of all, he is a natural unifier of the team. Whenever anyone runs into difficulties, he is always the first to check in and see how he can help.

In addition, I remember watching a crucial match where his team was losing. His calmness and confidence were inspiring. He managed to turn the whole situation around and finally made it. That is why I really look up to him, not just as an athlete, but also as a person who motivates others, especially under pressure.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：运动与比赛；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P031-Q1 Why do people enjoy watching live sport?

**观点1：** Live sport creates a strong shared atmosphere. **解释1：** The audience reacts together to uncertain events. **例子1：** For example, a late comeback can make a crowd feel involved.

**观点2：** It also allows close observation of skill. **解释2：** Viewers can appreciate movement and cooperation directly. **例子2：** For instance, watching a team organise itself can reveal tactics missed in a short highlight.

#### P031-Q2 What can children learn from team sports?

**观点1：** They can learn cooperation. **解释1：** Success depends on considering other people's positions and needs. **例子1：** For example, passing at the right time may help more than showing individual skill.

**观点2：** They can also practise handling setbacks. **解释2：** Games provide manageable experiences of disappointment. **例子2：** For instance, losing a match can lead to discussion about what to improve.

#### P031-Q3 Is winning the most important part of sport?

**观点1：** Winning can motivate people to practise seriously. **解释1：** A clear objective gives effort direction. **例子1：** For example, a team may work on weaknesses before a competition.

**观点2：** However, participation also has social and personal value. **解释2：** Enjoyment and development matter beyond the final score. **例子2：** For instance, beginners may gain confidence even when their team loses.

#### P031-Q4 What makes a good team captain?

**观点1：** A good captain communicates clearly. **解释1：** Players need a shared understanding under pressure. **例子1：** For example, a simple instruction can reduce confusion during a close game.

**观点2：** The captain should also support morale. **解释2：** Frustration spreads when nobody responds calmly. **例子2：** For instance, encouraging a teammate after a mistake can help the whole group refocus.

#### P031-Q5 Should schools provide more sporting opportunities?

**观点1：** Yes, varied opportunities help students find an activity they enjoy. **解释1：** Not every child likes the same sport. **例子1：** For example, offering several team and individual options broadens participation.

**观点2：** Schools should also make access inclusive. **解释2：** Equipment or ability should not automatically exclude beginners. **例子2：** For instance, introductory sessions can focus on learning rather than selection for competition.

#### P031-Q6 Do professional athletes influence ordinary people?

**观点1：** They can encourage interest in physical activity. **解释1：** Visible achievements make a sport attractive. **例子1：** For example, a memorable performance may inspire someone to join a club.

**观点2：** They can also shape attitudes towards competition. **解释2：** Their behaviour communicates what success involves. **例子2：** For instance, respectful conduct after a loss can model patience and fairness.


## P032 浪费时间的活动

截图：第6张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a regular activity that wastes your time.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿社交媒体、紧张工作素材改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The activity I want to describe is scrolling through short videos on my phone. I usually start doing it after work, telling myself that I only need a quick break.

The problem is that there is always another clip waiting. Even when a video is not especially interesting, I keep moving to the next one. Before I notice, much more time has passed than I intended.

I consider it wasteful because I often remember very little afterwards. It does not leave me as refreshed as reading, walking or having a proper conversation. It can also push my English practice later into the evening, when I have less energy.

I do not think all online videos are useless. Some are educational or genuinely entertaining. My problem is watching without a clear purpose or stopping point. I am trying to put my phone aside after a short break and choose an activity that I will feel better about when it is finished.

I want my free time to feel like a real choice, rather than something that disappears before I have noticed.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：科技与效率；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P032-Q1 How does technology save time?

**观点1：** Technology can handle repetitive steps efficiently. **解释1：** People can spend less time repeating routine actions. **例子1：** For example, software can help organise a large collection of data.

**观点2：** It also reduces the need to travel for simple tasks. **解释2：** Information and communication are available remotely. **例子2：** For instance, a quick online meeting can replace a journey for a short discussion.

#### P032-Q2 Can people become too dependent on technology?

**观点1：** Yes, dependence becomes a problem when basic alternatives disappear. **解释1：** A small failure can interrupt an entire routine. **例子1：** For example, a lost file may stop work without a backup.

**观点2：** However, regular use is not automatically harmful. **解释2：** Useful tools can support rather than replace understanding. **例子2：** For instance, checking a generated draft keeps the user involved in the result.

#### P032-Q3 How should people respond to a technical problem?

**观点1：** They should identify the problem calmly. **解释1：** Random actions can make it harder to understand what happened. **例子1：** For example, checking a saved version may help after a programme freezes.

**观点2：** They should also learn from the incident. **解释2：** A preventive habit can reduce future disruption. **例子2：** For instance, keeping backups makes an unexpected failure less stressful.

#### P032-Q4 Should children learn basic computer skills?

**观点1：** Yes, practical skills support study and everyday tasks. **解释1：** Children need more than familiarity with entertainment apps. **例子1：** For example, organising files helps them manage school projects.

**观点2：** They should also learn to evaluate information. **解释2：** Confidence with a device does not guarantee sound judgement. **例子2：** For instance, a pupil can compare sources before accepting an online claim.

#### P032-Q5 Why do people buy new electronic products?

**观点1：** Some purchases meet a practical need. **解释1：** An existing device may no longer perform required tasks well. **例子1：** For example, a worker may need a more suitable computer.

**观点2：** Other purchases reflect curiosity or social influence. **解释2：** Novelty can feel exciting even without a clear purpose. **例子2：** For instance, an impressive demonstration may tempt someone to exceed a budget.

#### P032-Q6 Will technology replace human judgement?

**观点1：** It may assist with routine decisions. **解释1：** Clear patterns and repeated tasks can be handled consistently. **例子1：** For example, software can flag unusual entries for review.

**观点2：** However, people still need to interpret context and responsibility. **解释2：** A technically plausible suggestion may not suit the real situation. **例子2：** For instance, a worker should check whether a generated solution addresses the actual problem.


## P033 尊敬的比你年长的人

截图：第6张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an older person you respect.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿Part1邻居Mrs Chen改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to talk about my neighbour, Mrs Chen, who is a retired teacher. I got to know her through everyday conversations in our building, and I have come to respect her greatly.

She is friendly and interested in what other people are doing. When I mention a difficulty, she listens before offering an opinion. I appreciate that she does not assume her experience gives her an immediate answer to every situation.

Once, I told her that I felt discouraged about my English progress. She reminded me that learning takes time and suggested noticing small improvements rather than comparing myself constantly with others. Her advice was simple, but it made me feel more capable.

We sometimes talk about books, local places and the differences between our daily routines. I respect her because she combines experience with an open mind. Spending time with her has shown me that a friendship between different generations can be comfortable, useful and enjoyable for both people.

I hope to keep learning from her while also sharing things that she finds interesting about my generation.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：代际沟通；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P033-Q1 What can younger people learn from older people?

**观点1：** They can learn from accumulated experience. **解释1：** Older people may recognise patterns in familiar difficulties. **例子1：** For example, a retired teacher can suggest a realistic approach to learning.

**观点2：** They can also learn about earlier ways of living. **解释2：** Personal memories make social change concrete. **例子2：** For instance, a neighbour can describe how a local street developed.

#### P033-Q2 What can older people learn from younger people?

**观点1：** Younger people may introduce unfamiliar tools. **解释1：** Practical demonstrations can make technology less intimidating. **例子1：** For example, a younger neighbour can explain how to organise digital photographs.

**观点2：** They can also share different perspectives. **解释2：** New experiences may challenge established assumptions. **例子2：** For instance, discussing modern working routines can help both generations understand each other better.

#### P033-Q3 Why do misunderstandings occur between generations?

**观点1：** Daily experiences can be very different. **解释1：** People may assume that others face the same choices they once did. **例子1：** For example, older career advice may overlook a changed working environment.

**观点2：** Communication habits can also differ. **解释2：** One group may prefer a call while another sends short messages. **例子2：** For instance, a brief reply may be mistaken for a lack of interest.

#### P033-Q4 How can different generations communicate better?

**观点1：** They should ask rather than assume. **解释1：** Questions reveal the reasoning behind unfamiliar choices. **例子1：** For example, a young worker can explain why flexible hours matter.

**观点2：** Shared activities also help. **解释2：** Conversation becomes easier when people have something practical to do together. **例子2：** For instance, looking at family photographs can create a comfortable exchange of stories.

#### P033-Q5 Should older people stay active after retirement?

**观点1：** Yes, enjoyable activities can provide routine and connection. **解释1：** Retirement does not remove curiosity or social needs. **例子1：** For example, gardening can offer a satisfying daily interest.

**观点2：** However, activity should reflect personal choice and circumstances. **解释2：** People should not feel pressured to remain constantly busy. **例子2：** For instance, reading quietly can be as meaningful as joining a group.

#### P033-Q6 Is respect mainly about age?

**观点1：** Age deserves consideration because people have different experiences and needs. **解释1：** A patient attitude supports comfortable interaction. **例子1：** For example, allowing time for an explanation can show care.

**观点2：** However, respect should also be mutual. **解释2：** Younger people deserve to have their opinions heard. **例子2：** For instance, an older mentor can offer guidance while recognising the learner's own goals.


## P034 不喜欢的短途旅行

截图：第6张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a short journey you did not enjoy.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿交通拥堵素材改编；短途情节补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a short car journey across the city that turned out to be frustrating. I was going with a friend to meet several people for dinner at the weekend.

The destination was not particularly far away, so we expected the trip to be straightforward. Unfortunately, we left during a busy period and soon became stuck in slow traffic. The car moved forward a little and then stopped again.

At first, we chatted and listened to music. However, as the delay continued, I became worried about keeping everyone waiting. I sent a message explaining the situation, but I still felt uncomfortable because we had not allowed enough time.

We eventually arrived late and slightly tired before the evening had properly started. I disliked the journey because it felt avoidable. Since then, I have tried to check transport options more carefully and consider the subway when roads are likely to be crowded. A short distance does not always mean a quick trip.

I now allow extra time whenever being late could affect other people or spoil shared plans.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：旅行与交通；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P034-Q1 Why do traffic jams happen?

**观点1：** Many people travel at similar times. **解释1：** Road space becomes insufficient when demand rises together. **例子1：** For example, commuting hours can bring large numbers of cars into one area.

**观点2：** Limited alternatives can make the problem worse. **解释2：** People may drive because other options are inconvenient. **例子2：** For instance, a poor connection can make public transport difficult to use.

#### P034-Q2 How can cities reduce traffic congestion?

**观点1：** They can improve practical public transport options. **解释1：** People are more willing to change when alternatives are reliable. **例子1：** For example, frequent connections make commuting easier to plan.

**观点2：** They can also reduce the need for identical travel times. **解释2：** Flexible routines can spread demand. **例子2：** For instance, some workplaces may allow different starting hours where the job permits.

#### P034-Q3 Why do people enjoy travelling by car?

**观点1：** Cars offer flexibility about routes and stops. **解释1：** Travellers can adapt the journey to their needs. **例子1：** For example, a family can pause when someone needs a break.

**观点2：** They also make luggage easier to manage. **解释2：** People can carry items for different activities. **例子2：** For instance, a seaside trip may involve food, sports equipment and several bags.

#### P034-Q4 What disadvantages do car journeys have?

**观点1：** Traffic can make arrival times unpredictable. **解释1：** A short route may still involve a long delay. **例子1：** For example, travelling across a city at dinner time can be frustrating.

**观点2：** Driving also requires continuous attention. **解释2：** The driver cannot relax in the same way as passengers. **例子2：** For instance, a long trip needs sensible breaks and a realistic schedule.

#### P034-Q5 Should people plan journeys carefully?

**观点1：** Yes, basic planning prevents avoidable difficulties. **解释1：** Travellers need to understand routes and timing. **例子1：** For example, checking transport options can reduce the risk of arriving late.

**观点2：** However, plans should leave flexibility. **解释2：** Unexpected changes are difficult to predict fully. **例子2：** For instance, allowing spare time makes a delayed connection less stressful.

#### P034-Q6 Are short trips as valuable as long holidays?

**观点1：** They can be valuable because they fit ordinary routines. **解释1：** People may not have time for an extended break. **例子1：** For example, a nearby park outing can provide a refreshing afternoon.

**观点2：** Longer holidays offer different opportunities. **解释2：** Travellers can explore a place more deeply. **例子2：** For instance, several days in a city allow both major sights and relaxed local experiences.


## P035 嘈杂地

截图：第6张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a noisy place you have visited.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P29原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a noisy livehouse in the city centre. I visited it with a friend a few months ago, hoping to enjoy a relaxing evening after work.

I went with a close friend of mine who is a huge fan of indie music. He insisted that this new band was the next big thing, so I tagged along. Mainly, I wanted to blow off some steam. My job as a data scientist can be mentally draining, so I was looking forward to some loud and energetic rock music.

Here is the thing: I expected a high-energy rock show, but it turned out to be underwhelming. The lead singer had almost zero stage presence. He just stood there, stared at his shoes and mumbled the lyrics. The amplified music was loud and repetitive. We had to lean towards each other to hear even a simple comment.

It felt like the band did not even want to be there. I kept waiting for the climax of the show, but it never came. The volume made a disappointing evening worse.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：噪声与公共空间；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P035-Q1 Why are cities often noisy?

**观点1：** Many activities take place close together. **解释1：** Transport, businesses and crowds produce overlapping sounds. **例子1：** For example, a busy road beside restaurants can remain loud into the evening.

**观点2：** Building design also matters. **解释2：** Sound can spread when spaces lack suitable separation. **例子2：** For instance, a poorly insulated apartment may expose residents to neighbours' everyday activities.

#### P035-Q2 How can people be considerate about noise?

**观点1：** They can adjust behaviour to the setting. **解释1：** A volume suitable for a celebration may disturb a shared quiet space. **例子1：** For example, someone can lower their voice on public transport.

**观点2：** They can also communicate expectations beforehand. **解释2：** Clear arrangements reduce conflict. **例子2：** For instance, an event organiser can explain when music will end to nearby residents.

#### P035-Q3 Should children always be quiet in public?

**观点1：** No, children need opportunities to play and communicate. **解释1：** Some noise is normal in places designed for activity. **例子1：** For example, laughter is expected in a playground.

**观点2：** However, they can learn to recognise different settings. **解释2：** Consideration means adapting rather than remaining silent everywhere. **例子2：** For instance, a library requires a quieter voice than a park.

#### P035-Q4 Why do some people enjoy loud music?

**观点1：** Loud music can create excitement. **解释1：** People may want an atmosphere that feels different from ordinary life. **例子1：** For example, a lively concert can help friends enjoy a night out.

**观点2：** Shared intensity can also create belonging. **解释2：** The audience experiences the same rhythm together. **例子2：** For instance, singing along at a performance can feel more involving than listening alone.

#### P035-Q5 Can noise affect how people use public places?

**观点1：** Yes, it can change whether people feel comfortable. **解释1：** A place may be unsuitable for concentration or private discussion. **例子1：** For example, a noisy cafe can make a serious conversation difficult.

**观点2：** It can also signal activity and energy. **解释2：** Some visitors deliberately seek a lively atmosphere. **例子2：** For instance, a festival crowd may be part of the attraction.

#### P035-Q6 How can venues manage sound better?

**观点1：** They can match sound levels to the activity. **解释1：** More volume does not always improve enjoyment. **例子1：** For example, clear music can be preferable to an overwhelmingly loud performance.

**观点2：** They can also provide quieter areas. **解释2：** Visitors have different needs during the same event. **例子2：** For instance, a seating area away from speakers allows people to talk comfortably.


## P036 近期改变

截图：第6张，显示共6个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a recent change in your routine.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P44原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

A recent change in my life is practising a short meditation in the morning. I began after noticing how calmly my friend Alex handled a demanding study schedule.

At that time, frequent phone notifications distracted me, and I often struggled with sleep. However, my friend Alex always remained calm and centered. He told me his secret was meditating for fifteen minutes every morning to recharge.

I noticed that this habit made him more focused, peaceful and energetic. Inspired by him, I decided to start this habit myself. At first, it was a bit boring, but gradually I found it useful.

I want to keep this habit because it can help me calm my mind, regain inner peace and handle a tight work schedule in a healthier way.

I started with only a few minutes because I did not want the habit to become another source of pressure. Some mornings were easier than others, but I kept returning to the routine. Gradually, having a quiet pause before checking messages began to feel more natural, and I wanted to continue with it.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：生活习惯与医疗职业；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P036-Q1 How can people build healthier daily routines?

**观点1：** They can begin with manageable changes. **解释1：** Small actions are easier to repeat during a busy week. **例子1：** For example, a short walk after lunch is a realistic starting point.

**观点2：** Supportive surroundings also help. **解释2：** A convenient option requires less willpower. **例子2：** For instance, keeping a regular break in the schedule makes it easier to leave the desk.

#### P036-Q2 Why do some people struggle to exercise regularly?

**观点1：** Time pressure can make exercise difficult. **解释1：** Work and family responsibilities may use most of the day. **例子1：** For example, a long commute leaves little time for a sports session.

**观点2：** Some people also choose activities they dislike. **解释2：** An unpleasant routine is harder to maintain. **例子2：** For instance, someone who dislikes running may prefer a relaxed team game.

#### P036-Q3 Can friends encourage better habits?

**观点1：** Yes, friends can make a new activity more enjoyable. **解释1：** Shared company reduces the feeling of doing another task. **例子1：** For example, two friends can take a regular evening walk.

**观点2：** They can also provide gentle accountability. **解释2：** Knowing someone is expecting you can support consistency. **例子2：** For instance, a training partner may help someone keep an agreed practice time.

#### P036-Q4 What attracts people to healthcare careers?

**观点1：** Some people want work that helps others directly. **解释1：** Seeing a practical benefit can make effort feel meaningful. **例子1：** For example, a nurse may value reassuring an anxious family.

**观点2：** Others are interested in science and problem solving. **解释2：** They enjoy applying knowledge to complex situations. **例子2：** For instance, a student may like both biology and careful practical work.

#### P036-Q5 What qualities are useful in healthcare work?

**观点1：** Careful attention is essential. **解释1：** Small details can matter when people depend on accurate information. **例子1：** For example, a worker needs to communicate instructions clearly.

**观点2：** Empathy is also valuable. **解释2：** People may feel worried or confused in unfamiliar situations. **例子2：** For instance, a patient explanation can make a family feel more comfortable asking questions.

#### P036-Q6 Should workplaces encourage staff to take breaks?

**观点1：** Yes, reasonable breaks can support concentration. **解释1：** Continuous demanding work can leave people mentally tired. **例子1：** For example, a brief pause may help someone return to a complicated report.

**观点2：** Breaks also show respect for employees' needs. **解释2：** A sustainable routine is easier to maintain. **例子2：** For instance, predictable lunch time lets staff organise meals and short walks.


## P037 改变重要想法

截图：第6张，显示共5个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a time you changed an important opinion.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P20原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I used to believe that a useful study session had to be long. My opinion changed after reading Atomic Habits and trying a smaller daily reading goal.

I first came across this book when I wanted to build a better reading habit. At that time, my original plan was to read for one hour every night before bed, but I soon realized this goal was not easy enough. This book taught me that a good habit should start from something and manageable.

What I found useful was the idea of splitting a tough goal into tiny steps. After reading it, I changed my plan and started with only a few pages a day. This made reading less stressful and much easier to stick to.

I felt it was practical and inspiring. It helped me understand that real progress does not always come from huge changes, but from small actions repeated every day.

Now I judge a routine by whether I can maintain it and learn something useful, rather than by how impressive the original target sounds when I describe it.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：决定与改变；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P037-Q1 Should important decisions be made quickly?

**观点1：** Usually, important decisions deserve time for relevant information. **解释1：** Rushing can hide costs or practical difficulties. **例子1：** For example, choosing a course requires checking its schedule and content.

**观点2：** However, endless delay can also be unhelpful. **解释2：** Some uncertainty remains even after careful thought. **例子2：** For instance, a suitable opportunity may disappear while someone keeps comparing similar options.

#### P037-Q2 Who should help young people make decisions?

**观点1：** Parents can provide experience and support. **解释1：** They may notice consequences that young people overlook. **例子1：** For example, they can help compare the practical costs of study options.

**观点2：** Young people should still have a voice. **解释2：** They are more committed when they understand and accept the choice. **例子2：** For instance, selecting a hobby should reflect the child's actual interests.

#### P037-Q3 Why do people change their decisions?

**观点1：** New information can alter what seems sensible. **解释1：** The original plan may no longer fit the situation. **例子1：** For example, bad weather can make an outdoor event unsuitable.

**观点2：** Experience also reveals unrealistic expectations. **解释2：** Trying something shows limits that planning missed. **例子2：** For instance, a long study session may prove impossible after a full working day.

#### P037-Q4 Is advice from friends useful for decisions?

**观点1：** Yes, friends can offer a different perspective. **解释1：** They may recognise habits or strengths that someone overlooks. **例子1：** For example, a friend can suggest a more realistic daily routine.

**观点2：** However, advice should be evaluated personally. **解释2：** Friends do not share every responsibility or preference. **例子2：** For instance, a career choice must fit the decision-maker's own circumstances.

#### P037-Q5 What should a group do when members disagree?

**观点1：** The group should clarify its shared purpose. **解释1：** People may agree on the goal while preferring different methods. **例子1：** For example, friends may all want time together despite disagreeing about the venue.

**观点2：** It should then compare practical options fairly. **解释2：** Listening prevents the loudest person from deciding everything. **例子2：** For instance, budget and travel time can guide a compromise.

#### P037-Q6 Does a good result mean a decision was good?

**观点1：** A good result can suggest the choice worked well. **解释1：** It may show that planning matched the circumstances. **例子1：** For example, a manageable study routine can lead to consistent practice.

**观点2：** However, luck also affects outcomes. **解释2：** The decision process matters as well as the result. **例子2：** For instance, a risky choice may succeed once without being sensible to repeat.


## P038 保护环境的法律

截图：第6张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an environmental law or rule you know about.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 规则或法律针对什么问题；你如何了解或想到它；具体怎样实施；你认为它有什么作用。区分现行规则和自己的政策设想。

**答案来源：** 上海垃圾分类官方资料核实；个人经历为素材改编。
事实依据：[上海现行生活垃圾分类指引](https://english.shanghai.gov.cn/en-WasteDisposal/20231212/15dc5685586b44b3ad419639b102687c.html)、[上海生活垃圾管理条例](https://english.shanghai.gov.cn/en-LocalRules/20240904/3169307f43954478bbbb90bd9a83e434.html)。这里只概述分类，不添加罚款数额等未经本次核实的细节。


### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe Shanghai's household waste sorting rules. I became familiar with them through everyday life in the city, especially the different containers and instructions in residential areas.

The system separates household waste into different categories, including recyclable materials, hazardous waste, food waste and residual waste. The basic idea is to keep materials apart so that they can be handled more appropriately.

At first, I sometimes felt unsure about where an item belonged. Clear examples on signs were useful because they turned a general instruction into a practical action. I also became more aware of how much packaging I brought home after shopping.

I think these rules are valuable because environmental protection requires regular habits as well as public discussion. However, people need convenient facilities and understandable guidance if they are expected to follow them consistently. For me, the most noticeable effect has been paying more attention to ordinary objects that I previously would have thrown into the same bin.

I appreciate rules more when I can understand their purpose and see how to follow them in practice.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：环保与规则；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P038-Q1 Should environmental protection depend on individuals or governments?

**观点1：** Individuals influence everyday behaviour. **解释1：** Repeated small actions can reduce unnecessary waste. **例子1：** For example, visitors can take their rubbish away after a picnic.

**观点2：** Governments and organisers shape the available choices. **解释2：** People need systems that make responsible action practical. **例子2：** For instance, clear collection points support proper waste sorting.

#### P038-Q2 What makes an environmental rule effective?

**观点1：** The rule should be easy to understand. **解释1：** People need to know exactly what action is expected. **例子1：** For example, a sign can show which items belong in each bin.

**观点2：** It also needs practical support. **解释2：** A demand is difficult to follow without suitable facilities. **例子2：** For instance, reusable cups require an accessible return process.

#### P038-Q3 Is education more useful than penalties for environmental protection?
