**观点1：** Education helps people understand the purpose of a rule. **解释1：** Understanding can support long-term cooperation. **例子1：** For example, showing how litter affects a park can encourage considerate behaviour.

**观点2：** However, consequences may still be needed for repeated disregard. **解释2：** Otherwise responsible users may feel unfairly burdened. **例子2：** For instance, organisers should remain accountable for cleaning up after an event.

#### P038-Q4 How can businesses reduce unnecessary waste?

**观点1：** They can reconsider packaging and routine materials. **解释1：** Small changes repeated across many sales can matter. **例子1：** For example, a shop can avoid adding an unwanted disposable bag.

**观点2：** They can also make repair or reuse practical. **解释2：** Products need not be discarded after a minor problem. **例子2：** For instance, a store can help customers replace a damaged part.

#### P038-Q5 Should economic development take priority over nature?

**观点1：** Development matters because people need work and services. **解释1：** Ignoring livelihoods can make environmental proposals difficult to support. **例子1：** For example, a community may depend on visitor income.

**观点2：** However, damaged surroundings can weaken long-term wellbeing. **解释2：** Growth should account for the resources it uses. **例子2：** For instance, tourism loses value if the attraction becomes polluted or unpleasant.

#### P038-Q6 How can schools encourage environmental responsibility?

**观点1：** They can connect lessons with practical routines. **解释1：** Children understand responsibility better through repeated actions. **例子1：** For example, sorting classroom waste makes the idea concrete.

**观点2：** They can also organise thoughtful outdoor activities. **解释2：** Direct experience creates a stronger connection with nature. **例子2：** For instance, a supervised park visit can include observing wildlife and discussing litter.


## P039 长久目标/抱负

截图：第6张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a long-term goal you hope to achieve.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 长期目标是什么；何时产生；准备采取哪些步骤；实现它对你有什么意义。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P15 AI职业与Part1英语目标改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

My long-term goal is to become more skilled in artificial intelligence and contribute to useful projects in that field. I already work with data, so this ambition builds on experience I have rather than starting from nothing.

I became interested through technical articles and the problems I encountered at work. What attracts me is the chance to turn complex ideas into tools that help people solve practical tasks. I would also enjoy learning from experienced researchers and colleagues.

To move towards this goal, I need to keep improving my mathematics, coding and understanding of machine learning. I also want stronger English so that I can learn from more resources and explain ideas clearly.

I know this will take time, so I am trying to focus on regular progress instead of expecting a sudden transformation. Completing a small project or understanding a difficult concept can be a useful step. Achieving this goal would give me both professional satisfaction and more choices for the future.

I would rather build a solid foundation gradually than chase an impressive title before I am ready.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：工作与目标；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P039-Q1 What makes a job attractive?

**观点1：** Interesting work can provide a sense of progress. **解释1：** People enjoy using and developing skills they value. **例子1：** For example, a data scientist may appreciate solving an unfamiliar problem.

**观点2：** Working conditions are equally important. **解释2：** A meaningful role still needs a sustainable routine. **例子2：** For instance, predictable hours can help someone maintain family relationships and hobbies.

#### P039-Q2 Is salary more important than job satisfaction?

**观点1：** Salary matters because people must meet practical needs. **解释1：** Financial pressure can make even interesting work difficult. **例子1：** For example, a worker needs to cover housing and normal expenses.

**观点2：** However, satisfaction affects daily motivation. **解释2：** A role that constantly feels pointless can become exhausting. **例子2：** For instance, useful projects may matter more than a small increase in pay.

#### P039-Q3 Why do people want to work abroad?

**观点1：** They may want to learn through a different environment. **解释1：** New colleagues and routines can challenge familiar assumptions. **例子1：** For example, a temporary research role may introduce another approach to teamwork.

**观点2：** They may also seek personal independence. **解释2：** Living elsewhere involves everyday decisions outside a familiar setting. **例子2：** For instance, arranging transport and accommodation can build confidence.

#### P039-Q4 Is temporary overseas work useful?

**观点1：** Yes, it allows people to explore a working environment. **解释1：** They can learn before considering a permanent move. **例子1：** For example, a short project may clarify which team culture suits them.

**观点2：** It can also create transferable experience. **解释2：** The value may remain after returning home. **例子2：** For instance, clearer communication habits can improve cooperation in later jobs.

#### P039-Q5 What skills will help people adapt at work?

**观点1：** A willingness to learn is essential. **解释1：** Tools and responsibilities can change during a career. **例子1：** For example, a worker may need to understand unfamiliar software.

**观点2：** Clear communication is also useful. **解释2：** Adaptation often depends on asking questions and coordinating with others. **例子2：** For instance, explaining uncertainty early can prevent a project from moving in the wrong direction.

#### P039-Q6 Should employers offer flexible working arrangements?

**观点1：** Where practical, flexibility can help people manage responsibilities. **解释1：** Different tasks and personal situations require different routines. **例子1：** For example, focused writing may suit a quiet home setting.

**观点2：** However, teams still need reliable coordination. **解释2：** Flexibility should not leave colleagues unsure about availability. **例子2：** For instance, agreed meeting times can preserve cooperation while allowing varied working hours.


## P040 很久没收到回复的信息

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a message for which you waited a long time for a reply.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿Luwen与团队素材改编；消息情节补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a message I sent to Luwen about a weekend training plan. Several friends wanted to play frisbee, and I needed to know whether he could join us.

I sent the message in the afternoon and expected a fairly quick reply. However, the evening passed without an answer. I checked my phone several times because the others were waiting for me to confirm the time and place.

At first, I felt slightly annoyed. Then I remembered that he had been busy preparing for an exam. Rather than sending repeated messages, I explained the deadline for our decision and made a simple backup plan with the group.

He replied the next morning and apologised for missing the message. He had put his phone away while studying. I understood his reason and felt relieved that nothing was wrong. The experience taught me to state clearly when an answer is needed instead of assuming that everyone checks messages as often as I do.

I also learned to leave some flexibility when a group plan depends on another person's availability.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：倾听与消息；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P040-Q1 What shows that someone is listening carefully?

**观点1：** Relevant questions show attention. **解释1：** The listener responds to what was actually said. **例子1：** For example, asking about one unclear step shows an effort to understand.

**观点2：** Appropriate body language also helps. **解释2：** People feel heard when attention is visibly directed towards them. **例子2：** For instance, putting a phone away can make a conversation more comfortable.

#### P040-Q2 Should people listen to topics they do not enjoy?

**观点1：** Sometimes, because listening shows consideration. **解释1：** The topic may matter to the person speaking. **例子1：** For example, a friend may want to share progress on a difficult exam.

**观点2：** However, people can set polite limits. **解释2：** Attention becomes less useful when someone is exhausted or rushed. **例子2：** For instance, they can suggest continuing the conversation another time.

#### P040-Q3 Why do people sometimes reply slowly to messages?

**观点1：** They may be concentrating on another responsibility. **解释1：** A delayed reply does not necessarily indicate a lack of care. **例子1：** For example, a student may put the phone away while revising.

**观点2：** The message may also be unclear about urgency. **解释2：** People cannot always guess when an answer is needed. **例子2：** For instance, a group organiser should state a decision deadline.

#### P040-Q4 Is face-to-face conversation better than messaging?

**观点1：** Face-to-face conversation provides more emotional information. **解释1：** Expressions and tone help people interpret meaning. **例子1：** For example, a sensitive disagreement may be easier to resolve in person.

**观点2：** Messaging offers flexibility. **解释2：** People can communicate when their schedules do not match. **例子2：** For instance, friends in different cities can maintain regular contact through short messages.

#### P040-Q5 How can misunderstandings be reduced?

**观点1：** People can check what they have understood. **解释1：** Repeating the main point reveals differences early. **例子1：** For example, teammates can confirm the agreed meeting time.

**观点2：** Clear language also reduces confusion. **解释2：** Unnecessary detail may hide the main request. **例子2：** For instance, a work message can state the task and deadline before explaining the background.

#### P040-Q6 Do people need to respond to every message immediately?

**观点1：** No, immediate replies can interrupt important work. **解释1：** People need time when they can concentrate. **例子1：** For example, constant notifications can make a complex report take longer.

**观点2：** However, expectations should be communicated. **解释2：** Others need to know how urgent matters will be handled. **例子2：** For instance, a team can agree to call when a quick answer is essential.


## P041 包含动物的故事或书

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[动物故事话题核对](https://ieltspeaking.com/topics/part2/describe-a-story-book-with-animals-in-it.html)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a story featuring animals.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体对象；怎样接触或获得；外观、内容或用途；它为什么对你重要。

**答案来源：** 新编示范：公版龟兔赛跑故事；不借用网页范文。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The animal story I would like to describe is The Tortoise and the Hare. I first heard it when I was a child, and I still remember its simple but useful message.

The story is about a fast hare and a slow tortoise who agree to race. The hare is so confident of winning that he stops to rest along the way. Meanwhile, the tortoise continues moving at a steady pace. In the end, the tortoise reaches the finish before the hare.

As a child, I found the result surprising because the faster animal seemed certain to win. Later, I began to appreciate the contrast between natural ability and consistent effort. It reminded me that being confident is different from taking a task seriously.

I like the story because its message is easy to apply to learning and work. When I practise English, a short session every day is often more useful than one enthusiastic session followed by a week of doing nothing.

That familiar story still gives me a useful reminder whenever I become impatient with my own progress.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：动物与故事；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P041-Q1 Why are animals common in children's stories?

**观点1：** Animal characters can make a story memorable. **解释1：** Distinctive creatures provide clear contrasts. **例子1：** For example, a slow tortoise and fast hare create an understandable race.

**观点2：** They also offer a simple way to explore behaviour. **解释2：** Children can discuss choices without focusing on a real classmate. **例子2：** For instance, an overconfident character can illustrate why steady effort matters.

#### P041-Q2 What can children learn from caring for pets?

**观点1：** They can learn responsibility through regular tasks. **解释1：** An animal depends on care even when the owner is busy. **例子1：** For example, preparing food becomes part of a daily routine.

**观点2：** They can also practise noticing another creature's needs. **解释2：** Care requires attention beyond personal wishes. **例子2：** For instance, a child can learn when a pet needs quiet rather than play.

#### P041-Q3 Why do people regard pets as family members?

**观点1：** Pets become part of everyday companionship. **解释1：** Repeated contact creates a strong emotional connection. **例子1：** For example, a cat waiting at home can make an evening feel warmer.

**观点2：** Caring for them also involves commitment. **解释2：** Owners organise time and resources around their needs. **例子2：** For instance, holiday plans may include arranging someone reliable to provide care.

#### P041-Q4 Is keeping a pet in a city suitable?

**观点1：** It can be suitable when the animal's needs are met. **解释1：** Responsible care matters more than a simple city label. **例子1：** For example, an owner can provide appropriate space and daily attention.

**观点2：** However, shared living creates additional responsibilities. **解释2：** Noise and cleanliness affect neighbours. **例子2：** For instance, someone should consider whether their routine suits the pet before adopting it.

#### P041-Q5 How can people learn more about wild animals?

**观点1：** They can use clear educational resources. **解释1：** Books and documentaries can introduce behaviour and habitats. **例子1：** For example, a programme about cranes can explain wetland life.

**观点2：** Responsible visits can also encourage curiosity. **解释2：** Seeing a natural setting makes the topic more concrete. **例子2：** For instance, observing birds from an appropriate distance can deepen interest without disturbing them.

#### P041-Q6 Should schools teach children to protect animals?

**观点1：** Yes, it can develop consideration for living things. **解释1：** Children learn that their actions affect other creatures. **例子1：** For example, leaving rubbish near water can damage an animal's surroundings.

**观点2：** It also supports understanding of nature. **解释2：** Animals are connected with the places where they live. **例子2：** For instance, learning about wetland birds can lead to discussion of habitat protection.


## P042 名人出演的广告

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an advertisement featuring a famous person.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体对象；怎样接触或获得；外观、内容或用途；它为什么对你重要。

**答案来源：** 新编示范：未指定真实广告；运动素材改编。
示范提醒：采访、广告或社区通知的具体情节为练习补写，未确认某次真实节目、广告或新闻事件。


### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a sports advertisement featuring a well-known athlete. I saw it online while watching videos, and its simple idea caught my attention.

Instead of showing only a victory celebration, the advertisement focused on training. The athlete repeated basic movements, made mistakes and tried again. The product appeared as part of the routine rather than being the only thing on the screen.

I found that approach more interesting than an exaggerated promise. It connected the athlete's public success with effort that ordinary people could understand. As someone who enjoys frisbee, I recognised the feeling of practising a skill repeatedly before it finally improves.

The advertisement did not make me buy anything immediately. I would still consider the price and whether I needed the product. However, I remembered the message because it matched my own experience of learning through practice. For me, a famous person is most convincing in an advertisement when there is a clear connection between their experience and the product.

I remembered the attitude towards practice more clearly than any specific claim about the product.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：广告与影响；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P042-Q1 Why do advertisers use celebrities?

**观点1：** Celebrities attract attention quickly. **解释1：** People recognise a familiar face before considering the product. **例子1：** For example, a sports fan may notice an athlete in a short advertisement.

**观点2：** They also create associations. **解释2：** The person's image can influence how the product feels. **例子2：** For instance, training scenes may connect sports equipment with effort and determination.

#### P042-Q2 Should people trust celebrity recommendations?

**观点1：** They should evaluate the product independently. **解释1：** A famous person's appearance does not prove suitability. **例子1：** For example, advertised shoes may not fit a particular buyer comfortably.

**观点2：** However, relevant experience can add useful information. **解释2：** A clear explanation matters more than fame alone. **例子2：** For instance, an athlete discussing a specific feature is more informative than simply posing.

#### P042-Q3 What makes an advertisement memorable?

**观点1：** A clear idea is often memorable. **解释1：** Viewers can recall a simple message without much effort. **例子1：** For example, a short story about practice can stay in the mind.

**观点2：** Personal relevance also matters. **解释2：** People notice connections with their interests. **例子2：** For instance, someone learning a sport may remember an advertisement about overcoming beginner mistakes.

#### P042-Q4 Can advertising encourage unnecessary spending?

**观点1：** Yes, advertising can make an ordinary product feel urgent. **解释1：** Limited offers may reduce careful comparison. **例子1：** For example, a buyer may upgrade a device that still works.

**观点2：** It can also connect purchases with social acceptance. **解释2：** People may want the lifestyle shown around the product. **例子2：** For instance, fashionable clothes may be presented as a route to popularity.

#### P042-Q5 Should advertisements aimed at children be restricted?

**观点1：** Some restrictions are reasonable because children may struggle to judge persuasion. **解释1：** They can confuse an attractive story with a reliable claim. **例子1：** For example, a toy advertisement may hide the product's limitations.

**观点2：** Parents and schools should also build understanding. **解释2：** Rules alone cannot explain every message a child encounters. **例子2：** For instance, discussing how an advertisement creates excitement can develop judgement.

#### P042-Q6 Are public-interest advertisements useful?

**观点1：** They can draw attention to overlooked issues. **解释1：** A short message may reach people outside formal lessons. **例子1：** For example, an image of litter can prompt thought about public spaces.

**观点2：** However, awareness needs a practical next step. **解释2：** A moving message is less useful without guidance. **例子2：** For instance, showing where to recycle helps viewers act on the idea.


## P043 推荐旅行过的地方

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a place you have visited and would recommend.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P50原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The place I'd recommend is Qingdao, a seaside city I visited with my family. It would especially suit someone who enjoys local food, coastal walks and a lively holiday atmosphere.

Last year, my family and I went there by car, and it took us about six hours to get there. We stayed there for several days. In summer, Qingdao is distinctive, vibrant and thriving. It has its own unique atmosphere.

What impressed me most was the seafood and the beer festival. The city is famous for its fresh and affordable seafood. We picked out crabs, prawns and abalone, and then had them cooked right away for a small processing fee. After that, we enjoyed Qingdao's world-renowned beer festival, which attracted tourists from all over the world.

I enjoyed visiting it because we were engrossed in a vivid and festive atmosphere. If I have a chance, I will definitely go there again.

I'd encourage a visitor to leave room for unexpected discoveries, because some of our best memories came from ordinary conversations and meals rather than from a fixed sightseeing list.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：城市与拥挤；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P043-Q1 Why do people move to large cities?

**观点1：** Large cities often offer more professional opportunities. **解释1：** Different organisations create a wider range of possible roles. **例子1：** For example, a data specialist may find more relevant teams there.

**观点2：** They also provide varied services and activities. **解释2：** Residents can explore interests beyond work. **例子2：** For instance, someone may join a sports club or attend cultural events.

#### P043-Q2 What difficulties do city residents face?

**观点1：** High costs can create pressure. **解释1：** Housing and daily expenses may absorb a large part of income. **例子1：** For example, a young worker may need to share accommodation.

**观点2：** Crowded transport can also be tiring. **解释2：** A short distance may still take considerable time. **例子2：** For instance, rush-hour traffic can make an ordinary commute stressful.

#### P043-Q3 Why do tourists return to the same city?

**观点1：** Familiarity can make a trip more relaxed. **解释1：** Visitors already know some places they enjoy. **例子1：** For example, a family may return to a favourite seafood market.

**观点2：** A second visit also allows deeper exploration. **解释2：** The first trip may have covered only major attractions. **例子2：** For instance, returning visitors can spend time in quieter neighbourhoods.

#### P043-Q4 How can cities become more pleasant to live in?

**观点1：** Convenient transport can reduce everyday stress. **解释1：** Residents need practical alternatives to crowded roads. **例子1：** For example, a reliable connection can make commuting more predictable.

**观点2：** Accessible public spaces are also important. **解释2：** People need places to rest and meet outside home. **例子2：** For instance, a nearby park gives workers a simple evening break.

#### P043-Q5 Should tourists visit famous attractions or ordinary neighbourhoods?

**观点1：** Famous attractions can provide an introduction to a city. **解释1：** They often reveal distinctive scenery or history. **例子1：** For example, a waterfront may show important parts of the skyline.

**观点2：** Ordinary neighbourhoods offer a different perspective. **解释2：** Visitors can see how local people use the city. **例子2：** For instance, a market visit can reveal everyday food and conversation.

#### P043-Q6 Are crowded places always unpleasant?

**观点1：** No, crowds can create a lively atmosphere. **解释1：** Shared excitement can make an event feel special. **例子1：** For example, spectators cheering together can enrich a festival or match.

**观点2：** However, crowding becomes unpleasant when it prevents basic comfort. **解释2：** People need space, clear routes and reasonable access. **例子2：** For instance, a packed station feels stressful when someone must catch a train.


## P044 遇到的科技问题

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a problem you had with technology.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿电脑工作素材改编；文件故障补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a computer problem I had while preparing a report for work. I had spent a long time organising the information when the programme suddenly stopped responding.

At first, I felt worried because I was not sure whether my recent changes had been saved. My first instinct was to click repeatedly, but that did not help. I stopped, checked the available recovery options and looked for the latest saved version.

Fortunately, I could recover most of the work. I still had to repeat a small part, which was frustrating, but the whole report was not lost. I also asked a colleague to help me check what might have caused the problem.

The experience reminded me not to take technology for granted. Since then, I have paid more attention to saving regularly and keeping a backup of important files. The most useful lesson was to respond calmly, because panicking made it harder to think through the steps that could actually help.

A reliable routine for saving work now feels just as important as using powerful software tools.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：科技与效率；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P044-Q1 How does technology save time?

**观点1：** Technology can handle repetitive steps efficiently. **解释1：** People can spend less time repeating routine actions. **例子1：** For example, software can help organise a large collection of data.

**观点2：** It also reduces the need to travel for simple tasks. **解释2：** Information and communication are available remotely. **例子2：** For instance, a quick online meeting can replace a journey for a short discussion.

#### P044-Q2 Can people become too dependent on technology?

**观点1：** Yes, dependence becomes a problem when basic alternatives disappear. **解释1：** A small failure can interrupt an entire routine. **例子1：** For example, a lost file may stop work without a backup.

**观点2：** However, regular use is not automatically harmful. **解释2：** Useful tools can support rather than replace understanding. **例子2：** For instance, checking a generated draft keeps the user involved in the result.

#### P044-Q3 How should people respond to a technical problem?

**观点1：** They should identify the problem calmly. **解释1：** Random actions can make it harder to understand what happened. **例子1：** For example, checking a saved version may help after a programme freezes.

**观点2：** They should also learn from the incident. **解释2：** A preventive habit can reduce future disruption. **例子2：** For instance, keeping backups makes an unexpected failure less stressful.

#### P044-Q4 Should children learn basic computer skills?

**观点1：** Yes, practical skills support study and everyday tasks. **解释1：** Children need more than familiarity with entertainment apps. **例子1：** For example, organising files helps them manage school projects.

**观点2：** They should also learn to evaluate information. **解释2：** Confidence with a device does not guarantee sound judgement. **例子2：** For instance, a pupil can compare sources before accepting an online claim.

#### P044-Q5 Why do people buy new electronic products?

**观点1：** Some purchases meet a practical need. **解释1：** An existing device may no longer perform required tasks well. **例子1：** For example, a worker may need a more suitable computer.

**观点2：** Other purchases reflect curiosity or social influence. **解释2：** Novelty can feel exciting even without a clear purpose. **例子2：** For instance, an impressive demonstration may tempt someone to exceed a budget.

#### P044-Q6 Will technology replace human judgement?

**观点1：** It may assist with routine decisions. **解释1：** Clear patterns and repeated tasks can be handled consistently. **例子1：** For example, software can flag unusual entries for review.

**观点2：** However, people still need to interpret context and responsibility. **解释2：** A technically plausible suggestion may not suit the real situation. **例子2：** For instance, a worker should check whether a generated solution addresses the actual problem.


## P045 擅长学习和说语言的人

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe someone who is good at learning languages.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿Luwen、英语交流素材改编；语言能力补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to talk about my friend Luwen, who seems very comfortable learning and using languages. I noticed this quality when we discussed English practice and met people through sport.

What impresses me is not that he knows every word. He is willing to start a conversation even when he is unsure. If he does not understand something, he asks a simple question or requests an example instead of becoming embarrassed.

He also learns phrases in context. After hearing a useful expression in a video, he writes it down with the situation where it appeared. Later, he tries to use it in a conversation. This makes his learning feel connected to real communication.

I think his patience and curiosity are more important than any special talent. He treats mistakes as information rather than proof that he is bad at languages. His approach has encouraged me to practise more openly and focus on getting my meaning across before worrying about making every sentence perfect.

That has made the learning process feel more manageable and less like a test of my intelligence.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：语言学习；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P045-Q1 Why do people learn foreign languages?

**观点1：** They want to communicate with more people. **解释1：** A shared language makes practical exchanges easier. **例子1：** For example, a traveller can ask a shopkeeper about equipment.

**观点2：** Languages also provide access to learning resources. **解释2：** Useful information may not exist in a familiar language. **例子2：** For instance, a worker may read a technical explanation from another country.

#### P045-Q2 What helps people become confident speakers?

**观点1：** Regular meaningful practice builds confidence. **解释1：** People discover that communication is possible despite mistakes. **例子1：** For example, asking a real question in a shop can feel rewarding.

**观点2：** Supportive feedback is also important. **解释2：** Learners need specific guidance rather than embarrassment. **例子2：** For instance, a partner can clarify one unclear phrase after the speaker finishes.

#### P045-Q3 Is living abroad necessary to learn a language?

**观点1：** No, consistent practice can happen at home. **解释1：** Learners can use recordings, books and conversations. **例子1：** For example, a daily speaking partner can create regular opportunities.

**观点2：** However, living abroad may increase practical use. **解释2：** Everyday tasks provide repeated reasons to communicate. **例子2：** For instance, arranging transport or shopping can turn learning into an immediate need.

#### P045-Q4 Should children start learning languages early?

**观点1：** Early exposure can make a language feel familiar. **解释1：** Children can explore sounds and expressions through enjoyable activities. **例子1：** For example, simple songs can introduce useful words.

**观点2：** However, the experience should remain appropriate and encouraging. **解释2：** Pressure can reduce curiosity. **例子2：** For instance, a short game may be more suitable than a long formal test.

#### P045-Q5 Is accuracy more important than fluency?

**观点1：** Accuracy matters when unclear language changes meaning. **解释1：** Some situations require precise information. **例子1：** For example, a work instruction should communicate the correct deadline.

**观点2：** Fluency also matters because conversation needs to continue. **解释2：** Excessive hesitation can interrupt the exchange. **例子2：** For instance, a beginner can use simple sentences to ask for help successfully.

#### P045-Q6 Can technology improve language learning?

**观点1：** It provides convenient access to practice materials. **解释1：** Learners can repeat audio and organise useful expressions. **例子1：** For example, recording an answer helps someone notice hesitation.

**观点2：** However, active use remains necessary. **解释2：** Collecting resources alone does not develop communication. **例子2：** For instance, a learner should try a new phrase in conversation rather than only save it.


## P046 特别场合的食物

截图：第7张，显示共5个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe food you eat on a special occasion.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体对象；怎样接触或获得；外观、内容或用途；它为什么对你重要。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P38青岛家庭海鲜改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The special food I would like to describe is a seafood meal my family enjoyed during a holiday in Qingdao. It became a highlight of the trip because we could choose the ingredients together.

We visited a market near the coast and looked at the different stalls. There were prawns, crabs and clams, so we selected a mixture rather than ordering a single dish. A nearby restaurant cooked everything for a small fee.

The food tasted fresh, and I particularly enjoyed the tender texture and natural flavour. However, what made the meal special was the occasion. We had time to sit together without rushing back to work or dealing with everyday responsibilities.

During the meal, we talked about the places we had visited and laughed at small moments from the journey. I would associate that kind of seafood dinner with family holidays rather than an ordinary weekday. It reminds me that a memorable meal depends on the people and the atmosphere as much as the ingredients.

I would gladly share that experience again when we next have a holiday together.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：食物与庆祝；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P046-Q1 Why is food important at celebrations?

**观点1：** Food gives people a shared activity. **解释1：** Sitting together creates time for conversation. **例子1：** For example, a family meal can reconnect relatives after a busy period.

**观点2：** It also carries personal or cultural meaning. **解释2：** Certain dishes become connected with particular occasions. **例子2：** For instance, a birthday cake can symbolise care and attention from family members.

#### P046-Q2 Why do people spend more on special meals?

**观点1：** They want the occasion to feel different from daily life. **解释1：** Extra care can express appreciation. **例子1：** For example, a family may choose favourite ingredients for a reunion.

**观点2：** They may also value the shared experience. **解释2：** The memory can seem worth more than the price alone. **例子2：** For instance, a holiday seafood dinner can become a trip's highlight.

#### P046-Q3 Is home cooking better than eating out?

**观点1：** Home cooking gives people control over the meal. **解释1：** They can adjust ingredients and portions to their preferences. **例子1：** For example, a family can prepare familiar dishes for a celebration.

**观点2：** Eating out offers convenience and variety. **解释2：** It removes some preparation and cleaning tasks. **例子2：** For instance, busy friends may meet at a restaurant after work.

#### P046-Q4 Why are some people less interested in cooking?

**观点1：** Time pressure can make preparation feel difficult. **解释1：** Shopping, cooking and cleaning compete with other responsibilities. **例子1：** For example, a late-working employee may choose a quick alternative.

**观点2：** Some people also lack confidence. **解释2：** They may not know how to begin with simple meals. **例子2：** For instance, clear beginner recipes could make cooking feel more manageable.

#### P046-Q5 Should people use phones during shared meals?

**观点1：** Usually, limiting phone use improves conversation. **解释1：** Constant checking divides attention between the table and other activities. **例子1：** For example, a family celebration feels warmer when everyone participates.

**观点2：** However, brief necessary use can be reasonable. **解释2：** A strict rule may ignore practical needs. **例子2：** For instance, someone may need to confirm travel arrangements before returning to the conversation.

#### P046-Q6 Can traditional food remain popular with young people?

**观点1：** Yes, familiar food can connect young people with family memories. **解释1：** Its appeal goes beyond the ingredients. **例子1：** For example, a pastry may remind someone of a childhood celebration.

**观点2：** Traditions can also adapt in presentation. **解释2：** Convenient options can fit modern routines. **例子2：** For instance, a small portion may suit someone who wants to try a traditional speciality.


## P047 喜欢的现场体育赛事

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a live sporting event you enjoyed.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P07飞盘逆转比赛改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a frisbee match I watched at my university. My friend Luwen was playing, and several of us went to support his team.

The game became exciting because his team fell behind early. Some players looked frustrated, and I began to worry that they would lose confidence. During a short break, Luwen spoke calmly to the others and encouraged them to focus on simple passes and better movement.

Gradually, their cooperation improved. They stopped rushing and created more space for each other. When they finally caught up, everyone around me became much louder. The final moments were tense, but they managed to secure a narrow win.

I enjoyed the event because I could see the effect of teamwork happening directly in front of me. The atmosphere was much more involving than watching a recording alone. I also felt proud of Luwen, whose calm attitude helped the team respond to pressure instead of giving up when the result looked uncertain.

It made me want to return to training and contribute more positively when my own team struggled.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：运动与比赛；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P047-Q1 Why do people enjoy watching live sport?

**观点1：** Live sport creates a strong shared atmosphere. **解释1：** The audience reacts together to uncertain events. **例子1：** For example, a late comeback can make a crowd feel involved.

**观点2：** It also allows close observation of skill. **解释2：** Viewers can appreciate movement and cooperation directly. **例子2：** For instance, watching a team organise itself can reveal tactics missed in a short highlight.

#### P047-Q2 What can children learn from team sports?

**观点1：** They can learn cooperation. **解释1：** Success depends on considering other people's positions and needs. **例子1：** For example, passing at the right time may help more than showing individual skill.

**观点2：** They can also practise handling setbacks. **解释2：** Games provide manageable experiences of disappointment. **例子2：** For instance, losing a match can lead to discussion about what to improve.

#### P047-Q3 Is winning the most important part of sport?

**观点1：** Winning can motivate people to practise seriously. **解释1：** A clear objective gives effort direction. **例子1：** For example, a team may work on weaknesses before a competition.

**观点2：** However, participation also has social and personal value. **解释2：** Enjoyment and development matter beyond the final score. **例子2：** For instance, beginners may gain confidence even when their team loses.

#### P047-Q4 What makes a good team captain?

**观点1：** A good captain communicates clearly. **解释1：** Players need a shared understanding under pressure. **例子1：** For example, a simple instruction can reduce confusion during a close game.

**观点2：** The captain should also support morale. **解释2：** Frustration spreads when nobody responds calmly. **例子2：** For instance, encouraging a teammate after a mistake can help the whole group refocus.

#### P047-Q5 Should schools provide more sporting opportunities?

**观点1：** Yes, varied opportunities help students find an activity they enjoy. **解释1：** Not every child likes the same sport. **例子1：** For example, offering several team and individual options broadens participation.

**观点2：** Schools should also make access inclusive. **解释2：** Equipment or ability should not automatically exclude beginners. **例子2：** For instance, introductory sessions can focus on learning rather than selection for competition.

#### P047-Q6 Do professional athletes influence ordinary people?

**观点1：** They can encourage interest in physical activity. **解释1：** Visible achievements make a sport attractive. **例子1：** For example, a memorable performance may inspire someone to join a club.

**观点2：** They can also shape attitudes towards competition. **解释2：** Their behaviour communicates what success involves. **例子2：** For instance, respectful conduct after a loss can model patience and fairness.


## P048 想从事医疗行业的人

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe someone who wants to work in healthcare.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 新编示范人物Anna；不把职业愿望当成已确认经历。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a friend called Anna who hopes to become a nurse. I got to know her through a group activity, and we later talked about our career plans.

She said she wanted work that involved both practical skills and direct contact with people. Her interest became stronger after spending time with an older relative who needed support. She noticed how much a patient explanation and a considerate attitude could matter to a worried family.

She understands that the job can be demanding. It requires training, attention to detail and cooperation with other professionals. Rather than describing it as an easy way to help people, she talks seriously about the responsibilities involved.

I think her patience would suit that direction. She listens carefully and does not become irritated when someone asks the same question twice. I respect her ambition because it combines a personal interest with useful service. I hope she finds a training path that lets her discover whether the work suits her expectations.

Her thoughtful attitude makes me feel optimistic about her future professional development.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：生活习惯与医疗职业；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P048-Q1 How can people build healthier daily routines?

**观点1：** They can begin with manageable changes. **解释1：** Small actions are easier to repeat during a busy week. **例子1：** For example, a short walk after lunch is a realistic starting point.

**观点2：** Supportive surroundings also help. **解释2：** A convenient option requires less willpower. **例子2：** For instance, keeping a regular break in the schedule makes it easier to leave the desk.

#### P048-Q2 Why do some people struggle to exercise regularly?

**观点1：** Time pressure can make exercise difficult. **解释1：** Work and family responsibilities may use most of the day. **例子1：** For example, a long commute leaves little time for a sports session.

**观点2：** Some people also choose activities they dislike. **解释2：** An unpleasant routine is harder to maintain. **例子2：** For instance, someone who dislikes running may prefer a relaxed team game.

#### P048-Q3 Can friends encourage better habits?

**观点1：** Yes, friends can make a new activity more enjoyable. **解释1：** Shared company reduces the feeling of doing another task. **例子1：** For example, two friends can take a regular evening walk.

**观点2：** They can also provide gentle accountability. **解释2：** Knowing someone is expecting you can support consistency. **例子2：** For instance, a training partner may help someone keep an agreed practice time.

#### P048-Q4 What attracts people to healthcare careers?

**观点1：** Some people want work that helps others directly. **解释1：** Seeing a practical benefit can make effort feel meaningful. **例子1：** For example, a nurse may value reassuring an anxious family.

**观点2：** Others are interested in science and problem solving. **解释2：** They enjoy applying knowledge to complex situations. **例子2：** For instance, a student may like both biology and careful practical work.

#### P048-Q5 What qualities are useful in healthcare work?

**观点1：** Careful attention is essential. **解释1：** Small details can matter when people depend on accurate information. **例子1：** For example, a worker needs to communicate instructions clearly.

**观点2：** Empathy is also valuable. **解释2：** People may feel worried or confused in unfamiliar situations. **例子2：** For instance, a patient explanation can make a family feel more comfortable asking questions.

#### P048-Q6 Should workplaces encourage staff to take breaks?

**观点1：** Yes, reasonable breaks can support concentration. **解释1：** Continuous demanding work can leave people mentally tired. **例子1：** For example, a brief pause may help someone return to a complicated report.

**观点2：** Breaks also show respect for employees' needs. **解释2：** A sustainable routine is easier to maintain. **例子2：** For instance, predictable lunch time lets staff organise meals and short walks.


## P049 在团队中工作

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an occasion when you worked in a team.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P10原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a crucial match when I worked closely with my university frisbee team. Our captain was Luwen, whose calm attitude helped us when the game became difficult.

It was about five years ago that we were first introduced to each other. We had trained together regularly, so we already understood each other quite well. I have known him ever since.

The reason why he is so important to me is that he taught me the true meaning of perseverance. I remember we had a crucial match, and we were falling behind. I was exhausted and about to give up. Without hesitation, Luwen came over, patted my back, and his calmness and confidence were incredibly contagious. Inspired by him, we all pulled ourselves together and eventually won the game.

That experience was a turning point for me. He showed me how to face challenges with courage. That is why he is not just a friend, but a mentor to me.

I learned to focus on helping the team instead of worrying only about my own individual performance.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：团队合作；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P049-Q1 Why is teamwork useful?

**观点1：** Teamwork combines different strengths. **解释1：** One person may provide knowledge that another lacks. **例子1：** For example, a project can benefit from both technical and communication skills.

**观点2：** It also distributes demanding work. **解释2：** Shared responsibility can make a large task manageable. **例子2：** For instance, players coordinate movement instead of expecting one person to control the whole match.

#### P049-Q2 What causes problems within teams?

**观点1：** Unclear responsibilities can create confusion. **解释1：** People may assume that someone else is handling a task. **例子1：** For example, an event may lack equipment because nobody was assigned to bring it.

**观点2：** Poor communication can also increase tension. **解释2：** Small concerns become larger when they remain unspoken. **例子2：** For instance, a teammate may feel ignored after repeated unanswered suggestions.

#### P049-Q3 What should a leader do when a team struggles?

**观点1：** A leader should clarify the immediate priority. **解释1：** A manageable next step reduces panic. **例子1：** For example, a captain can ask players to focus on simple passes.

**观点2：** The leader should also protect morale. **解释2：** Blame can make people afraid to contribute. **例子2：** For instance, acknowledging effort after a mistake helps a teammate rejoin the task confidently.

#### P049-Q4 Is teamwork learned mainly at school?

**观点1：** School can provide valuable practice. **解释1：** Group tasks expose children to cooperation and disagreement. **例子1：** For example, a class project requires sharing materials and responsibilities.

**观点2：** However, learning continues outside school. **解释2：** Families, clubs and workplaces create different challenges. **例子2：** For instance, organising a weekend activity teaches adults to consider other people's time and preferences.

#### P049-Q5 Should teams always follow their most skilled member?

**观点1：** A skilled member can provide useful guidance. **解释1：** Experience may help identify an effective approach. **例子1：** For example, an experienced player can explain a difficult technique.

**观点2：** However, other perspectives still matter. **解释2：** Technical ability does not reveal every practical concern. **例子2：** For instance, a beginner may notice that the instructions are unclear to new members.

#### P049-Q6 How can teams handle different opinions?

**观点1：** They should compare ideas against the shared goal. **解释1：** This keeps discussion focused on usefulness rather than personal status. **例子1：** For example, an organiser can judge venues by access and budget.

**观点2：** They should also give everyone a fair chance to speak. **解释2：** Quiet members may have useful information. **例子2：** For instance, a short round of suggestions can reveal a workable compromise.


## P050 拥有成功商业的人

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe someone who runs a successful business.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P11 Lily改编；改成独立小糕点店，避免虚构品牌所有权。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to talk about my neighbour Lily, who helps run a small family bakery. I know her through our neighbourhood and sometimes visit the shop when I want a simple gift.

The bakery sells traditional pastries and has a group of regular customers. Lily pays attention to small details, such as keeping the counter tidy, explaining ingredients clearly and packing purchases carefully. People seem comfortable asking her for suggestions.

I remember a customer who was unsure what to buy for an older relative. Lily asked about the person's preferences instead of immediately recommending the most expensive box. Her practical advice helped the customer choose something suitable.

I see the business as successful because people return and trust the service. It may not be a large company, but it appears to have a clear place in the community. Lily's example reminds me that success can come from consistent quality and understanding customers, rather than simply attracting attention or expanding as quickly as possible.

I would trust her recommendations because they seem to reflect the customer's needs rather than immediate profit.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：经营与服务；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P050-Q1 What helps a small business succeed?

**观点1：** Consistent quality encourages customers to return. **解释1：** People appreciate knowing what they can expect. **例子1：** For example, a bakery benefits when its familiar products remain reliable.

**观点2：** Personal service can also build trust. **解释2：** Understanding customers creates a reason to choose that shop. **例子2：** For instance, thoughtful advice may matter more than an aggressive discount.

#### P050-Q2 Is starting a business easy?

**观点1：** It can be difficult because several responsibilities arrive together. **解释1：** A good product alone does not manage costs or customers. **例子1：** For example, a cafe owner also needs to organise supplies and service.

**观点2：** However, starting on a manageable scale can support learning. **解释2：** Small experiments reveal problems before expansion. **例子2：** For instance, testing a limited menu can clarify customer preferences.

#### P050-Q3 What makes an employee valuable to a company?

**观点1：** Reliability makes cooperation easier. **解释1：** Colleagues need work to be completed with appropriate care. **例子1：** For example, accurate records prevent confusion later.

**观点2：** A helpful attitude also improves the customer's experience. **解释2：** Ordinary interactions shape the company's reputation. **例子2：** For instance, a patient explanation can make a visitor comfortable returning to the shop.

#### P050-Q4 How important is solving problems creatively in business?

**观点1：** It is important because unexpected situations are common. **解释1：** A rigid routine may not provide a suitable answer. **例子1：** For example, a shopkeeper can cooperate with a neighbour to resolve a payment difficulty.

**观点2：** However, solutions must remain responsible. **解释2：** Creativity should not create a larger problem elsewhere. **例子2：** For instance, staff should explain an alternative clearly before a customer accepts it.

#### P050-Q5 Should family members work together in a business?

**观点1：** It can work well when there is trust. **解释1：** Relatives may share a strong commitment to the business. **例子1：** For example, a family bakery may value a long-standing local reputation.

**观点2：** However, roles still need clear boundaries. **解释2：** Personal relationships can make disagreements harder to discuss. **例子2：** For instance, responsibilities should be agreed rather than assumed because of family status.

#### P050-Q6 Should companies focus only on profit?

**观点1：** No, customer trust matters for lasting success. **解释1：** Short-term gains can damage a reputation if service is poor. **例子1：** For example, an unsuitable recommendation may lose a regular customer.

**观点2：** Employees' working conditions also matter. **解释2：** A business depends on people who can perform sustainably. **例子2：** For instance, clear training and reasonable schedules can support reliable service.


## P051 喜欢在家/花园种植物的人

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe someone who enjoys growing plants at home.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿父母教种植、邻居素材改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to talk about my neighbour Mrs Chen, who enjoys growing plants at home. She is retired and often tells me about the small changes she notices in her plants.

She keeps several pots near a bright window and chooses plants that suit the space. Her routine is quite careful. She checks the soil, adjusts where the pots stand and removes damaged leaves. She does not simply water everything in exactly the same way.

Once, I asked why one plant was not growing well. She explained that it needed attention to light and watering rather than just more water. The conversation reminded me of the basic planting skills my parents taught me when I was younger.

I think she enjoys the patience involved. A new leaf or flower gives her something small to look forward to. Her plants also make the room feel welcoming. Seeing how much she enjoys them has made me more interested in keeping a manageable number of plants myself.

I would start small, so the activity remains enjoyable and fits comfortably into my daily routine.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：自然与植物；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P051-Q1 Why do people enjoy visiting natural places?

**观点1：** Natural surroundings provide a change from demanding routines. **解释1：** People can slow down and focus on something beyond work. **例子1：** For example, a walk among trees can feel refreshing after computer tasks.

**观点2：** They also offer shared recreation. **解释2：** Friends can spend time together without a complicated programme. **例子2：** For instance, a park visit can combine conversation and gentle exercise.

#### P051-Q2 Should cities provide more green spaces?

**观点1：** Yes, nearby greenery makes recreation more accessible. **解释1：** Residents do not always have time to travel far. **例子1：** For example, a small neighbourhood park can support an evening walk.

**观点2：** Green spaces also improve shared surroundings. **解释2：** They give people pleasant places to meet and rest. **例子2：** For instance, shaded seating can make a busy district more welcoming.

#### P051-Q3 How can people be encouraged to protect nature?

**观点1：** Practical activities make the issue tangible. **解释1：** People see the effect of their own behaviour. **例子1：** For example, collecting litter can reveal how much waste visitors leave.

**观点2：** Clear facilities also support better choices. **解释2：** Good intentions are easier to follow when alternatives are convenient. **例子2：** For instance, visible bins can help visitors dispose of rubbish properly.

#### P051-Q4 Why do some people enjoy growing plants?

**观点1：** Growing plants offers visible progress. **解释1：** Small changes provide a sense of achievement. **例子1：** For example, a new leaf can reward weeks of regular care.

**观点2：** The activity can also create a calming routine. **解释2：** People focus on simple tasks away from screens. **例子2：** For instance, checking a few pots can become a pleasant morning habit.

#### P051-Q5 Should children spend more time outdoors?

**观点1：** Yes, outdoor activity offers different experiences from classroom learning. **解释1：** Children can move, explore and notice living things. **例子1：** For example, a park visit may lead to questions about insects.

**观点2：** However, the activity should suit the setting. **解释2：** Adults need to balance freedom with practical supervision. **例子2：** For instance, a nature walk can use clear boundaries while allowing exploration.

#### P051-Q6 Can tourism harm natural places?

**观点1：** Yes, heavy use can damage the environment. **解释1：** Visitors may leave rubbish or disturb sensitive areas. **例子1：** For example, walking away from paths can affect vegetation.

**观点2：** However, well-managed tourism can support protection. **解释2：** Visitor interest can create reasons to maintain a place carefully. **例子2：** For instance, clear routes and educational signs can guide more considerate behaviour.


## P052 有趣视频

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an interesting video you watched.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体对象；怎样接触或获得；外观、内容或用途；它为什么对你重要。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P49原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

One of the most interesting things I have ever seen on social media was a short science video, which I came across on Instagram about six months ago.

The presenters used cognitive testing puzzles and reaction time tests to explore how people make decisions under pressure. I found it fascinating because it combined science, entertainment and practical knowledge.

What impressed me most was that the experiment was presented in a very vivid way. It was not a boring lecture. Instead, it used real scenes, clear comparisons and a bit of humor to explain the result.

I shared it with my friends because I thought it was not only interesting but also useful. It reminded me that social media can broaden our horizons if we follow the right content.

I also liked that the presenters compared different attempts instead of simply announcing an answer. Seeing the process helped me understand why the result was interesting. It made me want to ask better questions about the information I encounter online and to distinguish a convincing explanation from something that only looks impressive.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：影视与视频；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P052-Q1 What makes a film or programme enjoyable?

**观点1：** A convincing story helps people care about the outcome. **解释1：** Characters need motives that viewers can understand. **例子1：** For example, a difficult choice can create more interest than a long action scene.

**观点2：** Presentation also matters. **解释2：** Acting, sound and visual details support the atmosphere. **例子2：** For instance, an unusual office design can strengthen a mysterious television story.

#### P052-Q2 Why do different people enjoy different kinds of entertainment?

**观点1：** People use entertainment to meet different needs. **解释1：** Some want excitement while others want a quiet break. **例子1：** For example, a tired worker may choose a gentle comedy.

**观点2：** Personal experience also shapes taste. **解释2：** Familiar themes can feel more meaningful. **例子2：** For instance, someone who plays sport may enjoy a documentary about teamwork.

#### P052-Q3 Are online videos useful for learning?

**观点1：** They can make a process easy to observe. **解释1：** Visual demonstrations show steps that text may not explain clearly. **例子1：** For example, a learner can watch how to hold a frisbee.

**观点2：** However, quality varies considerably. **解释2：** An attractive presentation does not guarantee a reliable explanation. **例子2：** For instance, viewers should question a dramatic claim without supporting evidence.

#### P052-Q4 Is watching at home better than going to a cinema?

**观点1：** Watching at home offers convenience. **解释1：** People can choose a time that fits their routine. **例子1：** For example, friends can watch together after dinner without travelling.

**观点2：** A cinema provides a more focused experience. **解释2：** The large screen and shared atmosphere can increase involvement. **例子2：** For instance, an impressive visual sequence may feel more powerful there.

#### P052-Q5 Why do people share entertaining videos?

**观点1：** They want to share an enjoyable reaction. **解释1：** A short clip can make someone laugh quickly. **例子1：** For example, friends may send a funny scene during a busy day.

**观点2：** Sharing also expresses personal interests. **解释2：** It gives people a reason to start a conversation. **例子2：** For instance, a science video can lead to discussion among curious colleagues.

#### P052-Q6 Should entertainment always teach people something?

**观点1：** No, relaxation is a valid purpose. **解释1：** People sometimes need a break from demanding tasks. **例子1：** For example, a light comedy can make an ordinary evening pleasant.

**观点2：** However, meaningful ideas can add lasting value. **解释2：** Viewers may continue thinking after the programme ends. **例子2：** For instance, a story about work and identity can inspire a thoughtful conversation.


## P053 喜欢或不喜欢的高建筑

截图：第7张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a tall building you like or dislike.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿上海高楼、Building素材改编；未指定真实建筑。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a tall office building near a busy area of Shanghai. I often notice it while travelling through the neighbourhood, although I have not worked inside it.

The building has a simple modern appearance with large glass windows. During the day, it reflects the changing light, and at night it becomes part of the city's bright skyline. I like the way it looks from a distance, especially beside lower buildings.

However, my feelings are mixed when I imagine using it every day. A high floor might provide an impressive view, but I would not enjoy depending on crowded lifts or spending a long time entering and leaving the building.

Overall, I like it as part of the cityscape rather than as my ideal home. It represents the energy and opportunities of a large city, but comfort and convenience matter more to me personally. A building can be visually impressive without being the place where I would feel most relaxed.

I would judge a place to live by daily comfort rather than its appearance in a photograph.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：建筑与居住；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P053-Q1 Why do people enjoy visiting traditional buildings?

**观点1：** Traditional buildings make history visible. **解释1：** Visitors can see how earlier generations organised daily life. **例子1：** For example, a courtyard suggests how a family shared outdoor space.

**观点2：** They also provide visual variety. **解释2：** Distinctive designs make a place memorable. **例子2：** For instance, older houses can give a neighbourhood character beside modern offices.

#### P053-Q2 Should cities preserve old buildings?

**观点1：** Yes, valuable buildings can protect local identity. **解释1：** Once demolished, an original setting cannot easily be recreated. **例子1：** For example, a historic street can show a city's development.

**观点2：** However, preservation needs practical maintenance. **解释2：** A neglected building cannot serve visitors or residents well. **例子2：** For instance, repairs may improve safety while retaining important design features.

#### P053-Q3 What makes a home comfortable?

**观点1：** A practical layout supports everyday activities. **解释1：** People need suitable space for rest, work and storage. **例子1：** For example, a quiet corner can separate study from family noise.

**观点2：** Personal connections also create comfort. **解释2：** Familiar objects make a space feel like home. **例子2：** For instance, family photographs can make a simple room welcoming.

#### P053-Q4 Are tall buildings always suitable for city life?

**观点1：** Tall buildings can use limited land efficiently. **解释1：** They place many homes or workplaces in a compact area. **例子1：** For example, an office tower can accommodate several companies.

**观点2：** However, daily access needs careful planning. **解释2：** Crowded lifts and surrounding traffic can create inconvenience. **例子2：** For instance, many people leaving at once may face long waits.

#### P053-Q5 Should a building's appearance matter more than its function?

**观点1：** Function should usually come first. **解释1：** People must use the space comfortably every day. **例子1：** For example, an attractive office is frustrating without suitable light or quiet.

**观点2：** However, appearance still affects experience. **解释2：** Thoughtful design can make a place pleasant and distinctive. **例子2：** For instance, a welcoming entrance can improve how visitors feel.

#### P053-Q6 Why might people enjoy visiting a home but dislike living there?

**观点1：** A visit highlights atmosphere and novelty. **解释1：** People can appreciate an unusual setting for a short time. **例子1：** For example, a courtyard may feel peaceful during an afternoon.

**观点2：** Permanent residence involves practical demands. **解释2：** Maintenance, transport and comfort matter throughout the year. **例子2：** For instance, a beautiful house may be inconvenient for a daily commute.


## P054 去过的无聊地方

截图：第8张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a place you found boring.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 新编示范：商业展厅，借用原稿不喜欢活动的情绪。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to describe a commercial showroom I visited with a friend one weekend. He was interested in the products, so I agreed to go along even though I knew little about them.

At first, I expected to see demonstrations or try something myself. Instead, most of the visit involved walking past similar displays and listening to long explanations from sales staff. The information was detailed, but I did not have a clear reason to care about it.

My friend asked several questions, while I gradually lost concentration. I tried to remain polite and looked for something that connected with my own interests. Unfortunately, the visit continued much longer than I had expected.

I found the place boring mainly because the activity was passive and did not suit me. I do not think it was necessarily a bad showroom; my friend enjoyed it. The experience reminded me that choosing an outing should involve everyone's interests, especially when people have limited free time together.

Next time, I would agree on a reasonable visit length before joining an outing like that again.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：无聊与兴趣；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P054-Q1 Why do people become bored?

**观点1：** Repetition can reduce interest. **解释1：** An activity may offer little novelty or challenge. **例子1：** For example, checking nearly identical information for hours can feel tiring.

**观点2：** A lack of personal relevance also matters. **解释2：** People struggle to care when they cannot see a purpose. **例子2：** For instance, a visitor may dislike a detailed showroom presentation about unfamiliar products.

#### P054-Q2 Is boredom always harmful?

**观点1：** No, it can encourage people to find a new activity. **解释1：** A quiet period may lead to curiosity or imagination. **例子1：** For example, a child might invent a game without planned entertainment.

**观点2：** However, persistent boredom can reduce engagement. **解释2：** People may stop paying attention to important tasks. **例子2：** For instance, a passive lesson may leave students remembering very little.

#### P054-Q3 How can teachers reduce boredom in lessons?

**观点1：** They can involve students actively. **解释1：** Participation gives learners something concrete to do. **例子1：** For example, a demonstration followed by practice is more engaging than continuous explanation.

**观点2：** They can also connect content with practical questions. **解释2：** A clear purpose makes effort feel worthwhile. **例子2：** For instance, a familiar daily problem can introduce an abstract scientific idea.

#### P054-Q4 Why do children and adults find different things boring?

**观点1：** Their experiences and interests differ. **解释1：** An activity may be new to one person and repetitive to another. **例子1：** For example, a child may enjoy a simple discovery that an adult already understands.

**观点2：** Their responsibilities also change expectations. **解释2：** Adults may value quietness after a demanding day. **例子2：** For instance, a peaceful walk can feel refreshing rather than uneventful.

#### P054-Q5 Can phones solve boredom?

**观点1：** They can provide a quick change of attention. **解释1：** A useful or amusing clip may make a wait more pleasant. **例子1：** For example, someone can listen to an interesting recording on a journey.

**观点2：** However, aimless use may only fill time. **解释2：** It does not always leave the person satisfied. **例子2：** For instance, prolonged scrolling can replace a more enjoyable conversation.

#### P054-Q6 How can people make an ordinary day more interesting?

**观点1：** They can notice or try something slightly different. **解释1：** A major event is not necessary for variety. **例子1：** For example, taking another walking route may reveal an unfamiliar street.

**观点2：** They can also share activities with others. **解释2：** Conversation adds perspectives to familiar experiences. **例子2：** For instance, discussing a film with friends can make the evening more memorable.


## P055 近期改变的计划

截图：第8张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a plan you recently changed.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿公园朋友素材改编；天气改计划补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a weekend plan that I changed because of the weather. Several friends and I had arranged to play frisbee in a park and have a simple picnic afterwards.

I had checked who could come and suggested that everyone bring a little food. However, on the morning of the outing, heavy rain made the outdoor plan unrealistic. At first, I felt disappointed because organising a time that suited everyone had taken some effort.

Rather than cancelling the whole gathering, I sent a message suggesting an indoor alternative. We decided to meet at home, share the food and watch a film. I made sure everyone knew about the change before setting off.

The afternoon turned out to be relaxed and enjoyable. We still had the conversation and company that we had wanted from the original plan. I learned that having a clear purpose makes it easier to be flexible about the details, instead of treating every change as a failure.

I would keep a similar backup idea in mind whenever I arrange another outdoor activity with friends.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：计划与条理；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P055-Q1 Why do people plan their daily activities?

**观点1：** Planning helps people identify priorities. **解释1：** Without a clear order, easy tasks can replace important ones. **例子1：** For example, a student can revise a difficult chapter before checking messages.

**观点2：** It also makes cooperation easier. **解释2：** Other people can prepare when they know the schedule. **例子2：** For instance, teammates can arrange transport before a training session.

#### P055-Q2 Should children learn to make plans?

**观点1：** Yes, small plans can develop responsibility. **解释1：** Children learn that an activity requires preparation. **例子1：** For example, they can pack school materials the evening before.

**观点2：** However, plans should leave room for play. **解释2：** An overly detailed schedule can make ordinary days stressful. **例子2：** For instance, a free afternoon lets a child choose a game independently.

#### P055-Q3 Is a flexible plan better than a strict one?

**观点1：** Usually, flexibility makes a plan more realistic. **解释1：** Unexpected delays are part of everyday life. **例子1：** For example, extra travel time prevents one late bus from ruining a schedule.

**观点2：** However, some fixed points are necessary. **解释2：** Other people need commitments they can rely on. **例子2：** For instance, a group still needs an agreed meeting time.

#### P055-Q4 How can someone become more organised?

**观点1：** They can give important items a fixed place. **解释1：** This reduces the effort needed to find things. **例子1：** For example, keeping keys by the door avoids a rushed search.

**观点2：** They can also review priorities regularly. **解释2：** A list becomes less useful when circumstances change. **例子2：** For instance, a worker can update tomorrow's tasks before leaving the office.

#### P055-Q5 Does planning always lead to success?

**观点1：** Planning improves the chance of success. **解释1：** It turns a large goal into manageable actions. **例子1：** For example, a revision timetable can spread practice across several weeks.

**观点2：** However, results also depend on execution. **解释2：** A detailed plan achieves little if nobody follows it. **例子2：** For instance, a team must actually practise the skills listed in its programme.

#### P055-Q6 Why do some people dislike making plans?

**观点1：** Some people value freedom and spontaneity. **解释1：** A fixed schedule can feel restrictive during leisure time. **例子1：** For example, a traveller may want to explore a street unexpectedly.

**观点2：** Others have experienced unrealistic plans. **解释2：** Repeated failure can make planning itself seem pointless. **例子2：** For instance, an exhausting study timetable may discourage someone from trying again.


## P056 想颁布的新法律

截图：第8张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a new law you would like to introduce.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 规则或法律针对什么问题；你如何了解或想到它；具体怎样实施；你认为它有什么作用。区分现行规则和自己的政策设想。

**答案来源：** 新编政策设想：公园一次性用品减量；不是现行法律。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to introduce a rule that reduces unnecessary disposable packaging in public parks. The idea came to me after noticing how much rubbish can remain after a busy weekend.

Under my proposal, large organised events in parks would need a clear waste reduction plan. Food sellers would offer practical reusable options where possible, and visitors would have easy access to clearly labelled collection points. The organisers would also be responsible for cleaning the site properly.

I would not expect a rule to work through penalties alone. People need convenient alternatives and simple explanations. For example, an event could make it easy to return reusable cups instead of leaving visitors unsure what to do with them.

I would support this law because parks should remain pleasant places for people and wildlife. My friend Luwen's environmental activities have shown me how much difference small actions can make. A clear rule could help those actions become a normal part of organising public events.

I would want residents and organisers to discuss the practical details before the proposal was put into effect.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：环保与规则；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P056-Q1 Should environmental protection depend on individuals or governments?

**观点1：** Individuals influence everyday behaviour. **解释1：** Repeated small actions can reduce unnecessary waste. **例子1：** For example, visitors can take their rubbish away after a picnic.

**观点2：** Governments and organisers shape the available choices. **解释2：** People need systems that make responsible action practical. **例子2：** For instance, clear collection points support proper waste sorting.

#### P056-Q2 What makes an environmental rule effective?

**观点1：** The rule should be easy to understand. **解释1：** People need to know exactly what action is expected. **例子1：** For example, a sign can show which items belong in each bin.

**观点2：** It also needs practical support. **解释2：** A demand is difficult to follow without suitable facilities. **例子2：** For instance, reusable cups require an accessible return process.

#### P056-Q3 Is education more useful than penalties for environmental protection?

**观点1：** Education helps people understand the purpose of a rule. **解释1：** Understanding can support long-term cooperation. **例子1：** For example, showing how litter affects a park can encourage considerate behaviour.

**观点2：** However, consequences may still be needed for repeated disregard. **解释2：** Otherwise responsible users may feel unfairly burdened. **例子2：** For instance, organisers should remain accountable for cleaning up after an event.

#### P056-Q4 How can businesses reduce unnecessary waste?

**观点1：** They can reconsider packaging and routine materials. **解释1：** Small changes repeated across many sales can matter. **例子1：** For example, a shop can avoid adding an unwanted disposable bag.

**观点2：** They can also make repair or reuse practical. **解释2：** Products need not be discarded after a minor problem. **例子2：** For instance, a store can help customers replace a damaged part.

#### P056-Q5 Should economic development take priority over nature?

**观点1：** Development matters because people need work and services. **解释1：** Ignoring livelihoods can make environmental proposals difficult to support. **例子1：** For example, a community may depend on visitor income.

**观点2：** However, damaged surroundings can weaken long-term wellbeing. **解释2：** Growth should account for the resources it uses. **例子2：** For instance, tourism loses value if the attraction becomes polluted or unpleasant.

#### P056-Q6 How can schools encourage environmental responsibility?

**观点1：** They can connect lessons with practical routines. **解释1：** Children understand responsibility better through repeated actions. **例子1：** For example, sorting classroom waste makes the idea concrete.

**观点2：** They can also organise thoughtful outdoor activities. **解释2：** Direct experience creates a stronger connection with nature. **例子2：** For instance, a supervised park visit can include observing wildlife and discussing litter.


## P057 重要决定

截图：第8张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an important decision made with the help of other people

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P46原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, I'd like to share my own personal experience.

It was last year when I was under a lot of stress from both study and daily life. My best friend and I discussed it and finally made the decision to take a meditation course together.
