The reason why we made this decision was that both of us had a tight schedule and often struggled with sleep. My friend helped me make the decision because he had already tried meditation for a while and found it really helpful. He told me it could calm my mind and help me get my head straight.

In the end, I felt it was a wise decision. It helped me recharge myself, regain inner peace and build a better routine.

My friend did not simply make the choice for me. We compared what the course involved with the time we could realistically spare. That conversation helped me feel responsible for the decision. I was more willing to continue afterwards because I understood why I had chosen it and what I hoped to gain.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：决定与改变；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P057-Q1 Should important decisions be made quickly?

**观点1：** Usually, important decisions deserve time for relevant information. **解释1：** Rushing can hide costs or practical difficulties. **例子1：** For example, choosing a course requires checking its schedule and content.

**观点2：** However, endless delay can also be unhelpful. **解释2：** Some uncertainty remains even after careful thought. **例子2：** For instance, a suitable opportunity may disappear while someone keeps comparing similar options.

#### P057-Q2 Who should help young people make decisions?

**观点1：** Parents can provide experience and support. **解释1：** They may notice consequences that young people overlook. **例子1：** For example, they can help compare the practical costs of study options.

**观点2：** Young people should still have a voice. **解释2：** They are more committed when they understand and accept the choice. **例子2：** For instance, selecting a hobby should reflect the child's actual interests.

#### P057-Q3 Why do people change their decisions?

**观点1：** New information can alter what seems sensible. **解释1：** The original plan may no longer fit the situation. **例子1：** For example, bad weather can make an outdoor event unsuitable.

**观点2：** Experience also reveals unrealistic expectations. **解释2：** Trying something shows limits that planning missed. **例子2：** For instance, a long study session may prove impossible after a full working day.

#### P057-Q4 Is advice from friends useful for decisions?

**观点1：** Yes, friends can offer a different perspective. **解释1：** They may recognise habits or strengths that someone overlooks. **例子1：** For example, a friend can suggest a more realistic daily routine.

**观点2：** However, advice should be evaluated personally. **解释2：** Friends do not share every responsibility or preference. **例子2：** For instance, a career choice must fit the decision-maker's own circumstances.

#### P057-Q5 What should a group do when members disagree?

**观点1：** The group should clarify its shared purpose. **解释1：** People may agree on the goal while preferring different methods. **例子1：** For example, friends may all want time together despite disagreeing about the venue.

**观点2：** It should then compare practical options fairly. **解释2：** Listening prevents the loudest person from deciding everything. **例子2：** For instance, budget and travel time can guide a compromise.

#### P057-Q6 Does a good result mean a decision was good?

**观点1：** A good result can suggest the choice worked well. **解释1：** It may show that planning matched the circumstances. **例子1：** For example, a manageable study routine can lead to consistent practice.

**观点2：** However, luck also affects outcomes. **解释2：** The decision process matters as well as the result. **例子2：** For instance, a risky choice may succeed once without being sensible to repeat.


## P058 发小

截图：第8张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a friend from your childhood.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P03绘画同学素材改编；改名Ming以区分大学认识的Luwen。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to describe my childhood friend Ming, who was in my primary school art club. We spent many afternoons drawing together and gradually became close friends.

He was curious and imaginative, and he often carried a small sketchbook. If he noticed an unusual cloud or a funny expression, he wanted to draw it immediately. I was less confident, but his enthusiasm encouraged me to join in.

One memory that stands out is a group painting activity. I had forgotten some of my materials and felt embarrassed. Ming shared what he had and helped us reorganise the work. We finished the picture together, and the teacher was pleased with our cooperation.

I remember him warmly because he made ordinary school days more enjoyable. He was willing to help without making me feel foolish. Although our lives have changed since then, that friendship showed me how shared interests and small acts of kindness can create a connection that stays meaningful for years.

I hope to reconnect when the opportunity arises, because old friendships can preserve a valuable part of our lives.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：朋友与人际；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P058-Q1 What makes someone a good friend?

**观点1：** Reliability is important in friendship. **解释1：** People need to know that support is available when it matters. **例子1：** For example, a friend can listen patiently during a difficult week.

**观点2：** Shared enjoyment also keeps the relationship alive. **解释2：** Friendship should include ordinary pleasant experiences. **例子2：** For instance, a regular game gives two people time to laugh together.

#### P058-Q2 Why are some people more popular than others?

**观点1：** Considerate people make others feel comfortable. **解释1：** Listening and including newcomers help people feel welcome. **例子1：** For example, a captain who supports beginners can become well liked.

**观点2：** Visible ability can also attract attention. **解释2：** People often admire skills they hope to develop. **例子2：** For instance, an excellent musician may draw others into a school club.

#### P058-Q3 Is it easy to maintain friendships after leaving school?

**观点1：** It becomes harder because routines separate. **解释1：** Friends may work in different places and have limited free time. **例子1：** For example, a spontaneous meeting may require planning several weeks ahead.

**观点2：** However, regular small contact can preserve a connection. **解释2：** Friendship does not require meeting every day. **例子2：** For instance, a thoughtful message can show continuing interest.

#### P058-Q4 Can people with different interests become close friends?

**观点1：** Yes, shared values can matter more than identical hobbies. **解释1：** People can respect and support each other despite different preferences. **例子1：** For example, a quiet reader and an athlete may both value honesty.

**观点2：** Different interests can also enrich a friendship. **解释2：** Each person introduces the other to unfamiliar experiences. **例子2：** For instance, one friend might suggest a first museum visit.

#### P058-Q5 Why do people want to meet someone again?

**观点1：** An interesting conversation can create curiosity. **解释1：** People want to understand a perspective they have only briefly encountered. **例子1：** For example, a resourceful shop owner may have useful stories about business.

**观点2：** A welcoming manner also encourages further contact. **解释2：** Feeling respected makes a second conversation appealing. **例子2：** For instance, a patient listener can leave a stronger impression than an impressive job title.

#### P058-Q6 Should friends always agree with one another?

**观点1：** No, honest disagreement can be helpful. **解释1：** A friend may notice a problem that someone has overlooked. **例子1：** For example, they can question an unrealistic study plan.

**观点2：** However, disagreement should remain respectful. **解释2：** Criticising the idea is different from insulting the person. **例子2：** For instance, offering a practical alternative keeps the conversation constructive.


## P059 交通拥堵

截图：第8张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a time you were delayed by heavy traffic.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿交通拥堵素材改编；短途情节补写；调整叙述角度。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe being stuck in traffic while travelling across the city with a friend. We were on our way to dinner and had underestimated how busy the roads would be.

The destination was not particularly far away, so we expected the trip to be straightforward. Unfortunately, we left during a busy period and soon became stuck in slow traffic. The car moved forward a little and then stopped again.

At first, we chatted and listened to music. However, as the delay continued, I became worried about keeping everyone waiting. I sent a message explaining the situation, but I still felt uncomfortable because we had not allowed enough time.

We eventually arrived late and slightly tired before the evening had properly started. I disliked the journey because it felt avoidable. Since then, I have tried to check transport options more carefully and consider the subway when roads are likely to be crowded. A short distance does not always mean a quick trip.

It also reminded me that choosing transport carefully is part of being considerate towards other people.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：旅行与交通；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P059-Q1 Why do traffic jams happen?

**观点1：** Many people travel at similar times. **解释1：** Road space becomes insufficient when demand rises together. **例子1：** For example, commuting hours can bring large numbers of cars into one area.

**观点2：** Limited alternatives can make the problem worse. **解释2：** People may drive because other options are inconvenient. **例子2：** For instance, a poor connection can make public transport difficult to use.

#### P059-Q2 How can cities reduce traffic congestion?

**观点1：** They can improve practical public transport options. **解释1：** People are more willing to change when alternatives are reliable. **例子1：** For example, frequent connections make commuting easier to plan.

**观点2：** They can also reduce the need for identical travel times. **解释2：** Flexible routines can spread demand. **例子2：** For instance, some workplaces may allow different starting hours where the job permits.

#### P059-Q3 Why do people enjoy travelling by car?

**观点1：** Cars offer flexibility about routes and stops. **解释1：** Travellers can adapt the journey to their needs. **例子1：** For example, a family can pause when someone needs a break.

**观点2：** They also make luggage easier to manage. **解释2：** People can carry items for different activities. **例子2：** For instance, a seaside trip may involve food, sports equipment and several bags.

#### P059-Q4 What disadvantages do car journeys have?

**观点1：** Traffic can make arrival times unpredictable. **解释1：** A short route may still involve a long delay. **例子1：** For example, travelling across a city at dinner time can be frustrating.

**观点2：** Driving also requires continuous attention. **解释2：** The driver cannot relax in the same way as passengers. **例子2：** For instance, a long trip needs sensible breaks and a realistic schedule.

#### P059-Q5 Should people plan journeys carefully?

**观点1：** Yes, basic planning prevents avoidable difficulties. **解释1：** Travellers need to understand routes and timing. **例子1：** For example, checking transport options can reduce the risk of arriving late.

**观点2：** However, plans should leave flexibility. **解释2：** Unexpected changes are difficult to predict fully. **例子2：** For instance, allowing spare time makes a delayed connection less stressful.

#### P059-Q6 Are short trips as valuable as long holidays?

**观点1：** They can be valuable because they fit ordinary routines. **解释1：** People may not have time for an extended break. **例子1：** For example, a nearby park outing can provide a refreshing afternoon.

**观点2：** Longer holidays offer different opportunities. **解释2：** Travellers can explore a place more deeply. **例子2：** For instance, several days in a city allow both major sights and relaxed local experiences.


## P060 花费甚少的外出日

截图：第8张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a day out that cost very little.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 原稿公园、朋友与飞盘素材改编。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to describe a simple day out in a local park with friends. We wanted to spend time together but did not feel like paying for an expensive activity.

We chose a park that was easy to reach and brought our own water and snacks. One friend brought a frisbee, so we started with a relaxed game in a suitable open area. We were not keeping a serious score; the main aim was to move around and enjoy ourselves.

Afterwards, we found somewhere to sit and share the food. We talked about work, films and plans for future trips. Nobody seemed to miss having a complicated programme or a restaurant reservation.

The day cost very little because we used a public space and things we already owned. I enjoyed it because it reminded me that a good outing does not need to impress anyone. Having enough time, pleasant surroundings and friends who are comfortable together can be more important than spending money.

I would recommend this kind of simple outing to friends who want to relax on a budget.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：休闲与组织活动；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P060-Q1 Do enjoyable activities need to be expensive?

**观点1：** No, enjoyment often comes from company and attention. **解释1：** A simple activity can create meaningful time together. **例子1：** For example, friends can play frisbee in a park with existing equipment.

**观点2：** However, some activities involve unavoidable costs. **解释2：** Special facilities or travel may need payment. **例子2：** For instance, attending a performance requires supporting the venue and performers.

#### P060-Q2 What makes organising a group activity difficult?

**观点1：** Different schedules can be hard to coordinate. **解释1：** People may have work and family commitments at different times. **例子1：** For example, choosing one free afternoon can require several conversations.

**观点2：** Preferences and budgets may also conflict. **解释2：** An enjoyable plan should consider everyone involved. **例子2：** For instance, an expensive restaurant may exclude a friend with limited spare money.

#### P060-Q3 How can an organiser help everyone enjoy an event?

**观点1：** The organiser can clarify the purpose and basic details. **解释1：** People feel comfortable when they know what to expect. **例子1：** For example, a picnic invitation can explain transport and what to bring.

**观点2：** A flexible atmosphere also helps. **解释2：** An overly packed schedule can create pressure. **例子2：** For instance, leaving unplanned time allows conversation and spontaneous games.

#### P060-Q4 Why do people get up early for special activities?

**观点1：** An unusual experience can make the effort worthwhile. **解释1：** Some moments are only available at a particular time. **例子1：** For example, watching a sunrise requires arriving before daylight.

**观点2：** Preparation can also make the event feel meaningful. **解释2：** People appreciate an experience they have deliberately arranged. **例子2：** For instance, a quiet morning walk may become a memorable part of a trip.

#### P060-Q5 Do younger and older people use leisure time differently?

**观点1：** They may differ in energy and routine. **解释1：** Some younger people seek physically demanding or late activities. **例子1：** For example, friends may choose an evening sports session.

**观点2：** However, interests overlap across ages. **解释2：** Personal preference is more useful than a strict age rule. **例子2：** For instance, both young and older people can enjoy gardening or a museum visit.

#### P060-Q6 Why is free time important?

**观点1：** Free time gives people room to recover. **解释1：** A routine made entirely of responsibilities can feel exhausting. **例子1：** For example, reading after work can create a welcome mental break.

**观点2：** It also supports relationships and identity. **解释2：** People need activities beyond their professional role. **例子2：** For instance, a regular team game provides friendship and a sense of belonging.


## P061 组织快乐活动

截图：第8张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an enjoyable activity you organised.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 原稿公园、朋友与飞盘素材改编；调整叙述角度。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to describe a park outing that I organised for my friends. I suggested a simple plan because we wanted time together without paying for an expensive activity.

I chose an accessible park, checked everyone was free and asked them to bring water and snacks. One friend brought a frisbee, so we started with a relaxed game in a suitable open area. We were not keeping a serious score; the main aim was to move around and enjoy ourselves.

Afterwards, we found somewhere to sit and share the food. We talked about work, films and plans for future trips. Nobody seemed to miss having a complicated programme or a restaurant reservation.

The day cost very little because we used a public space and things we already owned. I enjoyed it because it reminded me that a good outing does not need to impress anyone. Having enough time, pleasant surroundings and friends who are comfortable together can be more important than spending money.

I was pleased that a little preparation had helped everyone enjoy an easy and affordable afternoon together.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：休闲与组织活动；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

**观点1：** They may differ in energy and routine. **解释1：** Some younger people seek physically demanding or late activities. **例子1：** For example, friends may choose an evening sports session.

**观点2：** However, interests overlap across ages. **解释2：** Personal preference is more useful than a strict age rule. **例子2：** For instance, both young and older people can enjoy gardening or a museum visit.

#### P061-Q6 Why is free time important?

**观点1：** Free time gives people room to recover. **解释1：** A routine made entirely of responsibilities can feel exhausting. **例子1：** For example, reading after work can create a welcome mental break.

**观点2：** It also supports relationships and identity. **解释2：** People need activities beyond their professional role. **例子2：** For instance, a regular team game provides friendship and a sense of belonging.


## P062 收到特殊蛋糕

截图：第8张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a special cake you received.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体对象；怎样接触或获得；外观、内容或用途；它为什么对你重要。

**答案来源：** 旧稿生日全家照片素材改编；蛋糕装饰补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a birthday cake my family prepared for me last year. It was a simple cake with fruit on top, but a small personal detail made it special.

They had added a decoration that looked like a frisbee because they knew how much I enjoyed the sport. It was not a perfect copy, but I immediately understood the idea and started laughing. The effort showed that they had thought about my interests.

We put the cake in the middle of the table and took a family photograph before cutting it. Everyone was smiling, and for a while nobody was thinking about work or other responsibilities. We simply enjoyed being together and sharing something sweet.

I do not remember the exact flavour as clearly as the atmosphere. The cake was special because it belonged to a warm family moment. Whenever I look at the photograph, I remember feeling understood and cared for, which matters much more to me than how expensive the cake was.

That is why this simple cake remains such a warm memory for me.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：食物与庆祝；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P062-Q1 Why is food important at celebrations?

**观点1：** Food gives people a shared activity. **解释1：** Sitting together creates time for conversation. **例子1：** For example, a family meal can reconnect relatives after a busy period.

**观点2：** It also carries personal or cultural meaning. **解释2：** Certain dishes become connected with particular occasions. **例子2：** For instance, a birthday cake can symbolise care and attention from family members.

#### P062-Q2 Why do people spend more on special meals?

**观点1：** They want the occasion to feel different from daily life. **解释1：** Extra care can express appreciation. **例子1：** For example, a family may choose favourite ingredients for a reunion.

**观点2：** They may also value the shared experience. **解释2：** The memory can seem worth more than the price alone. **例子2：** For instance, a holiday seafood dinner can become a trip's highlight.

#### P062-Q3 Is home cooking better than eating out?

**观点1：** Home cooking gives people control over the meal. **解释1：** They can adjust ingredients and portions to their preferences. **例子1：** For example, a family can prepare familiar dishes for a celebration.

**观点2：** Eating out offers convenience and variety. **解释2：** It removes some preparation and cleaning tasks. **例子2：** For instance, busy friends may meet at a restaurant after work.

#### P062-Q4 Why are some people less interested in cooking?

**观点1：** Time pressure can make preparation feel difficult. **解释1：** Shopping, cooking and cleaning compete with other responsibilities. **例子1：** For example, a late-working employee may choose a quick alternative.

**观点2：** Some people also lack confidence. **解释2：** They may not know how to begin with simple meals. **例子2：** For instance, clear beginner recipes could make cooking feel more manageable.

#### P062-Q5 Should people use phones during shared meals?

**观点1：** Usually, limiting phone use improves conversation. **解释1：** Constant checking divides attention between the table and other activities. **例子1：** For example, a family celebration feels warmer when everyone participates.

**观点2：** However, brief necessary use can be reasonable. **解释2：** A strict rule may ignore practical needs. **例子2：** For instance, someone may need to confirm travel arrangements before returning to the conversation.

#### P062-Q6 Can traditional food remain popular with young people?

**观点1：** Yes, familiar food can connect young people with family memories. **解释1：** Its appeal goes beyond the ingredients. **例子1：** For example, a pastry may remind someone of a childhood celebration.

**观点2：** Traditions can also adapt in presentation. **解释2：** Convenient options can fit modern routines. **例子2：** For instance, a small portion may suit someone who wants to try a traditional speciality.


## P063 语言学习

截图：第8张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a time when you first talked in a foreign language

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P41原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, I'd like to share my past experience with you.

It dates back to a few years ago. I was first introduced to a foreigner from England when I was buying some sports equipment in a physical store. I wanted to buy frisbee equipment, and the owner gave me some suggestions.

Then we chatted with each other for a while, which was the first time I talked with a foreigner in English. Although there were some grammar mistakes and my spoken English was not that fluent, we still understood each other completely.

Gradually, I was not nervous anymore. Instead, I started to enjoy the conversation. This experience gave me confidence and encouraged me to keep improving my spoken English.

What made the experience important was that the conversation had a real purpose. I wanted to understand the equipment, so I stopped worrying about sounding impressive and focused on asking clear questions. The owner responded patiently, which helped me realise that imperfect English can still be useful and that confidence grows through actually using the language with other people.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：语言学习；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P063-Q1 Why do people learn foreign languages?

**观点1：** They want to communicate with more people. **解释1：** A shared language makes practical exchanges easier. **例子1：** For example, a traveller can ask a shopkeeper about equipment.

**观点2：** Languages also provide access to learning resources. **解释2：** Useful information may not exist in a familiar language. **例子2：** For instance, a worker may read a technical explanation from another country.

#### P063-Q2 What helps people become confident speakers?

**观点1：** Regular meaningful practice builds confidence. **解释1：** People discover that communication is possible despite mistakes. **例子1：** For example, asking a real question in a shop can feel rewarding.

**观点2：** Supportive feedback is also important. **解释2：** Learners need specific guidance rather than embarrassment. **例子2：** For instance, a partner can clarify one unclear phrase after the speaker finishes.

#### P063-Q3 Is living abroad necessary to learn a language?

**观点1：** No, consistent practice can happen at home. **解释1：** Learners can use recordings, books and conversations. **例子1：** For example, a daily speaking partner can create regular opportunities.

**观点2：** However, living abroad may increase practical use. **解释2：** Everyday tasks provide repeated reasons to communicate. **例子2：** For instance, arranging transport or shopping can turn learning into an immediate need.

#### P063-Q4 Should children start learning languages early?

**观点1：** Early exposure can make a language feel familiar. **解释1：** Children can explore sounds and expressions through enjoyable activities. **例子1：** For example, simple songs can introduce useful words.

**观点2：** However, the experience should remain appropriate and encouraging. **解释2：** Pressure can reduce curiosity. **例子2：** For instance, a short game may be more suitable than a long formal test.

#### P063-Q5 Is accuracy more important than fluency?

**观点1：** Accuracy matters when unclear language changes meaning. **解释1：** Some situations require precise information. **例子1：** For example, a work instruction should communicate the correct deadline.

**观点2：** Fluency also matters because conversation needs to continue. **解释2：** Excessive hesitation can interrupt the exchange. **例子2：** For instance, a beginner can use simple sentences to ask for help successfully.

#### P063-Q6 Can technology improve language learning?

**观点1：** It provides convenient access to practice materials. **解释1：** Learners can repeat audio and organise useful expressions. **例子1：** For example, recording an answer helps someone notice hesitation.

**观点2：** However, active use remains necessary. **解释2：** Collecting resources alone does not develop communication. **例子2：** For instance, a learner should try a new phrase in conversation rather than only save it.


## P064 早起经历

截图：第8张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a time when you got up very early.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P35原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe getting up extremely early to watch the sunrise on Mount Tai. My friend and I wanted to reach a suitable viewing point before the sky became bright.

It dates back a couple of years ago. My friend highly recommended that I go to Mount Tai to watch an amazing sunrise. So we went there for a weekend getaway.

It took us around two or three hours to reach the top. It was chilly and windy up there. We waited in a designated viewing area and put on extra layers to keep warm.

During the wait, we felt tired and a little stressed, but everything paid off when the sun finally rose from the clouds. In the tranquil surroundings, the sunrise looked like a magnificent traditional Chinese painting.

At that moment, I realized the importance of environmental protection, because I hope such beautiful scenery can be preserved for the next generation.

I would prepare warmer clothes and a more comfortable waiting plan next time, but I would still be willing to wake early for that view.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：休闲与组织活动；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P064-Q1 Do enjoyable activities need to be expensive?

**观点1：** No, enjoyment often comes from company and attention. **解释1：** A simple activity can create meaningful time together. **例子1：** For example, friends can play frisbee in a park with existing equipment.

**观点2：** However, some activities involve unavoidable costs. **解释2：** Special facilities or travel may need payment. **例子2：** For instance, attending a performance requires supporting the venue and performers.

#### P064-Q2 What makes organising a group activity difficult?

**观点1：** Different schedules can be hard to coordinate. **解释1：** People may have work and family commitments at different times. **例子1：** For example, choosing one free afternoon can require several conversations.

**观点2：** Preferences and budgets may also conflict. **解释2：** An enjoyable plan should consider everyone involved. **例子2：** For instance, an expensive restaurant may exclude a friend with limited spare money.

#### P064-Q3 How can an organiser help everyone enjoy an event?

**观点1：** The organiser can clarify the purpose and basic details. **解释1：** People feel comfortable when they know what to expect. **例子1：** For example, a picnic invitation can explain transport and what to bring.

**观点2：** A flexible atmosphere also helps. **解释2：** An overly packed schedule can create pressure. **例子2：** For instance, leaving unplanned time allows conversation and spontaneous games.

#### P064-Q4 Why do people get up early for special activities?

**观点1：** An unusual experience can make the effort worthwhile. **解释1：** Some moments are only available at a particular time. **例子1：** For example, watching a sunrise requires arriving before daylight.

**观点2：** Preparation can also make the event feel meaningful. **解释2：** People appreciate an experience they have deliberately arranged. **例子2：** For instance, a quiet morning walk may become a memorable part of a trip.

#### P064-Q5 Do younger and older people use leisure time differently?

**观点1：** They may differ in energy and routine. **解释1：** Some younger people seek physically demanding or late activities. **例子1：** For example, friends may choose an evening sports session.

**观点2：** However, interests overlap across ages. **解释2：** Personal preference is more useful than a strict age rule. **例子2：** For instance, both young and older people can enjoy gardening or a museum visit.

#### P064-Q6 Why is free time important?

**观点1：** Free time gives people room to recover. **解释1：** A routine made entirely of responsibilities can feel exhausting. **例子1：** For example, reading after work can create a welcome mental break.

**观点2：** It also supports relationships and identity. **解释2：** People need activities beyond their professional role. **例子2：** For instance, a regular team game provides friendship and a sense of belonging.


## P065 当地新闻

截图：第8张，显示共5个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a piece of local news that interested you.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体对象；怎样接触或获得；外观、内容或用途；它为什么对你重要。

**答案来源：** 新编练习情节：社区公园改造通知，未经现实新闻核实。
示范提醒：采访、广告或社区通知的具体情节为练习补写，未确认某次真实节目、广告或新闻事件。


### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a local news item about improvements to a neighbourhood park. I saw the announcement in a community information group while checking messages one evening.

The item explained that some paths and seating areas would be repaired. It also invited residents to comment on how the space could be more useful. I paid attention because I often go to the park after work and had noticed a few areas that were uncomfortable to use.

What interested me was that the news connected directly with my daily routine. It was not a distant event that I could only read about. I could imagine how clearer paths and better seating might change an ordinary evening walk.

I shared the message with a neighbour and we discussed what we would suggest. The item reminded me that local news does not need to be dramatic to matter. Small changes to a familiar public place can affect many people's everyday lives and encourage them to participate.

It also made me more willing to read community updates instead of ignoring them automatically.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：当地新闻与信息判断；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P065-Q1 Why is local news useful?

**观点1：** Local news can affect immediate decisions. **解释1：** It tells residents about changes near their homes. **例子1：** For example, a park repair notice can alter an evening walking route.

**观点2：** It also creates opportunities for participation. **解释2：** People can respond when they understand what is proposed. **例子2：** For instance, a community announcement may invite suggestions about public facilities.

#### P065-Q2 Do people prefer local or international news?

**观点1：** Local news often feels directly relevant. **解释1：** It connects with familiar places and daily routines. **例子1：** For example, transport changes can matter immediately to commuters.

**观点2：** International news offers a wider perspective. **解释2：** Events elsewhere can help people understand broader issues. **例子2：** For instance, a technology story may interest workers even when the company is overseas.

#### P065-Q3 How do people decide whether news is reliable?

**观点1：** They should examine the source and supporting details. **解释1：** A confident statement alone is not enough. **例子1：** For example, a public notice should identify the organisation responsible.

**观点2：** They can also compare independent accounts. **解释2：** Agreement on evidence is more useful than repeated rumours. **例子2：** For instance, checking an original announcement can clarify a misleading social-media summary.

#### P065-Q4 Why is negative news often noticeable?

**观点1：** Negative events can signal an urgent problem. **解释1：** People pay attention to information that may affect their decisions. **例子1：** For example, a transport disruption matters before a journey.

**观点2：** Emotional presentation also attracts attention. **解释2：** Surprising or worrying stories encourage immediate reactions. **例子2：** For instance, a dramatic headline may spread faster than an ordinary community update.

#### P065-Q5 Should schools teach students how to read news critically?

**观点1：** Yes, students need to separate evidence from opinion. **解释1：** Online information often mixes both. **例子1：** For example, a class can identify which parts of a report are directly supported.

**观点2：** They should also learn to recognise missing context. **解释2：** A short clip may not explain the whole event. **例子2：** For instance, comparing a headline with the full announcement can reveal an important qualification.

#### P065-Q6 Can small local changes be newsworthy?

**观点1：** Yes, ordinary changes can affect many residents. **解释1：** A story does not need drama to be useful. **例子1：** For example, improved seating can matter to regular park visitors.

**观点2：** Small reports can also strengthen community awareness. **解释2：** People notice that shared spaces involve shared decisions. **例子2：** For instance, discussing a library's opening arrangements can encourage local participation.


## P066 想要颁布的环保法律

截图：第8张，显示共5个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an environmental law you would like to introduce.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 规则或法律针对什么问题；你如何了解或想到它；具体怎样实施；你认为它有什么作用。区分现行规则和自己的政策设想。

**答案来源：** 新编政策设想：公园一次性用品减量；不是现行法律；调整叙述角度。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to introduce a rule that reduces unnecessary disposable packaging in public parks. The idea came to me after noticing how much rubbish can remain after a busy weekend.

Under my proposal, large organised events in parks would need a clear waste reduction plan. Food sellers would offer practical reusable options where possible, and visitors would have easy access to clearly labelled collection points. The organisers would also be responsible for cleaning the site properly.

I would not expect a rule to work through penalties alone. People need convenient alternatives and simple explanations. For example, an event could make it easy to return reusable cups instead of leaving visitors unsure what to do with them.

I would support this law because parks should remain pleasant places for people and wildlife. My friend Luwen's environmental activities have shown me how much difference small actions can make. A clear rule could help those actions become a normal part of organising public events.

I would want residents and organisers to discuss the practical details before the proposal was put into effect.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：环保与规则；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P066-Q1 Should environmental protection depend on individuals or governments?

**观点1：** Individuals influence everyday behaviour. **解释1：** Repeated small actions can reduce unnecessary waste. **例子1：** For example, visitors can take their rubbish away after a picnic.

**观点2：** Governments and organisers shape the available choices. **解释2：** People need systems that make responsible action practical. **例子2：** For instance, clear collection points support proper waste sorting.

#### P066-Q2 What makes an environmental rule effective?

**观点1：** The rule should be easy to understand. **解释1：** People need to know exactly what action is expected. **例子1：** For example, a sign can show which items belong in each bin.

**观点2：** It also needs practical support. **解释2：** A demand is difficult to follow without suitable facilities. **例子2：** For instance, reusable cups require an accessible return process.

#### P066-Q3 Is education more useful than penalties for environmental protection?

**观点1：** Education helps people understand the purpose of a rule. **解释1：** Understanding can support long-term cooperation. **例子1：** For example, showing how litter affects a park can encourage considerate behaviour.

**观点2：** However, consequences may still be needed for repeated disregard. **解释2：** Otherwise responsible users may feel unfairly burdened. **例子2：** For instance, organisers should remain accountable for cleaning up after an event.

#### P066-Q4 How can businesses reduce unnecessary waste?

**观点1：** They can reconsider packaging and routine materials. **解释1：** Small changes repeated across many sales can matter. **例子1：** For example, a shop can avoid adding an unwanted disposable bag.

**观点2：** They can also make repair or reuse practical. **解释2：** Products need not be discarded after a minor problem. **例子2：** For instance, a store can help customers replace a damaged part.

#### P066-Q5 Should economic development take priority over nature?

**观点1：** Development matters because people need work and services. **解释1：** Ignoring livelihoods can make environmental proposals difficult to support. **例子1：** For example, a community may depend on visitor income.

**观点2：** However, damaged surroundings can weaken long-term wellbeing. **解释2：** Growth should account for the resources it uses. **例子2：** For instance, tourism loses value if the attraction becomes polluted or unpleasant.

#### P066-Q6 How can schools encourage environmental responsibility?

**观点1：** They can connect lessons with practical routines. **解释1：** Children understand responsibility better through repeated actions. **例子1：** For example, sorting classroom waste makes the idea concrete.

**观点2：** They can also organise thoughtful outdoor activities. **解释2：** Direct experience creates a stronger connection with nature. **例子2：** For instance, a supervised park visit can include observing wildlife and discussing litter.


## P067 喜欢拜访但不想住的家

截图：第8张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a home you enjoy visiting but would not want to live in.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P51四合院素材改编；拜访情节补写。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a traditional courtyard home that I visited during a trip to Beijing. I enjoyed spending time there, but I would not choose it for my current everyday life.

The rooms faced a shared courtyard with plants and a few chairs. Behind the entrance, a screen wall created a sense of privacy. Compared with the apartment buildings I usually see, the layout felt peaceful and full of character.

During the visit, I sat in the courtyard and talked with the person showing us around. It was easy to imagine family members gathering there in the evening. I also liked the connection between the indoor rooms and the small outdoor space.

However, living there would involve practical questions about maintenance, comfort and commuting. My present routine depends on convenient facilities and a suitable working space. I would happily visit again to enjoy the atmosphere, but I would need to think much more carefully before choosing that kind of home permanently.

For me, appreciating a traditional design and choosing a practical permanent home are two slightly different decisions.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：建筑与居住；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P067-Q1 Why do people enjoy visiting traditional buildings?

**观点1：** Traditional buildings make history visible. **解释1：** Visitors can see how earlier generations organised daily life. **例子1：** For example, a courtyard suggests how a family shared outdoor space.

**观点2：** They also provide visual variety. **解释2：** Distinctive designs make a place memorable. **例子2：** For instance, older houses can give a neighbourhood character beside modern offices.

#### P067-Q2 Should cities preserve old buildings?

**观点1：** Yes, valuable buildings can protect local identity. **解释1：** Once demolished, an original setting cannot easily be recreated. **例子1：** For example, a historic street can show a city's development.

**观点2：** However, preservation needs practical maintenance. **解释2：** A neglected building cannot serve visitors or residents well. **例子2：** For instance, repairs may improve safety while retaining important design features.

#### P067-Q3 What makes a home comfortable?

**观点1：** A practical layout supports everyday activities. **解释1：** People need suitable space for rest, work and storage. **例子1：** For example, a quiet corner can separate study from family noise.

**观点2：** Personal connections also create comfort. **解释2：** Familiar objects make a space feel like home. **例子2：** For instance, family photographs can make a simple room welcoming.

#### P067-Q4 Are tall buildings always suitable for city life?

**观点1：** Tall buildings can use limited land efficiently. **解释1：** They place many homes or workplaces in a compact area. **例子1：** For example, an office tower can accommodate several companies.

**观点2：** However, daily access needs careful planning. **解释2：** Crowded lifts and surrounding traffic can create inconvenience. **例子2：** For instance, many people leaving at once may face long waits.

#### P067-Q5 Should a building's appearance matter more than its function?

**观点1：** Function should usually come first. **解释1：** People must use the space comfortably every day. **例子1：** For example, an attractive office is frustrating without suitable light or quiet.

**观点2：** However, appearance still affects experience. **解释2：** Thoughtful design can make a place pleasant and distinctive. **例子2：** For instance, a welcoming entrance can improve how visitors feel.

#### P067-Q6 Why might people enjoy visiting a home but dislike living there?

**观点1：** A visit highlights atmosphere and novelty. **解释1：** People can appreciate an unusual setting for a short time. **例子1：** For example, a courtyard may feel peaceful during an afternoon.

**观点2：** Permanent residence involves practical demands. **解释2：** Maintenance, transport and comfort matter throughout the year. **例子2：** For instance, a beautiful house may be inconvenient for a daily commute.


## P068 重要河流/湖泊

截图：第9张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an important river or lake.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 上海素材改编；黄浦江地点信息经官方旅游网站核实。
地点依据：[上海官方旅游网站：外滩与黄浦江](https://www.meet-in-shanghai.net/en/huangpu-district/the-bund-648313/)。个人散步经历按旧稿上海生活素材改编。


### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to talk about the Huangpu River in Shanghai. It is an important part of the city's landscape, and I particularly associate it with walks along the waterfront.

The view brings together water, boats and very different buildings on the two sides. Around the Bund, the riverside is a place where visitors stop to take photographs and local people enjoy walking. The changing light can make the same stretch of river feel different at different times.

For me, its importance is not only visual. A river creates a strong connection between a city's history, daily activity and public space. Even when I am surrounded by buildings, looking across the water gives me a feeling of openness.

I would take a friend there because it is a good place to talk while enjoying the surroundings. I also think keeping the waterfront clean matters. An attractive river is something people can share, so its value depends partly on how carefully the city and its residents look after it.

That shared responsibility is part of what makes the river important to me.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：河流与公共空间；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P068-Q1 Why do people enjoy spending time near water?

**观点1：** Water can create a feeling of openness. **解释1：** It offers a contrast with crowded streets and buildings. **例子1：** For example, a riverside walk can feel refreshing after work.

**观点2：** Waterfronts also provide social and recreational space. **解释2：** People can enjoy simple activities together. **例子2：** For instance, friends may walk and talk while looking across a river.

#### P068-Q2 Why are rivers important to cities?

**观点1：** Rivers can shape a city's history and identity. **解释1：** People associate familiar views and activities with them. **例子1：** For example, Shanghai's waterfront is closely connected with its image.

**观点2：** They also provide shared public space when managed well. **解释2：** Residents need pleasant places outside buildings. **例子2：** For instance, accessible riverside paths can support everyday walking and relaxation.

#### P068-Q3 How can riverside areas attract visitors?

**观点1：** A distinctive view gives visitors a reason to stop. **解释1：** Water and surrounding buildings can create a memorable setting. **例子1：** For example, a skyline across a river can encourage photography.

**观点2：** Convenient access and useful facilities also matter. **解释2：** An attractive view alone may not support a comfortable visit. **例子2：** For instance, clear paths and seating help people stay and explore.

#### P068-Q4 Should cities protect rivers from pollution?

**观点1：** Yes, clean water supports the wider environment. **解释1：** Damage does not remain limited to one attractive viewpoint. **例子1：** For example, waste can affect habitats along a waterway.

**观点2：** Protection also preserves shared enjoyment. **解释2：** Residents and visitors value pleasant public surroundings. **例子2：** For instance, an unpleasant waterfront loses much of its appeal for walking and recreation.

#### P068-Q5 Who should look after public waterfronts?

**观点1：** Public authorities should provide maintenance and clear facilities. **解释1：** Large shared spaces require organised responsibility. **例子1：** For example, regular collection points help manage visitors' waste.

**观点2：** Individual users should also behave considerately. **解释2：** Maintenance cannot compensate for every careless action. **例子2：** For instance, taking picnic rubbish away helps keep a riverside area pleasant for the next visitors.

#### P068-Q6 Is tourism near water always beneficial?

**观点1：** It can support local businesses and public interest. **解释1：** Visitors create reasons to maintain attractive surroundings. **例子1：** For example, a waterfront walk may bring customers to nearby cafes.

**观点2：** However, heavy use can cause crowding and waste. **解释2：** Benefits depend on careful management. **例子2：** For instance, clear routes and suitable collection facilities can reduce pressure during busy periods.


## P069 在成功公司工作的人

截图：第9张，显示共4个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe someone who works for a successful company.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P11 Lily改编；改成独立小糕点店，避免虚构品牌所有权；调整叙述角度。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to talk about my neighbour Lily, who works for a well-established local bakery. I sometimes visit the shop and have noticed the careful service she provides.

The bakery sells traditional pastries and has a group of regular customers. Lily pays attention to small details, such as keeping the counter tidy, explaining ingredients clearly and packing purchases carefully. People seem comfortable asking her for suggestions.

I remember a customer who was unsure what to buy for an older relative. Lily asked about the person's preferences instead of immediately recommending the most expensive box. Her practical advice helped the customer choose something suitable.

I see her employer as successful because people return and trust the service. It may not be a large company, but it appears to have a clear place in the community. Lily's example reminds me that success can come from consistent quality and understanding customers, rather than simply attracting attention or expanding as quickly as possible.

Her work shows me that individual employees can contribute greatly to a company's reputation through ordinary, consistent acts of care.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：经营与服务；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P069-Q1 What helps a small business succeed?

**观点1：** Consistent quality encourages customers to return. **解释1：** People appreciate knowing what they can expect. **例子1：** For example, a bakery benefits when its familiar products remain reliable.

**观点2：** Personal service can also build trust. **解释2：** Understanding customers creates a reason to choose that shop. **例子2：** For instance, thoughtful advice may matter more than an aggressive discount.

#### P069-Q2 Is starting a business easy?

**观点1：** It can be difficult because several responsibilities arrive together. **解释1：** A good product alone does not manage costs or customers. **例子1：** For example, a cafe owner also needs to organise supplies and service.

**观点2：** However, starting on a manageable scale can support learning. **解释2：** Small experiments reveal problems before expansion. **例子2：** For instance, testing a limited menu can clarify customer preferences.

#### P069-Q3 What makes an employee valuable to a company?

**观点1：** Reliability makes cooperation easier. **解释1：** Colleagues need work to be completed with appropriate care. **例子1：** For example, accurate records prevent confusion later.

**观点2：** A helpful attitude also improves the customer's experience. **解释2：** Ordinary interactions shape the company's reputation. **例子2：** For instance, a patient explanation can make a visitor comfortable returning to the shop.

#### P069-Q4 How important is solving problems creatively in business?

**观点1：** It is important because unexpected situations are common. **解释1：** A rigid routine may not provide a suitable answer. **例子1：** For example, a shopkeeper can cooperate with a neighbour to resolve a payment difficulty.

**观点2：** However, solutions must remain responsible. **解释2：** Creativity should not create a larger problem elsewhere. **例子2：** For instance, staff should explain an alternative clearly before a customer accepts it.

#### P069-Q5 Should family members work together in a business?

**观点1：** It can work well when there is trust. **解释1：** Relatives may share a strong commitment to the business. **例子1：** For example, a family bakery may value a long-standing local reputation.

**观点2：** However, roles still need clear boundaries. **解释2：** Personal relationships can make disagreements harder to discuss. **例子2：** For instance, responsibilities should be agreed rather than assumed because of family status.

#### P069-Q6 Should companies focus only on profit?

**观点1：** No, customer trust matters for lasting success. **解释1：** Short-term gains can damage a reputation if service is poor. **例子1：** For example, an unsuitable recommendation may lose a regular customer.

**观点2：** Employees' working conditions also matter. **解释2：** A business depends on people who can perform sustainably. **例子2：** For instance, clear training and reasonable schedules can support reliable service.


## P070 想有空时去旅游的地方

截图：第9张，显示共3个问题。话题检索参考：[当季公开话题目录](https://joespeaking.com/ielts-speaking-questions/2026-sep-dec)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a place you would visit when you have free time.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P56原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

When I have enough free time, I'd like to return to Zhangjiajie National Forest Park in Hunan. I have visited before, but I would enjoy exploring it again at a slower pace.

I was first introduced to this place a few years ago through photos and documentaries. What impressed me most were its unique pillar-like mountains and the wild monkeys there. I do not go there very often because it is quite far from where I live, but it is still one of the quietest and most unforgettable places I have ever been to.

When I was there, I was completely stunned by the scenery. There were all kinds of trees reaching high into the sky, and I could hear birds chirping in the morning. The fresh air and the sea of green trees made me feel calm and peaceful.

In such tranquil surroundings, I felt incredibly relaxed. The whole experience was therapeutic and refreshing, and it also raised my awareness of environmental protection.

I would plan enough time to enjoy the surroundings rather than rushing through a crowded schedule.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：自然与植物；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P070-Q1 Why do people enjoy visiting natural places?

**观点1：** Natural surroundings provide a change from demanding routines. **解释1：** People can slow down and focus on something beyond work. **例子1：** For example, a walk among trees can feel refreshing after computer tasks.

**观点2：** They also offer shared recreation. **解释2：** Friends can spend time together without a complicated programme. **例子2：** For instance, a park visit can combine conversation and gentle exercise.

#### P070-Q2 Should cities provide more green spaces?

**观点1：** Yes, nearby greenery makes recreation more accessible. **解释1：** Residents do not always have time to travel far. **例子1：** For example, a small neighbourhood park can support an evening walk.

**观点2：** Green spaces also improve shared surroundings. **解释2：** They give people pleasant places to meet and rest. **例子2：** For instance, shaded seating can make a busy district more welcoming.

#### P070-Q3 How can people be encouraged to protect nature?

**观点1：** Practical activities make the issue tangible. **解释1：** People see the effect of their own behaviour. **例子1：** For example, collecting litter can reveal how much waste visitors leave.

**观点2：** Clear facilities also support better choices. **解释2：** Good intentions are easier to follow when alternatives are convenient. **例子2：** For instance, visible bins can help visitors dispose of rubbish properly.

#### P070-Q4 Why do some people enjoy growing plants?

**观点1：** Growing plants offers visible progress. **解释1：** Small changes provide a sense of achievement. **例子1：** For example, a new leaf can reward weeks of regular care.

**观点2：** The activity can also create a calming routine. **解释2：** People focus on simple tasks away from screens. **例子2：** For instance, checking a few pots can become a pleasant morning habit.

#### P070-Q5 Should children spend more time outdoors?

**观点1：** Yes, outdoor activity offers different experiences from classroom learning. **解释1：** Children can move, explore and notice living things. **例子1：** For example, a park visit may lead to questions about insects.

**观点2：** However, the activity should suit the setting. **解释2：** Adults need to balance freedom with practical supervision. **例子2：** For instance, a nature walk can use clear boundaries while allowing exploration.

#### P070-Q6 Can tourism harm natural places?

**观点1：** Yes, heavy use can damage the environment. **解释1：** Visitors may leave rubbish or disturb sensitive areas. **例子1：** For example, walking away from paths can affect vegetation.

**观点2：** However, well-managed tourism can support protection. **解释2：** Visitor interest can create reasons to maintain a place carefully. **例子2：** For instance, clear routes and educational signs can guide more considerate behaviour.


## P071 去过且喜欢的城市

截图：第10张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a city you enjoyed visiting

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P50原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The city I'd like to talk about is Qingdao, a gorgeous seaside city in China.

Last year, my family and I went there by car, and it took us about six hours to get there. We stayed there for several days. In summer, Qingdao is distinctive, vibrant and thriving. It has its own unique atmosphere.

What impressed me most was the seafood and the beer festival. The city is famous for its fresh and affordable seafood. We picked out crabs, prawns and abalone, and then had them cooked right away for a small processing fee. After that, we enjoyed Qingdao's world-renowned beer festival, which attracted tourists from all over the world.

I enjoyed visiting it because we were engrossed in a vivid and festive atmosphere. I would certainly enjoy going back when I have time.

Next time, I'd leave more free space in the schedule. I'd like to revisit the market, walk along the coast and enjoy a slower morning with my family. Returning would let us appreciate familiar places without feeling that we had to see everything at once.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：城市与拥挤；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P071-Q1 Why do people move to large cities?

**观点1：** Large cities often offer more professional opportunities. **解释1：** Different organisations create a wider range of possible roles. **例子1：** For example, a data specialist may find more relevant teams there.

**观点2：** They also provide varied services and activities. **解释2：** Residents can explore interests beyond work. **例子2：** For instance, someone may join a sports club or attend cultural events.

#### P071-Q2 What difficulties do city residents face?

**观点1：** High costs can create pressure. **解释1：** Housing and daily expenses may absorb a large part of income. **例子1：** For example, a young worker may need to share accommodation.

**观点2：** Crowded transport can also be tiring. **解释2：** A short distance may still take considerable time. **例子2：** For instance, rush-hour traffic can make an ordinary commute stressful.

#### P071-Q3 Why do tourists return to the same city?

**观点1：** Familiarity can make a trip more relaxed. **解释1：** Visitors already know some places they enjoy. **例子1：** For example, a family may return to a favourite seafood market.

**观点2：** A second visit also allows deeper exploration. **解释2：** The first trip may have covered only major attractions. **例子2：** For instance, returning visitors can spend time in quieter neighbourhoods.

#### P071-Q4 How can cities become more pleasant to live in?

**观点1：** Convenient transport can reduce everyday stress. **解释1：** Residents need practical alternatives to crowded roads. **例子1：** For example, a reliable connection can make commuting more predictable.

**观点2：** Accessible public spaces are also important. **解释2：** People need places to rest and meet outside home. **例子2：** For instance, a nearby park gives workers a simple evening break.

#### P071-Q5 Should tourists visit famous attractions or ordinary neighbourhoods?

**观点1：** Famous attractions can provide an introduction to a city. **解释1：** They often reveal distinctive scenery or history. **例子1：** For example, a waterfront may show important parts of the skyline.

**观点2：** Ordinary neighbourhoods offer a different perspective. **解释2：** Visitors can see how local people use the city. **例子2：** For instance, a market visit can reveal everyday food and conversation.

#### P071-Q6 Are crowded places always unpleasant?

**观点1：** No, crowds can create a lively atmosphere. **解释1：** Shared excitement can make an event feel special. **例子1：** For example, spectators cheering together can enrich a festival or match.

**观点2：** However, crowding becomes unpleasant when it prevents basic comfort. **解释2：** People need space, clear routes and reasonable access. **例子2：** For instance, a packed station feels stressful when someone must catch a train.


## P072 爱护自然之人

截图：第10张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a person who likes to look after the natural world

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P02原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

Well, for this topic, I'd like to describe a guy named Luwen. We became friends through university sport, and I later learned more about his interest in environmental activities.

To be honest, I was deeply impressed by how much he cares about the environment. He is the kind of person who often takes action instead of just talking about environmental protection. For example, he sometimes joins environmental activities such as picking up litter in parks and riding eco-friendly bikes. He encourages people around him to save water, reduce car use, and avoid wasting resources in daily life.

Apart from that, he participates in Earth Hour by switching off lights to raise awareness of climate change. I admire him because he is sensible and positive, and I've learned a lot from him.

What I appreciate most is that his suggestions are practical. He does not expect everyone to change their entire lifestyle immediately. Instead, he encourages a manageable action and sets an example himself. That makes me more willing to participate because environmental protection feels like something I can actually do.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：环保与规则；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P072-Q1 Should environmental protection depend on individuals or governments?

**观点1：** Individuals influence everyday behaviour. **解释1：** Repeated small actions can reduce unnecessary waste. **例子1：** For example, visitors can take their rubbish away after a picnic.

**观点2：** Governments and organisers shape the available choices. **解释2：** People need systems that make responsible action practical. **例子2：** For instance, clear collection points support proper waste sorting.

#### P072-Q2 What makes an environmental rule effective?

**观点1：** The rule should be easy to understand. **解释1：** People need to know exactly what action is expected. **例子1：** For example, a sign can show which items belong in each bin.

**观点2：** It also needs practical support. **解释2：** A demand is difficult to follow without suitable facilities. **例子2：** For instance, reusable cups require an accessible return process.

#### P072-Q3 Is education more useful than penalties for environmental protection?

**观点1：** Education helps people understand the purpose of a rule. **解释1：** Understanding can support long-term cooperation. **例子1：** For example, showing how litter affects a park can encourage considerate behaviour.

**观点2：** However, consequences may still be needed for repeated disregard. **解释2：** Otherwise responsible users may feel unfairly burdened. **例子2：** For instance, organisers should remain accountable for cleaning up after an event.

#### P072-Q4 How can businesses reduce unnecessary waste?

**观点1：** They can reconsider packaging and routine materials. **解释1：** Small changes repeated across many sales can matter. **例子1：** For example, a shop can avoid adding an unwanted disposable bag.

**观点2：** They can also make repair or reuse practical. **解释2：** Products need not be discarded after a minor problem. **例子2：** For instance, a store can help customers replace a damaged part.

#### P072-Q5 Should economic development take priority over nature?

**观点1：** Development matters because people need work and services. **解释1：** Ignoring livelihoods can make environmental proposals difficult to support. **例子1：** For example, a community may depend on visitor income.

**观点2：** However, damaged surroundings can weaken long-term wellbeing. **解释2：** Growth should account for the resources it uses. **例子2：** For instance, tourism loses value if the attraction becomes polluted or unpleasant.

#### P072-Q6 How can schools encourage environmental responsibility?

**观点1：** They can connect lessons with practical routines. **解释1：** Children understand responsibility better through repeated actions. **例子1：** For example, sorting classroom waste makes the idea concrete.

**观点2：** They can also organise thoughtful outdoor activities. **解释2：** Direct experience creates a stronger connection with nature. **例子2：** For instance, a supervised park visit can include observing wildlife and discussing litter.


## P073 想从事的短期海外工作

截图：第10张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a short-term job you would like to do abroad.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 想做什么工作；在哪种环境工作（海外题说明国家与期限）；需要哪些准备；为什么想做。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P14修订：补足国家、期限与岗位，不用泛泛理想工作代答。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to take a short research assistant role in an artificial intelligence team in Singapore. Ideally, it would last a few months, so I could experience working abroad without immediately making a permanent move.

My current work involves data, and I would like to build on those skills in a different environment. I imagine helping with data preparation, testing ideas and discussing results with colleagues. The specific project would matter more to me than the name of the employer.

Before applying, I would need to improve my English and make sure my technical skills matched the role. I would also prepare examples of work that showed how I approach practical problems.

The main attraction would be learning through daily cooperation with people who have different experiences. Outside work, I could explore the city and become more independent. Even if the role were temporary, I think it could give me a clearer understanding of the career direction and working environment that suit me best.

I would see the experience as a chance to learn, rather than simply an overseas holiday.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：工作与目标；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P073-Q1 What makes a job attractive?

**观点1：** Interesting work can provide a sense of progress. **解释1：** People enjoy using and developing skills they value. **例子1：** For example, a data scientist may appreciate solving an unfamiliar problem.

**观点2：** Working conditions are equally important. **解释2：** A meaningful role still needs a sustainable routine. **例子2：** For instance, predictable hours can help someone maintain family relationships and hobbies.

#### P073-Q2 Is salary more important than job satisfaction?

**观点1：** Salary matters because people must meet practical needs. **解释1：** Financial pressure can make even interesting work difficult. **例子1：** For example, a worker needs to cover housing and normal expenses.

**观点2：** However, satisfaction affects daily motivation. **解释2：** A role that constantly feels pointless can become exhausting. **例子2：** For instance, useful projects may matter more than a small increase in pay.

#### P073-Q3 Why do people want to work abroad?

**观点1：** They may want to learn through a different environment. **解释1：** New colleagues and routines can challenge familiar assumptions. **例子1：** For example, a temporary research role may introduce another approach to teamwork.

**观点2：** They may also seek personal independence. **解释2：** Living elsewhere involves everyday decisions outside a familiar setting. **例子2：** For instance, arranging transport and accommodation can build confidence.

#### P073-Q4 Is temporary overseas work useful?

**观点1：** Yes, it allows people to explore a working environment. **解释1：** They can learn before considering a permanent move. **例子1：** For example, a short project may clarify which team culture suits them.

**观点2：** It can also create transferable experience. **解释2：** The value may remain after returning home. **例子2：** For instance, clearer communication habits can improve cooperation in later jobs.

#### P073-Q5 What skills will help people adapt at work?

**观点1：** A willingness to learn is essential. **解释1：** Tools and responsibilities can change during a career. **例子1：** For example, a worker may need to understand unfamiliar software.

**观点2：** Clear communication is also useful. **解释2：** Adaptation often depends on asking questions and coordinating with others. **例子2：** For instance, explaining uncertainty early can prevent a project from moving in the wrong direction.

#### P073-Q6 Should employers offer flexible working arrangements?

**观点1：** Where practical, flexibility can help people manage responsibilities. **解释1：** Different tasks and personal situations require different routines. **例子1：** For example, focused writing may suit a quiet home setting.

**观点2：** However, teams still need reliable coordination. **解释2：** Flexibility should not leave colleagues unsure about availability. **例子2：** For instance, agreed meeting times can preserve cooperation while allowing varied working hours.


## P074 给别人建议

截图：第10张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a time when you gave advice to others

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P30原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, I'd like to talk about a guy named Luwen. We crossed paths when I joined the university frisbee team, where he was the captain.

A couple of months ago, he was preparing for the CFA test and attending club training. In that case, he suffered from a tight schedule and always struggled with sleep. So I recommended meditation to him.

In the beginning, he didn't want to try it because he thought meditation was boring and even time-wasting. He was distracted by his smartphone. But finally, he stuck to it for a short time. He found it could help him calm his mind and regain inner peace.

Although he has finished the CFA test, he still sticks to doing meditation. It has become part of his daily routine.

I tried to explain the suggestion without making him feel judged. We talked about what would fit his schedule, and I offered to practise with him at first. I think that friendly support mattered because he could explore the idea without feeling that someone was ordering him to change.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：建议与鼓励；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P074-Q1 What makes advice useful?

**观点1：** Useful advice matches the person's actual situation. **解释1：** A suggestion should fit their time, resources and goals. **例子1：** For example, a short routine may suit a busy learner better than an ambitious schedule.

**观点2：** It should also explain the reasoning. **解释2：** People need to understand why an approach may help. **例子2：** For instance, dividing tasks can make a large goal less overwhelming.

#### P074-Q2 Why do people reject good advice?

**观点1：** They may feel judged or controlled. **解释1：** Even a practical suggestion can sound critical. **例子1：** For example, telling a tired friend to organise life better may cause defensiveness.

**观点2：** They may also doubt that the advice fits them. **解释2：** Past experience can affect willingness to try. **例子2：** For instance, a difficult previous routine may make someone sceptical about another plan.

#### P074-Q3 Should people give advice without being asked?

**观点1：** Sometimes a gentle offer is appropriate. **解释1：** A person may not know that help is available. **例子1：** For example, a teammate can offer support to a struggling beginner.

**观点2：** However, boundaries should be respected. **解释2：** Unrequested instructions may feel intrusive. **例子2：** For instance, asking whether a friend wants suggestions can make the conversation more comfortable.

#### P074-Q4 Is advice from friends better than professional advice?

**观点1：** Friends understand personal context and preferences. **解释1：** Their support may feel accessible and reassuring. **例子1：** For example, a friend can help choose an enjoyable hobby.

**观点2：** Professionals offer specialised knowledge when the situation requires it. **解释2：** Familiarity does not replace expertise. **例子2：** For instance, a technical problem may need someone trained to assess it properly.

#### P074-Q5 How can people encourage someone without pressuring them?

**观点1：** They can offer a small voluntary first step. **解释1：** A manageable trial feels less demanding than a major commitment. **例子1：** For example, inviting a friend to practise for a few minutes may help.

**观点2：** They can also participate supportively. **解释2：** Shared effort reduces the feeling of being instructed. **例子2：** For instance, trying an activity together can make the beginner more comfortable.

#### P074-Q6 Should children always follow adults' advice?

**观点1：** Children should take experienced guidance seriously. **解释1：** Adults can help them notice practical consequences. **例子1：** For example, preparation advice can make a school trip smoother.

**观点2：** However, children should be allowed to ask questions. **解释2：** Understanding builds judgement rather than simple obedience. **例子2：** For instance, discussing why a rule exists helps a child apply it in another setting.


## P075 乐于助人的人

截图：第10张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a person who often helps others

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P01原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, I'd like to talk about Luwen, one of my closest friends from my university frisbee team.

I first got to know him when I joined the team. He was the captain, and he was always the first person to check in when someone ran into difficulties. For example, when I was new to frisbee, he gave me practical suggestions about throwing and catching, especially the safest way to catch, called the pancake catch. He encouraged other team members when we were falling behind in important matches.

I feel he is helpful, reliable and patient. He is not just a friend, but a mentor to me, because he showed me how to support others with calmness and confidence.

What makes his help especially valuable is that he does not make beginners feel embarrassed. He explains one thing at a time and gives them space to try again. I've seen nervous new players become more comfortable because of his attitude. His example has also encouraged me to offer practical help when I notice someone struggling in a similar situation.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：朋友与人际；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P075-Q1 What makes someone a good friend?

**观点1：** Reliability is important in friendship. **解释1：** People need to know that support is available when it matters. **例子1：** For example, a friend can listen patiently during a difficult week.

**观点2：** Shared enjoyment also keeps the relationship alive. **解释2：** Friendship should include ordinary pleasant experiences. **例子2：** For instance, a regular game gives two people time to laugh together.

#### P075-Q2 Why are some people more popular than others?

**观点1：** Considerate people make others feel comfortable. **解释1：** Listening and including newcomers help people feel welcome. **例子1：** For example, a captain who supports beginners can become well liked.

**观点2：** Visible ability can also attract attention. **解释2：** People often admire skills they hope to develop. **例子2：** For instance, an excellent musician may draw others into a school club.

#### P075-Q3 Is it easy to maintain friendships after leaving school?

**观点1：** It becomes harder because routines separate. **解释1：** Friends may work in different places and have limited free time. **例子1：** For example, a spontaneous meeting may require planning several weeks ahead.

**观点2：** However, regular small contact can preserve a connection. **解释2：** Friendship does not require meeting every day. **例子2：** For instance, a thoughtful message can show continuing interest.

#### P075-Q4 Can people with different interests become close friends?

**观点1：** Yes, shared values can matter more than identical hobbies. **解释1：** People can respect and support each other despite different preferences. **例子1：** For example, a quiet reader and an athlete may both value honesty.

**观点2：** Different interests can also enrich a friendship. **解释2：** Each person introduces the other to unfamiliar experiences. **例子2：** For instance, one friend might suggest a first museum visit.

#### P075-Q5 Why do people want to meet someone again?

**观点1：** An interesting conversation can create curiosity. **解释1：** People want to understand a perspective they have only briefly encountered. **例子1：** For example, a resourceful shop owner may have useful stories about business.

**观点2：** A welcoming manner also encourages further contact. **解释2：** Feeling respected makes a second conversation appealing. **例子2：** For instance, a patient listener can leave a stronger impression than an impressive job title.

#### P075-Q6 Should friends always agree with one another?

**观点1：** No, honest disagreement can be helpful. **解释1：** A friend may notice a problem that someone has overlooked. **例子1：** For example, they can question an unrealistic study plan.

**观点2：** However, disagreement should remain respectful. **解释2：** Criticising the idea is different from insulting the person. **例子2：** For instance, offering a practical alternative keeps the conversation constructive.


## P076 不享受的音乐活动

截图：第10张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an event you attended in which you didn’t enjoy the music played

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P29原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词
