I want to talk about a live gig I went to a couple of months ago at a popular livehouse in the city center.

I went with a close friend of mine who is a huge fan of indie music. He insisted that this new band was the next big thing, so I tagged along. Mainly, I wanted to blow off some steam. My job as a data scientist can be mentally draining, so I was looking forward to some loud and energetic rock music.

Here is the thing: I expected a high-energy rock show, but it turned out to be underwhelming. The lead singer had almost zero stage presence. He just stood there, stared at his shoes and mumbled the lyrics. The music was repetitive and monotonous, and the crowd was just standing there looking bored.

It felt like the band did not even want to be there. I kept waiting for the climax of the show, but it never came. It was a total letdown.

I'd check a performance more carefully before buying tickets for another similar evening.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：影视与视频；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P076-Q1 What makes a film or programme enjoyable?

**观点1：** A convincing story helps people care about the outcome. **解释1：** Characters need motives that viewers can understand. **例子1：** For example, a difficult choice can create more interest than a long action scene.

**观点2：** Presentation also matters. **解释2：** Acting, sound and visual details support the atmosphere. **例子2：** For instance, an unusual office design can strengthen a mysterious television story.

#### P076-Q2 Why do different people enjoy different kinds of entertainment?

**观点1：** People use entertainment to meet different needs. **解释1：** Some want excitement while others want a quiet break. **例子1：** For example, a tired worker may choose a gentle comedy.

**观点2：** Personal experience also shapes taste. **解释2：** Familiar themes can feel more meaningful. **例子2：** For instance, someone who plays sport may enjoy a documentary about teamwork.

#### P076-Q3 Are online videos useful for learning?

**观点1：** They can make a process easy to observe. **解释1：** Visual demonstrations show steps that text may not explain clearly. **例子1：** For example, a learner can watch how to hold a frisbee.

**观点2：** However, quality varies considerably. **解释2：** An attractive presentation does not guarantee a reliable explanation. **例子2：** For instance, viewers should question a dramatic claim without supporting evidence.

#### P076-Q4 Is watching at home better than going to a cinema?

**观点1：** Watching at home offers convenience. **解释1：** People can choose a time that fits their routine. **例子1：** For example, friends can watch together after dinner without travelling.

**观点2：** A cinema provides a more focused experience. **解释2：** The large screen and shared atmosphere can increase involvement. **例子2：** For instance, an impressive visual sequence may feel more powerful there.

#### P076-Q5 Why do people share entertaining videos?

**观点1：** They want to share an enjoyable reaction. **解释1：** A short clip can make someone laugh quickly. **例子1：** For example, friends may send a funny scene during a busy day.

**观点2：** Sharing also expresses personal interests. **解释2：** It gives people a reason to start a conversation. **例子2：** For instance, a science video can lead to discussion among curious colleagues.

#### P076-Q6 Should entertainment always teach people something?

**观点1：** No, relaxation is a valid purpose. **解释1：** People sometimes need a break from demanding tasks. **例子1：** For example, a light comedy can make an ordinary evening pleasant.

**观点2：** However, meaningful ideas can add lasting value. **解释2：** Viewers may continue thinking after the programme ends. **例子2：** For instance, a story about work and identity can inspire a thoughtful conversation.


## P077 朋友自学

截图：第10张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe one of your friends who learned something without a teacher

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P05原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to talk about Luwen, one of my closest friends, who learned music without a teacher.

It dates back to about five years ago, when I was first introduced to him after joining my university music club. He was a passionate rock guitarist and singer. As far as I know, he did not always rely on a teacher. Instead, he learned a lot by watching online videos, listening to live performances and practicing again and again.

He learned music because he had a genuine passion for rock music. I think learning from a teacher might be more efficient, but self-learning also made him more independent and creative. That is why I really admire him.

He told me that he often repeated a short section of a song until it sounded better. When he became stuck, he looked for a different explanation rather than giving up. He also recorded himself so that he could hear mistakes more clearly. I think that combination of curiosity, patient repetition and honest feedback made his independent learning much more effective and enjoyable over time.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：学习与教学；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P077-Q1 What makes a lesson memorable?

**观点1：** Active participation makes a lesson memorable. **解释1：** Doing something gives learners a concrete experience to recall. **例子1：** For example, practising a frisbee catch is clearer than hearing instructions alone.

**观点2：** A supportive teacher also helps. **解释2：** People learn more comfortably when mistakes are treated patiently. **例子2：** For instance, a language teacher can encourage a hesitant student to finish speaking.

#### P077-Q2 Is learning alone better than learning with others?

**观点1：** Learning alone offers control over pace. **解释1：** People can repeat difficult material without holding anyone back. **例子1：** For example, a learner can replay a short coding demonstration.

**观点2：** Group learning provides feedback and motivation. **解释2：** Other people notice problems and suggest different approaches. **例子2：** For instance, a teammate can point out why a catching technique is not working.

#### P077-Q3 What qualities make a good teacher?

**观点1：** A good teacher explains ideas clearly. **解释1：** They connect unfamiliar material with something learners understand. **例子1：** For example, a simple daily example can clarify an abstract concept.

**观点2：** Patience is equally valuable. **解释2：** Different students need different amounts of practice. **例子2：** For instance, a teacher may demonstrate the same movement again without embarrassing a beginner.

#### P077-Q4 Can adults learn new skills as well as children?

**观点1：** Adults can learn effectively through clear goals. **解释1：** They often understand why a skill matters to them. **例子1：** For example, someone learning English for work may practise consistently.

**观点2：** Children may be more willing to experiment. **解释2：** They can explore without worrying as much about appearing inexperienced. **例子2：** For instance, children often try unfamiliar games immediately.

#### P077-Q5 Can online videos replace teachers?

**观点1：** Videos are useful for accessible explanations. **解释1：** Learners can pause and repeat a demonstration whenever needed. **例子1：** For example, a beginner can watch a basic cooking technique several times.

**观点2：** However, videos cannot always provide personal feedback. **解释2：** The learner may repeat an error without noticing it. **例子2：** For instance, a teacher can correct an unclear pronunciation immediately.

#### P077-Q6 Why is practice important when learning?

**观点1：** Practice helps knowledge become usable. **解释1：** Understanding an explanation does not guarantee smooth performance. **例子1：** For example, knowing keyboard positions differs from typing a report accurately.

**观点2：** Practice also reveals specific weaknesses. **解释2：** Learners can then focus their effort more effectively. **例子2：** For instance, recording a speech may show that examples need clearer explanations.


## P078 想拥有的科技产品

截图：第10张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a piece of technology (not a phone) that you would like to own

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体物品；为什么想要；试用、储蓄或购买过程；价格与预算如何影响你的决定。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P13原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, I'd like to talk about a newer VR headset that I'd like to own.

This VR headset has a sleek, matte black body. It looks really modern and cool. It is also pretty handy, meaning it is not too bulky or heavy when I wear it, but at the same time it feels sturdy and well-made.

When I first used it, I was completely shocked by its incredibly immersive visuals. To be honest, I was amazed. It really felt like the characters in movies and games were right there face-to-face, interacting with me. I got quite hooked on it for a while, exploring different virtual worlds and games.

I wanted to own it because it can bring me a brand-new entertainment experience. Although upgrading would be expensive, I would consider it after checking my budget and deciding whether the improvement justified the cost.

Before buying, I would compare comfort, price and how often I would actually use it. I want an upgrade to serve a clear purpose, rather than simply satisfy a brief interest in something new.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：科技与效率；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P078-Q1 How does technology save time?

**观点1：** Technology can handle repetitive steps efficiently. **解释1：** People can spend less time repeating routine actions. **例子1：** For example, software can help organise a large collection of data.

**观点2：** It also reduces the need to travel for simple tasks. **解释2：** Information and communication are available remotely. **例子2：** For instance, a quick online meeting can replace a journey for a short discussion.

#### P078-Q2 Can people become too dependent on technology?

**观点1：** Yes, dependence becomes a problem when basic alternatives disappear. **解释1：** A small failure can interrupt an entire routine. **例子1：** For example, a lost file may stop work without a backup.

**观点2：** However, regular use is not automatically harmful. **解释2：** Useful tools can support rather than replace understanding. **例子2：** For instance, checking a generated draft keeps the user involved in the result.

#### P078-Q3 How should people respond to a technical problem?

**观点1：** They should identify the problem calmly. **解释1：** Random actions can make it harder to understand what happened. **例子1：** For example, checking a saved version may help after a programme freezes.

**观点2：** They should also learn from the incident. **解释2：** A preventive habit can reduce future disruption. **例子2：** For instance, keeping backups makes an unexpected failure less stressful.

#### P078-Q4 Should children learn basic computer skills?

**观点1：** Yes, practical skills support study and everyday tasks. **解释1：** Children need more than familiarity with entertainment apps. **例子1：** For example, organising files helps them manage school projects.

**观点2：** They should also learn to evaluate information. **解释2：** Confidence with a device does not guarantee sound judgement. **例子2：** For instance, a pupil can compare sources before accepting an online claim.

#### P078-Q5 Why do people buy new electronic products?

**观点1：** Some purchases meet a practical need. **解释1：** An existing device may no longer perform required tasks well. **例子1：** For example, a worker may need a more suitable computer.

**观点2：** Other purchases reflect curiosity or social influence. **解释2：** Novelty can feel exciting even without a clear purpose. **例子2：** For instance, an impressive demonstration may tempt someone to exceed a budget.

#### P078-Q6 Will technology replace human judgement?

**观点1：** It may assist with routine decisions. **解释1：** Clear patterns and repeated tasks can be handled consistently. **例子1：** For example, software can flag unusual entries for review.

**观点2：** However, people still need to interpret context and responsibility. **解释2：** A technically plausible suggestion may not suit the real situation. **例子2：** For instance, a worker should check whether a generated solution addresses the actual problem.


## P079 完美工作

截图：第10张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a perfect job you would like to have in the future

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 想做什么工作；在哪种环境工作（海外题说明国家与期限）；需要哪些准备；为什么想做。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P15原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

To kick things off, I'd like to talk about my dream job, which is being an AI scientist.

I first came across this career path through tech news and blogs, especially when companies like DeepSeek started making headlines. To get this job, I need to master advanced mathematics, coding and machine learning algorithms.

The reason why I think it's my perfect job is that it allows me to collaborate with the brightest minds in the industry. Working alongside prestigious researchers gives me access to cutting-edge knowledge and helps me constantly improve myself. I also find it rewarding when we develop AI products that contribute to the public good.

On top of that, I really value flexibility. My ideal setup is a hybrid work model, working two days in the office and three days from home. I'd also look for fair pay, because financial stability matters alongside interesting work.

I know the work would still involve difficult problems and routine tasks. However, being interested in the subject would make those challenges feel worthwhile and encourage me to keep learning from them.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：工作与目标；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P079-Q1 What makes a job attractive?

**观点1：** Interesting work can provide a sense of progress. **解释1：** People enjoy using and developing skills they value. **例子1：** For example, a data scientist may appreciate solving an unfamiliar problem.

**观点2：** Working conditions are equally important. **解释2：** A meaningful role still needs a sustainable routine. **例子2：** For instance, predictable hours can help someone maintain family relationships and hobbies.

#### P079-Q2 Is salary more important than job satisfaction?

**观点1：** Salary matters because people must meet practical needs. **解释1：** Financial pressure can make even interesting work difficult. **例子1：** For example, a worker needs to cover housing and normal expenses.

**观点2：** However, satisfaction affects daily motivation. **解释2：** A role that constantly feels pointless can become exhausting. **例子2：** For instance, useful projects may matter more than a small increase in pay.

#### P079-Q3 Why do people want to work abroad?

**观点1：** They may want to learn through a different environment. **解释1：** New colleagues and routines can challenge familiar assumptions. **例子1：** For example, a temporary research role may introduce another approach to teamwork.

**观点2：** They may also seek personal independence. **解释2：** Living elsewhere involves everyday decisions outside a familiar setting. **例子2：** For instance, arranging transport and accommodation can build confidence.

#### P079-Q4 Is temporary overseas work useful?

**观点1：** Yes, it allows people to explore a working environment. **解释1：** They can learn before considering a permanent move. **例子1：** For example, a short project may clarify which team culture suits them.

**观点2：** It can also create transferable experience. **解释2：** The value may remain after returning home. **例子2：** For instance, clearer communication habits can improve cooperation in later jobs.

#### P079-Q5 What skills will help people adapt at work?

**观点1：** A willingness to learn is essential. **解释1：** Tools and responsibilities can change during a career. **例子1：** For example, a worker may need to understand unfamiliar software.

**观点2：** Clear communication is also useful. **解释2：** Adaptation often depends on asking questions and coordinating with others. **例子2：** For instance, explaining uncertainty early can prevent a project from moving in the wrong direction.

#### P079-Q6 Should employers offer flexible working arrangements?

**观点1：** Where practical, flexibility can help people manage responsibilities. **解释1：** Different tasks and personal situations require different routines. **例子1：** For example, focused writing may suit a quiet home setting.

**观点2：** However, teams still need reliable coordination. **解释2：** Flexibility should not leave colleagues unsure about availability. **例子2：** For instance, agreed meeting times can preserve cooperation while allowing varied working hours.


## P080 喜欢画画的孩子

截图：第10张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a child who loves drawing/painting

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P03原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to talk about a boy named Ming, who was actually my classmate back in primary school. We first crossed paths when I joined the school's art club, where he was our team leader.

Honestly, he just had a natural gift for painting, but what really stood out was his dedication. He always carried a sketchbook and pencils with him wherever he went. Whenever he spotted something unique, like a strangely shaped cloud or a pink sunset, he would capture it on paper instantly.

One memory really sticks with me. During a painting competition, I realized I had forgotten my brushes. Instead of blaming me, Ming stepped up without hesitation and took over the section I was supposed to paint. Thanks to him, our final piece got high praise from the teacher. After that, he led our team to win first place in several other contests, and we became life-long confidants.

I also remember how pleased he looked when another child enjoyed his picture. Sharing that enjoyment seemed just as important to him as receiving praise from an adult.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：手工与创造力；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P080-Q1 Why is creativity useful in daily life?

**观点1：** Creativity helps people use limited resources. **解释1：** They can find another approach instead of immediately spending money. **例子1：** For example, rearranging furniture may improve a small room.

**观点2：** It also helps people respond to unfamiliar problems. **解释2：** A standard solution may not suit every situation. **例子2：** For instance, a shopkeeper may cooperate with a neighbour to solve a payment problem.

#### P080-Q2 Can creativity be taught?

**观点1：** It can be encouraged through practice. **解释1：** Trying different solutions helps people become less afraid of unusual ideas. **例子1：** For example, children can design several objects from the same materials.

**观点2：** A supportive environment also matters. **解释2：** People need room to make mistakes without ridicule. **例子2：** For instance, a teacher can discuss what a failed design still teaches.

#### P080-Q3 Should children learn to make things by hand?

**观点1：** Yes, practical activities develop patience. **解释1：** Children see that a result requires several careful steps. **例子1：** For example, making a card involves planning, cutting and decorating.

**观点2：** They also connect ideas with physical materials. **解释2：** Children discover limits that are hard to understand through pictures alone. **例子2：** For instance, a paper bridge must actually hold its own shape.

#### P080-Q4 Are handmade gifts better than purchased gifts?

**观点1：** Handmade gifts can feel personal. **解释1：** The giver invests time and includes individual details. **例子1：** For example, a card can mention a shared memory.

**观点2：** However, a purchased gift may be more useful. **解释2：** Understanding the recipient matters more than the production method. **例子2：** For instance, suitable sports equipment can support a friend's regular hobby.

#### P080-Q5 Why do people repair old objects?

**观点1：** Repairing can save money and materials. **解释1：** A small damaged part does not always make the whole item useless. **例子1：** For example, replacing a wheel may restore a toy car.

**观点2：** People also want to preserve memories. **解释2：** A replacement cannot carry the same personal history. **例子2：** For instance, a repaired childhood toy may represent a parent's care.

#### P080-Q6 Is imagination important at work?

**观点1：** Yes, imagination helps people consider alternatives. **解释1：** A difficult task may need more than an existing routine. **例子1：** For example, a team can present complex data through a clearer visual explanation.

**观点2：** However, ideas need practical checking. **解释2：** An attractive possibility may fail in real use. **例子2：** For instance, a new design should be tested before everyone depends on it.


## P081 App/程序

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a program or app on your computer or phone

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体对象；怎样接触或获得；外观、内容或用途；它为什么对你重要。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P18原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

First, I'd like to describe an application called Gemini.

As a data scientist, I use AI tools almost every day, but Gemini has become my go-to app. I use it daily because it is a perfect assistant for both my work and personal life.

In the past, my schedule was very tight with coding and reporting. But now, AI helps me draft code and documents, which I then check carefully. Most importantly, I used to spend a lot of time on repetitive tasks like data cleaning. Now I can offload those tasks to AI, so I can focus on more creative work.

In my personal life, it helps me organize my tasks and get my head straight for the day ahead. I've become a bit dependent on it, but honestly, I cannot imagine life without it.

However, I still need to judge its suggestions and test any code before relying on it. I treat it as a useful starting point, not a replacement for understanding my own work. That balance helps me gain convenience while keeping responsibility for the result.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：科技与效率；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P081-Q1 How does technology save time?

**观点1：** Technology can handle repetitive steps efficiently. **解释1：** People can spend less time repeating routine actions. **例子1：** For example, software can help organise a large collection of data.

**观点2：** It also reduces the need to travel for simple tasks. **解释2：** Information and communication are available remotely. **例子2：** For instance, a quick online meeting can replace a journey for a short discussion.

#### P081-Q2 Can people become too dependent on technology?

**观点1：** Yes, dependence becomes a problem when basic alternatives disappear. **解释1：** A small failure can interrupt an entire routine. **例子1：** For example, a lost file may stop work without a backup.

**观点2：** However, regular use is not automatically harmful. **解释2：** Useful tools can support rather than replace understanding. **例子2：** For instance, checking a generated draft keeps the user involved in the result.

#### P081-Q3 How should people respond to a technical problem?

**观点1：** They should identify the problem calmly. **解释1：** Random actions can make it harder to understand what happened. **例子1：** For example, checking a saved version may help after a programme freezes.

**观点2：** They should also learn from the incident. **解释2：** A preventive habit can reduce future disruption. **例子2：** For instance, keeping backups makes an unexpected failure less stressful.

#### P081-Q4 Should children learn basic computer skills?

**观点1：** Yes, practical skills support study and everyday tasks. **解释1：** Children need more than familiarity with entertainment apps. **例子1：** For example, organising files helps them manage school projects.

**观点2：** They should also learn to evaluate information. **解释2：** Confidence with a device does not guarantee sound judgement. **例子2：** For instance, a pupil can compare sources before accepting an online claim.

#### P081-Q5 Why do people buy new electronic products?

**观点1：** Some purchases meet a practical need. **解释1：** An existing device may no longer perform required tasks well. **例子1：** For example, a worker may need a more suitable computer.

**观点2：** Other purchases reflect curiosity or social influence. **解释2：** Novelty can feel exciting even without a clear purpose. **例子2：** For instance, an impressive demonstration may tempt someone to exceed a budget.

#### P081-Q6 Will technology replace human judgement?

**观点1：** It may assist with routine decisions. **解释1：** Clear patterns and repeated tasks can be handled consistently. **例子1：** For example, software can flag unusual entries for review.

**观点2：** However, people still need to interpret context and responsibility. **解释2：** A technically plausible suggestion may not suit the real situation. **例子2：** For instance, a worker should check whether a generated solution addresses the actual problem.


## P082 擅长做计划的人

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a person who makes plans a lot and is good at planning

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P06原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, I'd like to talk about Luwen, a friend who is really good at planning.

I knew him through my university frisbee team. At one time, he was preparing for the CFA exam while attending club training, so he had tons of tasks on his plate. However, he was not affected too much by stress. He made realistic daily plans, split big tasks into smaller steps and used simple tools to prioritize his work.

What impressed me most was that he could still keep calm and efficient even when he struggled with all kinds of tasks. I feel he is organized, self-driven and reliable. I really want to learn this habit from him.

One practical detail I noticed was that he left some empty space in his schedule. If a task took longer than expected, he could adjust without abandoning the whole plan. He also checked his progress at the end of the week. That flexibility made his planning useful rather than stressful, and it is the part I would most like to copy in my own routine.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：计划与条理；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P082-Q1 Why do people plan their daily activities?

**观点1：** Planning helps people identify priorities. **解释1：** Without a clear order, easy tasks can replace important ones. **例子1：** For example, a student can revise a difficult chapter before checking messages.

**观点2：** It also makes cooperation easier. **解释2：** Other people can prepare when they know the schedule. **例子2：** For instance, teammates can arrange transport before a training session.

#### P082-Q2 Should children learn to make plans?

**观点1：** Yes, small plans can develop responsibility. **解释1：** Children learn that an activity requires preparation. **例子1：** For example, they can pack school materials the evening before.

**观点2：** However, plans should leave room for play. **解释2：** An overly detailed schedule can make ordinary days stressful. **例子2：** For instance, a free afternoon lets a child choose a game independently.

#### P082-Q3 Is a flexible plan better than a strict one?

**观点1：** Usually, flexibility makes a plan more realistic. **解释1：** Unexpected delays are part of everyday life. **例子1：** For example, extra travel time prevents one late bus from ruining a schedule.

**观点2：** However, some fixed points are necessary. **解释2：** Other people need commitments they can rely on. **例子2：** For instance, a group still needs an agreed meeting time.

#### P082-Q4 How can someone become more organised?

**观点1：** They can give important items a fixed place. **解释1：** This reduces the effort needed to find things. **例子1：** For example, keeping keys by the door avoids a rushed search.

**观点2：** They can also review priorities regularly. **解释2：** A list becomes less useful when circumstances change. **例子2：** For instance, a worker can update tomorrow's tasks before leaving the office.

#### P082-Q5 Does planning always lead to success?

**观点1：** Planning improves the chance of success. **解释1：** It turns a large goal into manageable actions. **例子1：** For example, a revision timetable can spread practice across several weeks.

**观点2：** However, results also depend on execution. **解释2：** A detailed plan achieves little if nobody follows it. **例子2：** For instance, a team must actually practise the skills listed in its programme.

#### P082-Q6 Why do some people dislike making plans?

**观点1：** Some people value freedom and spontaneity. **解释1：** A fixed schedule can feel restrictive during leisure time. **例子1：** For example, a traveller may want to explore a street unexpectedly.

**观点2：** Others have experienced unrealistic plans. **解释2：** Repeated failure can make planning itself seem pointless. **例子2：** For instance, an exhausting study timetable may discourage someone from trying again.


## P083 商店

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a shop/store you enjoy visiting

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P54原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, I'd like to talk about a physical sports store near my home.

I was first introduced to this shop after a whole day's work when I wanted to buy some frisbee equipment. The shop was not huge, but it sold a wide range of sports products, like frisbees, shoes and protective gear. I visited it from time to time, especially when I needed something for training.

What I liked most was the owner. He was really passionate and enthusiastic. When he noticed I was a beginner, he gave me practical suggestions, like how to choose a suitable frisbee and how to catch it safely.

I enjoy visiting this store because it is not just a place for shopping. It also gives me useful advice and makes me feel connected with people who share the same interest.

I also appreciate being able to see and compare the equipment directly. A photograph online cannot always show how something feels in my hands. The combination of practical advice and a chance to inspect the products makes visiting worthwhile for me.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：购物与服务；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P083-Q1 What makes shopping service good?

**观点1：** Good service begins with understanding the customer's needs. **解释1：** Staff should listen before recommending a product. **例子1：** For example, a sports shop can ask how experienced a beginner is.

**观点2：** Helpful support after a purchase also matters. **解释2：** Customers want a practical response when problems occur. **例子2：** For instance, a small repair can leave a stronger impression than a sales pitch.

#### P083-Q2 Why do people shop online?

**观点1：** Online shopping saves travel time. **解释1：** People can compare options without visiting several shops. **例子1：** For example, a busy worker can check prices during a short break.

**观点2：** It also provides a broad range of choices. **解释2：** Local shops may not stock a particular item. **例子2：** For instance, someone can search for equipment suited to a specialised hobby.

#### P083-Q3 What advantages do physical shops offer?

**观点1：** Physical shops let customers inspect products directly. **解释1：** Appearance online may not show comfort or fit. **例子1：** For example, trying on shoes can prevent a poor purchase.

**观点2：** They also allow immediate conversation. **解释2：** A knowledgeable employee can respond to specific questions. **例子2：** For instance, a beginner can ask how to choose and use suitable sports equipment.

#### P083-Q4 Why do shopping centres attract people?

**观点1：** They combine several activities in one place. **解释1：** People can organise errands and entertainment together. **例子1：** For example, friends can shop and then have dinner.

**观点2：** They also provide a predictable environment. **解释2：** Visitors know they can find facilities and shelter. **例子2：** For instance, a family may choose a shopping centre during bad weather.

#### P083-Q5 Can small shops compete with large stores?

**观点1：** Yes, small shops can build personal relationships. **解释1：** Regular customers may value familiar and thoughtful service. **例子1：** For example, a baker can remember someone's preferences.

**观点2：** They can also specialise effectively. **解释2：** A narrow focus may provide knowledge that general stores lack. **例子2：** For instance, a sports shop can advise beginners on equipment and technique.

#### P083-Q6 How should businesses respond to complaints?

**观点1：** They should first understand the specific problem. **解释1：** Listening carefully prevents an irrelevant or defensive response. **例子1：** For example, staff can ask what happened before suggesting a repair.

**观点2：** They should then offer a clear next step. **解释2：** Customers need realistic expectations about the solution. **例子2：** For instance, explaining a replacement process is more useful than an empty apology.


## P084 想见的名人

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a famous person you would like to meet.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 新编示范：姚明；替换旧稿已认识的Luwen，避免答题矛盾。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The famous person I would like to meet is Yao Ming, the former professional basketball player. I know him mainly through basketball coverage, and I would be interested in hearing about his experiences directly.

I enjoy team sports, so I would not focus only on famous matches or individual achievements. I would like to ask how he handled pressure and communicated with teammates when a game was going badly. Those situations remind me of challenges I have experienced on a much smaller scale through frisbee.

If I had the opportunity, I would prefer a relaxed conversation after a sports event rather than simply asking for a photograph. I would also be curious about how his perspective on sport changed after his playing career.

Meeting him would be meaningful because I could learn from someone who experienced competition at a very high level. I do not expect a conversation to transform my life, but even one practical insight about patience or teamwork could stay with me for a long time.

I would prepare thoughtful questions so that the conversation felt worthwhile.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：名人与采访；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P084-Q1 Why do people admire famous athletes?

**观点1：** Athletes demonstrate achievement through visible effort. **解释1：** People can see skill and determination during competition. **例子1：** For example, a comeback may show how someone responds to pressure.

**观点2：** They can also represent shared interests. **解释2：** Supporters feel connected through following the same sport. **例子2：** For instance, a local champion may bring attention to a community team.

#### P084-Q2 Should famous people be role models?

**观点1：** They can offer useful examples of effort. **解释1：** Young people may become interested in a positive activity. **例子1：** For example, an athlete can encourage a child to try sport.

**观点2：** However, fame does not guarantee good judgement. **解释2：** Public achievements reveal only part of someone's character. **例子2：** For instance, being a skilled performer does not make every personal opinion reliable.

#### P084-Q3 Why do people watch interviews with celebrities?

**观点1：** Interviews can reveal the process behind achievement. **解释1：** Viewers want more than a polished public image. **例子1：** For example, an athlete discussing setbacks may explain how progress happened.

**观点2：** They also satisfy personal curiosity. **解释2：** Fans enjoy learning about interests and experiences. **例子2：** For instance, a musician's account of learning an instrument can feel more personal than a performance.

#### P084-Q4 What makes a good interview?

**观点1：** Good questions invite specific explanations. **解释1：** Vague questions often produce rehearsed answers. **例子1：** For example, asking about a difficult decision can reveal someone's thinking.

**观点2：** Careful listening is equally important. **解释2：** The interviewer should respond to interesting details. **例子2：** For instance, a follow-up question can clarify an unexpected point rather than rushing to the next topic.

#### P084-Q5 Is local fame different from international fame?

**观点1：** Local fame often involves direct community connections. **解释1：** People may know the person's everyday contribution. **例子1：** For example, a respected coach can be familiar to players and families.

**观点2：** International fame depends more on media exposure. **解释2：** Most followers never meet the person. **例子2：** For instance, an athlete's image may be shaped mainly by broadcasts and online coverage.

#### P084-Q6 Would meeting a famous person change someone's life?

**观点1：** It might provide encouragement or a useful insight. **解释1：** A personal conversation can make an ambition feel more concrete. **例子1：** For example, a young player may gain motivation from practical advice.

**观点2：** However, lasting change still requires action. **解释2：** Admiration alone does not develop a skill. **例子2：** For instance, the player must continue training after the meeting ends.


## P085 有趣的建筑

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an interesting building

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P51原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, the unusual building that I'd like to talk about is a courtyard house. In Chinese, we call it Siheyuan.

The history of this ancient building can date back hundreds of years. It is a traditional and unusual type of residence mainly located in Beijing.

A small or medium-sized courtyard usually has its main gate built on one side of the yard. Behind the gate, there is a screen wall, which helps block the wind and protect privacy. The buildings are arranged around a square yard, and each room faces the center, which makes the space feel organized and peaceful.

I find it interesting because it offers space, comfort and privacy. Since there is a small yard, people can plant flowers and plants there, making the whole space lively and gorgeous. It also represents China's profound history and diverse culture.

I would really enjoy spending an afternoon there with a book, listening to the sounds from the courtyard. The design makes me think about how a home can support both personal privacy and time together with family members.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：建筑与居住；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P085-Q1 Why do people enjoy visiting traditional buildings?

**观点1：** Traditional buildings make history visible. **解释1：** Visitors can see how earlier generations organised daily life. **例子1：** For example, a courtyard suggests how a family shared outdoor space.

**观点2：** They also provide visual variety. **解释2：** Distinctive designs make a place memorable. **例子2：** For instance, older houses can give a neighbourhood character beside modern offices.

#### P085-Q2 Should cities preserve old buildings?

**观点1：** Yes, valuable buildings can protect local identity. **解释1：** Once demolished, an original setting cannot easily be recreated. **例子1：** For example, a historic street can show a city's development.

**观点2：** However, preservation needs practical maintenance. **解释2：** A neglected building cannot serve visitors or residents well. **例子2：** For instance, repairs may improve safety while retaining important design features.

#### P085-Q3 What makes a home comfortable?

**观点1：** A practical layout supports everyday activities. **解释1：** People need suitable space for rest, work and storage. **例子1：** For example, a quiet corner can separate study from family noise.

**观点2：** Personal connections also create comfort. **解释2：** Familiar objects make a space feel like home. **例子2：** For instance, family photographs can make a simple room welcoming.

#### P085-Q4 Are tall buildings always suitable for city life?

**观点1：** Tall buildings can use limited land efficiently. **解释1：** They place many homes or workplaces in a compact area. **例子1：** For example, an office tower can accommodate several companies.

**观点2：** However, daily access needs careful planning. **解释2：** Crowded lifts and surrounding traffic can create inconvenience. **例子2：** For instance, many people leaving at once may face long waits.

#### P085-Q5 Should a building's appearance matter more than its function?

**观点1：** Function should usually come first. **解释1：** People must use the space comfortably every day. **例子1：** For example, an attractive office is frustrating without suitable light or quiet.

**观点2：** However, appearance still affects experience. **解释2：** Thoughtful design can make a place pleasant and distinctive. **例子2：** For instance, a welcoming entrance can improve how visitors feel.

#### P085-Q6 Why might people enjoy visiting a home but dislike living there?

**观点1：** A visit highlights atmosphere and novelty. **解释1：** People can appreciate an unusual setting for a short time. **例子1：** For example, a courtyard may feel peaceful during an afternoon.

**观点2：** Permanent residence involves practical demands. **解释2：** Maintenance, transport and comfort matter throughout the year. **例子2：** For instance, a beautiful house may be inconvenient for a daily commute.


## P086 近期看过且享受的电影

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a movie you watched and enjoyed recently

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体对象；怎样接触或获得；外观、内容或用途；它为什么对你重要。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P16原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to talk about a movie called Let the Bullets Fly, which I watched recently and enjoyed.

I watched it at home with my friends a few months ago. It's a clever Chinese movie, both a black comedy and an action film, set in China in the early 1900s. The story is about a brave and smart man who fights against a powerful local bully.

What I enjoyed most was the dialogue and the acting. The lines were sharp, humorous and thought-provoking. The movie was not just entertaining; it also reflected social problems and uncovered the real side of life.

I watched it because many people recommended it online, and after watching it, I understood why it's so well-received. It's definitely worth watching again.

Another enjoyable part was discussing different scenes after the film ended. My friends noticed details that I had missed, so our conversation made me appreciate it more. I like a film that stays interesting beyond the final scene. This one gave us both an entertaining evening and a reason to exchange our own interpretations afterwards.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：影视与视频；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P086-Q1 What makes a film or programme enjoyable?

**观点1：** A convincing story helps people care about the outcome. **解释1：** Characters need motives that viewers can understand. **例子1：** For example, a difficult choice can create more interest than a long action scene.

**观点2：** Presentation also matters. **解释2：** Acting, sound and visual details support the atmosphere. **例子2：** For instance, an unusual office design can strengthen a mysterious television story.

#### P086-Q2 Why do different people enjoy different kinds of entertainment?

**观点1：** People use entertainment to meet different needs. **解释1：** Some want excitement while others want a quiet break. **例子1：** For example, a tired worker may choose a gentle comedy.

**观点2：** Personal experience also shapes taste. **解释2：** Familiar themes can feel more meaningful. **例子2：** For instance, someone who plays sport may enjoy a documentary about teamwork.

#### P086-Q3 Are online videos useful for learning?

**观点1：** They can make a process easy to observe. **解释1：** Visual demonstrations show steps that text may not explain clearly. **例子1：** For example, a learner can watch how to hold a frisbee.

**观点2：** However, quality varies considerably. **解释2：** An attractive presentation does not guarantee a reliable explanation. **例子2：** For instance, viewers should question a dramatic claim without supporting evidence.

#### P086-Q4 Is watching at home better than going to a cinema?

**观点1：** Watching at home offers convenience. **解释1：** People can choose a time that fits their routine. **例子1：** For example, friends can watch together after dinner without travelling.

**观点2：** A cinema provides a more focused experience. **解释2：** The large screen and shared atmosphere can increase involvement. **例子2：** For instance, an impressive visual sequence may feel more powerful there.

#### P086-Q5 Why do people share entertaining videos?

**观点1：** They want to share an enjoyable reaction. **解释1：** A short clip can make someone laugh quickly. **例子1：** For example, friends may send a funny scene during a busy day.

**观点2：** Sharing also expresses personal interests. **解释2：** It gives people a reason to start a conversation. **例子2：** For instance, a science video can lead to discussion among curious colleagues.

#### P086-Q6 Should entertainment always teach people something?

**观点1：** No, relaxation is a valid purpose. **解释1：** People sometimes need a break from demanding tasks. **例子1：** For example, a light comedy can make an ordinary evening pleasant.

**观点2：** However, meaningful ideas can add lasting value. **解释2：** Viewers may continue thinking after the programme ends. **例子2：** For instance, a story about work and identity can inspire a thoughtful conversation.


## P087 喜欢的电视/网络节目

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a TV or online program you like to watch

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体对象；怎样接触或获得；外观、内容或用途；它为什么对你重要。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P26原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

The TV series I'd like to talk about is Severance.

It came out in 2022, and it focuses on a company called Lumon Industries, which uses a technology called memory severance to separate employees' work and personal memories. The idea sounds strange, but it is very fascinating.

What I like most is that this show explores how technology can deeply affect our lives and even our identity. The plot is mysterious and thought-provoking, so every episode makes me want to keep watching.

I also think it is visually impressive. The office scenes look cold and unusual, which matches the story perfectly. I enjoyed it because it is not just entertainment; it also made me think about work-life balance and personal freedom.

I usually prefer to watch it when I feel energetic to concentrate. There are small details that are easy to miss if I am checking my phone at the same time. Afterwards, I enjoy discussing possible explanations with friends. That combination of suspense and conversation makes the programme feel more engaging than something I'd simply leave playing in the background.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：影视与视频；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P087-Q1 What makes a film or programme enjoyable?

**观点1：** A convincing story helps people care about the outcome. **解释1：** Characters need motives that viewers can understand. **例子1：** For example, a difficult choice can create more interest than a long action scene.

**观点2：** Presentation also matters. **解释2：** Acting, sound and visual details support the atmosphere. **例子2：** For instance, an unusual office design can strengthen a mysterious television story.

#### P087-Q2 Why do different people enjoy different kinds of entertainment?

**观点1：** People use entertainment to meet different needs. **解释1：** Some want excitement while others want a quiet break. **例子1：** For example, a tired worker may choose a gentle comedy.

**观点2：** Personal experience also shapes taste. **解释2：** Familiar themes can feel more meaningful. **例子2：** For instance, someone who plays sport may enjoy a documentary about teamwork.

#### P087-Q3 Are online videos useful for learning?

**观点1：** They can make a process easy to observe. **解释1：** Visual demonstrations show steps that text may not explain clearly. **例子1：** For example, a learner can watch how to hold a frisbee.

**观点2：** However, quality varies considerably. **解释2：** An attractive presentation does not guarantee a reliable explanation. **例子2：** For instance, viewers should question a dramatic claim without supporting evidence.

#### P087-Q4 Is watching at home better than going to a cinema?

**观点1：** Watching at home offers convenience. **解释1：** People can choose a time that fits their routine. **例子1：** For example, friends can watch together after dinner without travelling.

**观点2：** A cinema provides a more focused experience. **解释2：** The large screen and shared atmosphere can increase involvement. **例子2：** For instance, an impressive visual sequence may feel more powerful there.

#### P087-Q5 Why do people share entertaining videos?

**观点1：** They want to share an enjoyable reaction. **解释1：** A short clip can make someone laugh quickly. **例子1：** For example, friends may send a funny scene during a busy day.

**观点2：** Sharing also expresses personal interests. **解释2：** It gives people a reason to start a conversation. **例子2：** For instance, a science video can lead to discussion among curious colleagues.

#### P087-Q6 Should entertainment always teach people something?

**观点1：** No, relaxation is a valid purpose. **解释1：** People sometimes need a break from demanding tasks. **例子1：** For example, a light comedy can make an ordinary evening pleasant.

**观点2：** However, meaningful ideas can add lasting value. **解释2：** Viewers may continue thinking after the programme ends. **例子2：** For instance, a story about work and identity can inspire a thoughtful conversation.


## P088 安静的地方

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a quiet place you like to go

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 地点与位置；何时接触；看到了什么或做了什么；喜欢、不喜欢或想去的原因。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P52原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to talk about a quiet place I really like, and it is Zhangjiajie National Forest Park in Hunan Province, China.

I was first introduced to this place a few years ago through photos and documentaries. What impressed me most were its unique pillar-like mountains and the wild monkeys there. I do not go there very often because it is quite far from where I live, but it is still one of the quietest and most unforgettable places I have ever been to.

When I was there, I was completely stunned by the scenery. There were all kinds of trees reaching high into the sky, and I could hear birds chirping in the morning. The fresh air and the sea of green trees made me feel calm and peaceful.

In such tranquil surroundings, I felt incredibly relaxed. The whole experience was therapeutic and refreshing, and it also raised my awareness of environmental protection.

I would happily return with someone who enjoys walking slowly, because this is a place where noticing small details is more rewarding than rushing between attractions.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：自然与植物；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P088-Q1 Why do people enjoy visiting natural places?

**观点1：** Natural surroundings provide a change from demanding routines. **解释1：** People can slow down and focus on something beyond work. **例子1：** For example, a walk among trees can feel refreshing after computer tasks.

**观点2：** They also offer shared recreation. **解释2：** Friends can spend time together without a complicated programme. **例子2：** For instance, a park visit can combine conversation and gentle exercise.

#### P088-Q2 Should cities provide more green spaces?

**观点1：** Yes, nearby greenery makes recreation more accessible. **解释1：** Residents do not always have time to travel far. **例子1：** For example, a small neighbourhood park can support an evening walk.

**观点2：** Green spaces also improve shared surroundings. **解释2：** They give people pleasant places to meet and rest. **例子2：** For instance, shaded seating can make a busy district more welcoming.

#### P088-Q3 How can people be encouraged to protect nature?

**观点1：** Practical activities make the issue tangible. **解释1：** People see the effect of their own behaviour. **例子1：** For example, collecting litter can reveal how much waste visitors leave.

**观点2：** Clear facilities also support better choices. **解释2：** Good intentions are easier to follow when alternatives are convenient. **例子2：** For instance, visible bins can help visitors dispose of rubbish properly.

#### P088-Q4 Why do some people enjoy growing plants?

**观点1：** Growing plants offers visible progress. **解释1：** Small changes provide a sense of achievement. **例子1：** For example, a new leaf can reward weeks of regular care.

**观点2：** The activity can also create a calming routine. **解释2：** People focus on simple tasks away from screens. **例子2：** For instance, checking a few pots can become a pleasant morning habit.

#### P088-Q5 Should children spend more time outdoors?

**观点1：** Yes, outdoor activity offers different experiences from classroom learning. **解释1：** Children can move, explore and notice living things. **例子1：** For example, a park visit may lead to questions about insects.

**观点2：** However, the activity should suit the setting. **解释2：** Adults need to balance freedom with practical supervision. **例子2：** For instance, a nature walk can use clear boundaries while allowing exploration.

#### P088-Q6 Can tourism harm natural places?

**观点1：** Yes, heavy use can damage the environment. **解释1：** Visitors may leave rubbish or disturb sensitive areas. **例子1：** For example, walking away from paths can affect vegetation.

**观点2：** However, well-managed tourism can support protection. **解释2：** Visitor interest can create reasons to maintain a place carefully. **例子2：** For instance, clear routes and educational signs can guide more considerate behaviour.


## P089 花费超过预期的物品

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe something that cost more than you expected.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体物品；为什么想要；试用、储蓄或购买过程；价格与预算如何影响你的决定。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P19原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a VR headset that cost more than I had originally planned. I became interested after a demonstration, but the model that suited me was above my first budget.

This VR headset has a sleek, matte black body. It looks really modern and cool. It is also pretty handy, meaning it is not too bulky or heavy when I wear it, but at the same time it feels sturdy and well-made.

When I first used it, I was completely shocked by its incredibly immersive visuals. To be honest, I was amazed. It really felt like the characters in movies and games were right there face-to-face, interacting with me. I got quite hooked on it for a while, exploring different virtual worlds and games.

I decided to raise my budget because the model offered the experience I wanted, although I compared alternatives carefully first. Even though it was expensive, I saw it as a reward for my hard work, so it was worth the money.

Next time, I would set a clearer limit before trying expensive products.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：消费与储蓄；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P089-Q1 Why is saving money important?

**观点1：** Savings provide flexibility when circumstances change. **解释1：** People can deal with unexpected costs more comfortably. **例子1：** For example, a broken computer may need replacement before the next payday.

**观点2：** Saving also supports larger goals. **解释2：** A regular habit makes an expensive purchase more manageable. **例子2：** For instance, someone can gradually put money aside for useful training.

#### P089-Q2 Why do people buy things they do not need?

**观点1：** Advertisements can create a temporary desire. **解释1：** Attractive presentation makes an item feel more valuable than it is. **例子1：** For example, a discount may encourage an unnecessary electronic upgrade.

**观点2：** People may also seek a quick emotional reward. **解释2：** Buying something new can briefly make a difficult day feel better. **例子2：** For instance, someone may order clothes after a stressful week.

#### P089-Q3 Should children learn to manage money?

**观点1：** Yes, simple practice develops awareness of limits. **解释1：** Children can learn that spending on one thing affects other choices. **例子1：** For example, a small allowance can be divided between snacks and savings.

**观点2：** Parents should also explain value. **解释2：** Price alone does not show whether something is useful. **例子2：** For instance, a durable school bag may be better than a fashionable replacement.

#### P089-Q4 Is shopping easier than saving?

**观点1：** Shopping is often easier in the short term. **解释1：** The reward is immediate and the process can be convenient. **例子1：** For example, a person can order a product with very little effort.

**观点2：** Saving becomes easier with a clear purpose. **解释2：** A specific goal makes delaying a purchase feel worthwhile. **例子2：** For instance, planning a family trip can motivate regular saving.

#### P089-Q5 Are expensive products always better?

**观点1：** No, price may reflect branding rather than practical quality. **解释1：** A buyer should consider the features they will actually use. **例子1：** For example, basic headphones may suit occasional listening.

**观点2：** However, higher quality can justify extra cost. **解释2：** A durable item may last longer or perform more reliably. **例子2：** For instance, comfortable sports shoes can be worth paying more for.

#### P089-Q6 How can people avoid spending beyond their budget?

**观点1：** They can decide a limit before browsing. **解释1：** This reduces the influence of attractive displays and upgrades. **例子1：** For example, a shopper can compare only products within an affordable range.

**观点2：** They can also delay nonessential purchases. **解释2：** Waiting reveals whether the interest lasts. **例子2：** For instance, someone may lose enthusiasm for a gadget after a few days.


## P090 为家人骄傲

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a time when you felt proud of a family member

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P42原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, I'd like to talk about a time when I felt proud of my father.

It happened when I was about seven years old. At that time, my favorite toy was a red remote-control car. One afternoon, our naughty pet dog bit it, and one of the wheels came off completely. I was so upset that I cried for a while.

My father didn't blame me. Instead, he spent the whole evening fixing it with glue and tape. In the beginning, I thought the car could never work again, but he restored it patiently.

I felt proud of him because he was practical, patient and caring. That small thing showed me his love, and it taught me to take better care of my belongings.

I noticed that he didn't immediately suggest buying a replacement. He wanted to understand the damage and see whether the original toy could be saved. That taught me a small lesson about patience and making good use of what we already have. I still associate practical kindness with that evening whenever something needs repairing.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：家庭与回忆；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P090-Q1 Why do family gatherings matter?

**观点1：** They give relatives time to reconnect. **解释1：** Everyday responsibilities can prevent proper conversations. **例子1：** For example, a shared dinner lets people discuss more than practical messages.

**观点2：** They also create shared memories. **解释2：** Repeated occasions become part of a family's history. **例子2：** For instance, a funny moment in a group photograph may be remembered for years.

#### P090-Q2 How can families spend more meaningful time together?

**观点1：** They can choose activities that allow conversation. **解释1：** An expensive outing is unnecessary if nobody has time to talk. **例子1：** For example, cooking a simple meal together can feel personal.

**观点2：** They should also protect some time from interruptions. **解释2：** Constant messages divide attention. **例子2：** For instance, putting phones aside during dinner can help everyone participate.

#### P090-Q3 Why do people keep old family objects?

**观点1：** Old objects can preserve emotional connections. **解释1：** They remind people of someone who gave or repaired them. **例子1：** For example, a childhood toy can represent a father's care.

**观点2：** They can also pass stories between generations. **解释2：** A physical object makes family history easier to discuss. **例子2：** For instance, an old photograph may lead to stories about grandparents.

#### P090-Q4 What makes people proud of relatives?

**观点1：** People feel proud when relatives overcome difficulties. **解释1：** They understand the effort behind the visible result. **例子1：** For example, completing a demanding course can mean more to family than strangers.

**观点2：** Kind behaviour also creates pride. **解释2：** A relative's character reflects what the family values. **例子2：** For instance, patiently helping a distressed child can leave a lasting impression.

#### P090-Q5 Are family celebrations different now from the past?

**观点1：** Some celebrations now involve more technology. **解释1：** People can include relatives who cannot travel. **例子1：** For example, a video call can connect a distant family member with a birthday.

**观点2：** However, busy routines can reduce time together. **解释2：** Work and distance make arrangements harder. **例子2：** For instance, a reunion may need to fit several different schedules.

#### P090-Q6 Do expensive presents show greater affection?

**观点1：** Not necessarily, because thoughtfulness matters more than price. **解释1：** A suitable gift shows understanding of the recipient. **例子1：** For example, a handmade card can refer to a shared memory.

**观点2：** However, practical value can also be meaningful. **解释2：** A gift may solve a real difficulty. **例子2：** For instance, useful equipment can support a relative's long-standing hobby.


## P091 自行车/摩托车/汽车旅行

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a journey by car, bike or motorbike that you would like to take.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P32青岛旅行修订为未来行程。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to take another car trip to Qingdao with my family. We visited the city before, and I would enjoy returning with a more relaxed plan.

The journey would give us time to talk and watch the surroundings change. We would allow enough time for breaks instead of trying to arrive as quickly as possible. I would also check the route in advance and avoid setting an overly ambitious schedule for the first day.

Once there, I would like to visit the seafood market again. On our previous trip, we chose fresh seafood and had it cooked for a small fee. I would also leave time for a quiet walk near the sea rather than filling every hour with attractions.

I want to make this journey because it combines familiar pleasures with time together. A car would give us flexibility about stops and luggage. More importantly, the trip would let us step away from work and enjoy the kind of ordinary conversation that busy routines often interrupt.

I would value the shared time as much as the destination.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：旅行与交通；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P091-Q1 Why do traffic jams happen?

**观点1：** Many people travel at similar times. **解释1：** Road space becomes insufficient when demand rises together. **例子1：** For example, commuting hours can bring large numbers of cars into one area.

**观点2：** Limited alternatives can make the problem worse. **解释2：** People may drive because other options are inconvenient. **例子2：** For instance, a poor connection can make public transport difficult to use.

#### P091-Q2 How can cities reduce traffic congestion?

**观点1：** They can improve practical public transport options. **解释1：** People are more willing to change when alternatives are reliable. **例子1：** For example, frequent connections make commuting easier to plan.

**观点2：** They can also reduce the need for identical travel times. **解释2：** Flexible routines can spread demand. **例子2：** For instance, some workplaces may allow different starting hours where the job permits.

#### P091-Q3 Why do people enjoy travelling by car?

**观点1：** Cars offer flexibility about routes and stops. **解释1：** Travellers can adapt the journey to their needs. **例子1：** For example, a family can pause when someone needs a break.

**观点2：** They also make luggage easier to manage. **解释2：** People can carry items for different activities. **例子2：** For instance, a seaside trip may involve food, sports equipment and several bags.

#### P091-Q4 What disadvantages do car journeys have?

**观点1：** Traffic can make arrival times unpredictable. **解释1：** A short route may still involve a long delay. **例子1：** For example, travelling across a city at dinner time can be frustrating.

**观点2：** Driving also requires continuous attention. **解释2：** The driver cannot relax in the same way as passengers. **例子2：** For instance, a long trip needs sensible breaks and a realistic schedule.

#### P091-Q5 Should people plan journeys carefully?

**观点1：** Yes, basic planning prevents avoidable difficulties. **解释1：** Travellers need to understand routes and timing. **例子1：** For example, checking transport options can reduce the risk of arriving late.

**观点2：** However, plans should leave flexibility. **解释2：** Unexpected changes are difficult to predict fully. **例子2：** For instance, allowing spare time makes a delayed connection less stressful.

#### P091-Q6 Are short trips as valuable as long holidays?

**观点1：** They can be valuable because they fit ordinary routines. **解释1：** People may not have time for an extended break. **例子1：** For example, a nearby park outing can provide a refreshing afternoon.

**观点2：** Longer holidays offer different opportunities. **解释2：** Travellers can explore a place more deeply. **例子2：** For instance, several days in a city allow both major sights and relaxed local experiences.


## P092 机智解决问题的人

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a person who solved a problem in a smart way

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 人物是谁；如何认识；一件能体现特点的具体事；你的感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P08原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

Last week, while vacationing in Vietnam with a friend, I encountered a shop owner 
  who solved a problem in a very smart way. 
  He was the owner of a cozy cafe we visited on our last afternoon there.

The issue was quite embarrassing. As we were about to pay, I realized the cafe only accepted cash, but we had spent every last cent of our local currency. All we had left were our credit cards.

Instead of getting frustrated or making us feel uncomfortable, the owner remained calm and patient. He told us not to worry and asked us to wait a moment. Then he went out and borrowed a credit card swiping machine from a neighboring store. About ten minutes later, he returned, and we successfully processed the payment.

I think his approach was brilliant because it was both resourceful and customer-oriented. His efficiency and warm-heartedness turned a potentially negative experience into a highlight of our trip.

I'd remember that calm response if I faced a similar problem, because a small practical idea can completely change how customers feel.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：经营与服务；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P092-Q1 What helps a small business succeed?

**观点1：** Consistent quality encourages customers to return. **解释1：** People appreciate knowing what they can expect. **例子1：** For example, a bakery benefits when its familiar products remain reliable.

**观点2：** Personal service can also build trust. **解释2：** Understanding customers creates a reason to choose that shop. **例子2：** For instance, thoughtful advice may matter more than an aggressive discount.

#### P092-Q2 Is starting a business easy?

**观点1：** It can be difficult because several responsibilities arrive together. **解释1：** A good product alone does not manage costs or customers. **例子1：** For example, a cafe owner also needs to organise supplies and service.

**观点2：** However, starting on a manageable scale can support learning. **解释2：** Small experiments reveal problems before expansion. **例子2：** For instance, testing a limited menu can clarify customer preferences.

#### P092-Q3 What makes an employee valuable to a company?

**观点1：** Reliability makes cooperation easier. **解释1：** Colleagues need work to be completed with appropriate care. **例子1：** For example, accurate records prevent confusion later.

**观点2：** A helpful attitude also improves the customer's experience. **解释2：** Ordinary interactions shape the company's reputation. **例子2：** For instance, a patient explanation can make a visitor comfortable returning to the shop.

#### P092-Q4 How important is solving problems creatively in business?

**观点1：** It is important because unexpected situations are common. **解释1：** A rigid routine may not provide a suitable answer. **例子1：** For example, a shopkeeper can cooperate with a neighbour to resolve a payment difficulty.

**观点2：** However, solutions must remain responsible. **解释2：** Creativity should not create a larger problem elsewhere. **例子2：** For instance, staff should explain an alternative clearly before a customer accepts it.

#### P092-Q5 Should family members work together in a business?

**观点1：** It can work well when there is trust. **解释1：** Relatives may share a strong commitment to the business. **例子1：** For example, a family bakery may value a long-standing local reputation.

**观点2：** However, roles still need clear boundaries. **解释2：** Personal relationships can make disagreements harder to discuss. **例子2：** For instance, responsibilities should be agreed rather than assumed because of family status.

#### P092-Q6 Should companies focus only on profit?

**观点1：** No, customer trust matters for lasting success. **解释1：** Short-term gains can damage a reputation if service is poor. **例子1：** For example, an unsuitable recommendation may lose a regular customer.

**观点2：** Employees' working conditions also matter. **解释2：** A business depends on people who can perform sustainably. **例子2：** For instance, clear training and reasonable schedules can support reliable service.


## P093 微笑的场合

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an occasion when many people were smiling

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P31原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, I'd like to share an experience with you.

It dates back to a few years ago. On Spring Festival, we enjoyed our reunion dinner, and after dinner, we took our first family photo. In the middle of the photo, there were my grandparents, my parents and me.

The tone of the photo was delightful and festive. Everyone was smiling because our little dog suddenly rushed into the room and barked a lot, which was so amusing. Besides, it was our first family photo, so it documented a memorable family time for us.

For me, it's a family treasure. Whenever I take a glance at this photo, I can recall sweet memories. In terms of a smiling occasion, that scene always springs to my mind.

What makes the photograph valuable is that the smiles were spontaneous. Nobody needed to pretend to be happy for the camera. The unexpected interruption relaxed everyone, and the picture captured that feeling. I think those moments can become meaningful later, when family members have fewer chances to gather in the same room together.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：快乐与微笑；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P093-Q1 What makes people happy in everyday life?

**观点1：** Supportive relationships are a major source of happiness. **解释1：** People feel valued when someone listens and shares their time. **例子1：** For example, a relaxed meal with friends can improve an ordinary evening.

**观点2：** Progress also creates satisfaction. **解释2：** Completing something difficult shows that effort has mattered. **例子2：** For instance, finishing a challenging project can leave someone feeling capable.

#### P093-Q2 Can money make people happier?

**观点1：** Money can reduce practical pressure. **解释1：** Meeting essential needs leaves people with more choices. **例子1：** For example, affordable housing can make daily life less stressful.

**观点2：** However, spending does not guarantee lasting enjoyment. **解释2：** A new object may lose its appeal quickly. **例子2：** For instance, a gadget may be forgotten while a shared family experience remains meaningful.

#### P093-Q3 Why do people smile at social events?

**观点1：** Smiling often expresses genuine enjoyment. **解释1：** Shared activities create moments of warmth and amusement. **例子1：** For example, an unexpected joke can make a whole family laugh.

**观点2：** It can also help others feel welcome. **解释2：** A friendly expression reduces tension between unfamiliar people. **例子2：** For instance, a host's smile can make a new guest more comfortable.

#### P093-Q4 Are children happier than adults?

**观点1：** Children may enjoy the present more easily. **解释1：** They often have fewer long-term responsibilities. **例子1：** For example, an afternoon game can absorb their full attention.

**观点2：** Adults can also find deeper satisfaction through choice and achievement. **解释2：** Independence gives them control over meaningful goals. **例子2：** For instance, completing a project or supporting family can create lasting pride.

#### P093-Q5 How can schools make students feel happier?

**观点1：** Schools can create a supportive atmosphere. **解释1：** Students need to feel safe asking questions and making mistakes. **例子1：** For example, patient feedback can help a nervous learner participate.

**观点2：** They should also provide varied activities. **解释2：** Enjoyment does not come only from academic success. **例子2：** For instance, clubs let children build friendships through art or sport.

#### P093-Q6 Is happiness mainly a matter of attitude?

**观点1：** Attitude can influence what people notice. **解释1：** Appreciating ordinary moments can make a routine more enjoyable. **例子1：** For example, someone may value a quiet walk after work.

**观点2：** However, circumstances also matter. **解释2：** A positive outlook cannot remove every practical difficulty. **例子2：** For instance, heavy responsibilities may require real support rather than advice to smile more.


## P094 禁用手机的场合

截图：第11张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe an occasion when you were not allowed to use your phone.

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P43空缺补写：现场演出要求收起手机。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I would like to describe a live performance where the audience was asked to put phones away. I attended it with a friend at a small venue in the city centre.

Before the show began, a staff member explained that filming and using bright screens during the performance were not allowed. The aim was to avoid distracting the performers and the people sitting nearby. We were told that anyone who needed to use a phone could step outside.

At first, I felt slightly uncomfortable because I was used to checking messages during quiet moments. However, once the performance started, I became more absorbed in what was happening on stage. I also noticed how pleasant it was not to see screens appearing in front of me.

Afterwards, my friend and I discussed the show instead of immediately comparing recordings. I understood the rule because it had a clear purpose and a reasonable alternative. The experience showed me how putting my phone away can sometimes make an activity more memorable.

I would willingly follow a similar request when its purpose was clear.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：规则与手机使用；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P094-Q1 Why do shared places need rules?

**观点1：** Rules help people understand how their behaviour affects others. **解释1：** Different visitors may otherwise have conflicting expectations. **例子1：** For example, a quiet performance needs an agreement about phone use.

**观点2：** They also make practical management easier. **解释2：** Staff can respond consistently to problems. **例子2：** For instance, clear entry instructions help a crowd move without unnecessary confusion.

#### P094-Q2 When are phone restrictions reasonable?

**观点1：** They are reasonable when phones clearly interfere with an activity. **解释1：** Bright screens or recording can distract people nearby. **例子1：** For example, a theatre may ask the audience to put devices away.

**观点2：** However, restrictions should allow practical alternatives. **解释2：** Visitors may occasionally need urgent communication. **例子2：** For instance, letting someone step outside is more reasonable than ignoring that need.

#### P094-Q3 Why do some people ignore rules?

**观点1：** They may not understand the purpose. **解释1：** An unexplained instruction can seem arbitrary. **例子1：** For example, visitors may dismiss a restriction without knowing whom it protects.

**观点2：** Others prioritise their own convenience. **解释2：** They underestimate the effect on people around them. **例子2：** For instance, someone filming a show may ignore the blocked view behind the phone.

#### P094-Q4 How can organisations encourage people to follow rules?

**观点1：** They should explain expectations clearly and early. **解释1：** People cooperate more easily when they know what to do. **例子1：** For example, a short announcement before a performance prevents confusion.

**观点2：** They should also apply rules consistently. **解释2：** Unequal treatment makes restrictions seem unfair. **例子2：** For instance, similar behaviour should receive the same response regardless of who is involved.

#### P094-Q5 Should children help create classroom rules?

**观点1：** Yes, discussion can help them understand the reasons. **解释1：** Participation encourages responsibility for shared behaviour. **例子1：** For example, pupils can discuss how interruptions affect learning.
