**观点2：** However, adults must still provide necessary boundaries. **解释2：** Some responsibilities cannot simply be decided by popularity. **例子2：** For instance, a teacher needs to ensure that every student can participate safely and respectfully.

#### P094-Q6 Are strict rules always more effective?

**观点1：** No, excessive strictness can create resistance. **解释1：** People may focus on avoiding punishment rather than understanding the purpose. **例子1：** For example, an unreasonable phone ban can distract from a venue's real needs.

**观点2：** However, clear boundaries are useful when consequences matter. **解释2：** Some activities depend on consistent behaviour. **例子2：** For instance, a performance needs predictable expectations about recording and disruption.


## P095 鼓励别人做不愿做的事

截图：第12张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a time when you encouraged someone to do something that he/she didn’t want to do

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P36原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, I'd like to talk about a guy named Luwen. We crossed paths when I joined the university frisbee team, where he was the captain.

A couple of months ago, he was preparing for the CFA test and attending club training. In that case, he suffered from a tight schedule and always struggled with sleep. So I recommended meditation to him.

In the beginning, he didn't want to try it because he thought meditation was boring and even time-wasting. He was distracted by his smartphone. But finally, he stuck to it for a short time. He found it could help him calm his mind and regain inner peace.

Although he has finished the CFA test, he still sticks to doing meditation. It has become part of his daily routine.

I tried to explain the suggestion without making him feel judged. We talked about what would fit his schedule, and I offered to practise with him at first. I think that friendly support mattered because he could explore the idea without feeling that someone was ordering him to change.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：建议与鼓励；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P095-Q1 What makes advice useful?

**观点1：** Useful advice matches the person's actual situation. **解释1：** A suggestion should fit their time, resources and goals. **例子1：** For example, a short routine may suit a busy learner better than an ambitious schedule.

**观点2：** It should also explain the reasoning. **解释2：** People need to understand why an approach may help. **例子2：** For instance, dividing tasks can make a large goal less overwhelming.

#### P095-Q2 Why do people reject good advice?

**观点1：** They may feel judged or controlled. **解释1：** Even a practical suggestion can sound critical. **例子1：** For example, telling a tired friend to organise life better may cause defensiveness.

**观点2：** They may also doubt that the advice fits them. **解释2：** Past experience can affect willingness to try. **例子2：** For instance, a difficult previous routine may make someone sceptical about another plan.

#### P095-Q3 Should people give advice without being asked?

**观点1：** Sometimes a gentle offer is appropriate. **解释1：** A person may not know that help is available. **例子1：** For example, a teammate can offer support to a struggling beginner.

**观点2：** However, boundaries should be respected. **解释2：** Unrequested instructions may feel intrusive. **例子2：** For instance, asking whether a friend wants suggestions can make the conversation more comfortable.

#### P095-Q4 Is advice from friends better than professional advice?

**观点1：** Friends understand personal context and preferences. **解释1：** Their support may feel accessible and reassuring. **例子1：** For example, a friend can help choose an enjoyable hobby.

**观点2：** Professionals offer specialised knowledge when the situation requires it. **解释2：** Familiarity does not replace expertise. **例子2：** For instance, a technical problem may need someone trained to assess it properly.

#### P095-Q5 How can people encourage someone without pressuring them?

**观点1：** They can offer a small voluntary first step. **解释1：** A manageable trial feels less demanding than a major commitment. **例子1：** For example, inviting a friend to practise for a few minutes may help.

**观点2：** They can also participate supportively. **解释2：** Shared effort reduces the feeling of being instructed. **例子2：** For instance, trying an activity together can make the beginner more comfortable.

#### P095-Q6 Should children always follow adults' advice?

**观点1：** Children should take experienced guidance seriously. **解释1：** Adults can help them notice practical consequences. **例子1：** For example, preparation advice can make a school trip smoother.

**观点2：** However, children should be allowed to ask questions. **解释2：** Understanding builds judgement rather than simple obedience. **例子2：** For instance, discussing why a rule exists helps a child apply it in another setting.


## P096 对家庭重要的东西

截图：第12张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe something important that has been kept in your family for a long time

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 具体对象；怎样接触或获得；外观、内容或用途；它为什么对你重要。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P23原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

For this topic, I'd like to talk about a red remote-control car that has been kept in my family for a long time.

My father gave me this toy when I was a child. It has a small red body and four wheels. One wheel was damaged while I was playing with our dog, and my father repaired it patiently.

It's important to my family because it records many sweet memories. I used to play with it with my father in the park near my home. Later, keeping it became a way to remember his care and the time we spent together.

For other people, it may just be a normal old toy, but for me, it has sentimental value. It links my childhood, my family and those early memories together.

I keep it carefully rather than using it every day now. When I notice the repaired wheel, I remember the evening my father spent helping me. It is a small object, but it tells a family story that a new and more expensive toy could never replace for me.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：家庭与回忆；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P096-Q1 Why do family gatherings matter?

**观点1：** They give relatives time to reconnect. **解释1：** Everyday responsibilities can prevent proper conversations. **例子1：** For example, a shared dinner lets people discuss more than practical messages.

**观点2：** They also create shared memories. **解释2：** Repeated occasions become part of a family's history. **例子2：** For instance, a funny moment in a group photograph may be remembered for years.

#### P096-Q2 How can families spend more meaningful time together?

**观点1：** They can choose activities that allow conversation. **解释1：** An expensive outing is unnecessary if nobody has time to talk. **例子1：** For example, cooking a simple meal together can feel personal.

**观点2：** They should also protect some time from interruptions. **解释2：** Constant messages divide attention. **例子2：** For instance, putting phones aside during dinner can help everyone participate.

#### P096-Q3 Why do people keep old family objects?

**观点1：** Old objects can preserve emotional connections. **解释1：** They remind people of someone who gave or repaired them. **例子1：** For example, a childhood toy can represent a father's care.

**观点2：** They can also pass stories between generations. **解释2：** A physical object makes family history easier to discuss. **例子2：** For instance, an old photograph may lead to stories about grandparents.

#### P096-Q4 What makes people proud of relatives?

**观点1：** People feel proud when relatives overcome difficulties. **解释1：** They understand the effort behind the visible result. **例子1：** For example, completing a demanding course can mean more to family than strangers.

**观点2：** Kind behaviour also creates pride. **解释2：** A relative's character reflects what the family values. **例子2：** For instance, patiently helping a distressed child can leave a lasting impression.

#### P096-Q5 Are family celebrations different now from the past?

**观点1：** Some celebrations now involve more technology. **解释1：** People can include relatives who cannot travel. **例子1：** For example, a video call can connect a distant family member with a birthday.

**观点2：** However, busy routines can reduce time together. **解释2：** Work and distance make arrangements harder. **例子2：** For instance, a reunion may need to fit several different schedules.

#### P096-Q6 Do expensive presents show greater affection?

**观点1：** Not necessarily, because thoughtfulness matters more than price. **解释1：** A suitable gift shows understanding of the recipient. **例子1：** For example, a handmade card can refer to a shared memory.

**观点2：** However, practical value can also be meaningful. **解释2：** A gift may solve a real difficulty. **例子2：** For instance, useful equipment can support a relative's long-standing hobby.


## P097 发挥想象力

截图：第12张，显示共7个问题。话题检索参考：[跨季度口语话题汇编](https://western-overseas.com/test-prep-materials/speaking-cue-cards)。

### Part 2 题意

Describe a time you needed to use your imagination

**内容提示（非截图原文）：** 何时何地；与谁有关；发生了什么及你做了什么；结果与感受。

**答案来源：** 旧稿P34原答为底稿，按当前题目及180词要求修订。

### Part 2 答案｜180词

I'd like to talk about a time when I needed to use my imagination to improve my bedroom.

It happened last year when I wanted my room to look nicer and more comfortable. However, I didn't have enough money to buy a lot of new furniture or decorations.

So I had to use my imagination. I thought about how to change the atmosphere of the room in simple and low-cost ways. For example, I rearranged the furniture, put up a few posters and added some soft lighting. One thing I was quite proud of was that I pushed two single beds together and turned them into a bigger bed.

At first, it was a little difficult because I did not know much about design. But after looking for some ideas online and thinking carefully, it became much easier. In the end, I felt satisfied because my room looked better and felt more practical.

The experience also made me more confident about trying small changes myself, because imagination can be practical and useful even without a large budget or professional training.

### Part 3｜6个完整问答

本组：手工与创造力；练习问法与双观点回答，复用旧稿相关素材后重新组织。

#### P097-Q1 Why is creativity useful in daily life?

**观点1：** Creativity helps people use limited resources. **解释1：** They can find another approach instead of immediately spending money. **例子1：** For example, rearranging furniture may improve a small room.

**观点2：** It also helps people respond to unfamiliar problems. **解释2：** A standard solution may not suit every situation. **例子2：** For instance, a shopkeeper may cooperate with a neighbour to solve a payment problem.

#### P097-Q2 Can creativity be taught?

**观点1：** It can be encouraged through practice. **解释1：** Trying different solutions helps people become less afraid of unusual ideas. **例子1：** For example, children can design several objects from the same materials.

**观点2：** A supportive environment also matters. **解释2：** People need room to make mistakes without ridicule. **例子2：** For instance, a teacher can discuss what a failed design still teaches.

#### P097-Q3 Should children learn to make things by hand?

**观点1：** Yes, practical activities develop patience. **解释1：** Children see that a result requires several careful steps. **例子1：** For example, making a card involves planning, cutting and decorating.

**观点2：** They also connect ideas with physical materials. **解释2：** Children discover limits that are hard to understand through pictures alone. **例子2：** For instance, a paper bridge must actually hold its own shape.

#### P097-Q4 Are handmade gifts better than purchased gifts?

**观点1：** Handmade gifts can feel personal. **解释1：** The giver invests time and includes individual details. **例子1：** For example, a card can mention a shared memory.

**观点2：** However, a purchased gift may be more useful. **解释2：** Understanding the recipient matters more than the production method. **例子2：** For instance, suitable sports equipment can support a friend's regular hobby.

#### P097-Q5 Why do people repair old objects?

**观点1：** Repairing can save money and materials. **解释1：** A small damaged part does not always make the whole item useless. **例子1：** For example, replacing a wheel may restore a toy car.

**观点2：** People also want to preserve memories. **解释2：** A replacement cannot carry the same personal history. **例子2：** For instance, a repaired childhood toy may represent a parent's care.

#### P097-Q6 Is imagination important at work?

**观点1：** Yes, imagination helps people consider alternatives. **解释1：** A difficult task may need more than an existing routine. **例子1：** For example, a team can present complex data through a clearer visual explanation.

**观点2：** However, ideas need practical checking. **解释2：** An attractive possibility may fail in real use. **例子2：** For instance, a new design should be tested before everyone depends on it.
